be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
F\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a map such that

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
. If
d\gg_{s}1
, then there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

F(x)\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}

for all
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
.

Proposition
4.11
is a special case of the following result:

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N},s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d,k}1
be a prime dividing
K
,
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
U_{1},\dots,U_{k},V\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be (possibly trivial) subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
U_{1},\dots,U_{k}
having the same dimension such that
(U_{i},V)
is a linearly independent pair for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
For convenience denote
U_{0}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
.
Let
F\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a map such that

(4.8)

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V)
being a linearly independent tuple for all
0\leq i\leq k
. If
d\geq s+\dim(U_{k})+2\dim(V)+8
, then there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

(4.9)

F(x)\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x)\}+V}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V)
being a linearly independent tuple for all
0\leq i\leq k
.

Note that Proposition
4.12
improves Proposition
4.11
in the following senses. Firstly, we work on a large subset
H
of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
instead of working on the entire
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
. Secondly,
we allow to quotient the map
F
by a module
J^{M}_{V}
. Finally, we allow (
4.8
) to fail on a set of tuples of
(x,y)
with small cardinality (at the cost that the expression (
4.9
) will also fail on a set with small cardinality). Proposition
4.12
is a key property that will be used frequently in the rest of this section.

Proof of Proposition
4.12
.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d,k}1
.
If
s=0
, then
F(x)
is a constant polynomial for all
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.
Since
J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}
is generated by nontrivial homogeneous polynomials and
d\geq\dim(V)+4
, we have that
1\notin J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}
and thus it follows from (
4.8
) that
F(x)=F(y)
for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples.
On the other hand, if
d\geq\dim(U_{k})+\dim(V)+2
, then for any
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),(\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples, there exists
z\in H
such that
(\iota(x),\iota(z),U_{i},V),(\iota(y),\iota(z),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
are all linearly independent tuples (in fact, the number of
z
such that this condition fails is at most
O_{k}(K^{\dim(U_{1})+\dim(V)+1})
). So we have that
F(x)=F(z)=F(y)
and thus
F(x)
is independent of
x
. This proves the case
s=0
.

Suppose now the conclusion holds for
s-1
for some
s\geq 1
. We prove that the conclusion holds for
s
. For all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuple, since
d\geq 2(\dim(V)+2)+3
,
by (
4.8
) and Proposition
4.10
, there exist
A_{x,y},B_{x,y}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
such that

F(x)-F(y)\equiv L_{x}A_{x,y}-L_{y}B_{x,y}\mod J^{M}_{V}.

Using the identity
F(x)-F(z)=(F(x)-F(y))+(F(y)-F(z))
, we deduce that

L_{x}(A_{x,y}-A_{x,z})+L_{y}(A_{y,z}-B_{x,y})+L_{z}(B_{x,z}-B_{y,z})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{V}

for all
x,y,z\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples.
This implies that

L_{x}(A_{x,y}-A_{x,z})\in J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(y),\iota(z)\}+V}
. By Lemma
4.8
, if
d\geq 2\dim(V)+9
, then

(4.10)

A_{x,y}\equiv A_{x,z}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(y),\iota(z)\}+V}

if
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
are linearly independent tuples.
Similarly,

(4.11)

B_{x,z}\equiv B_{y,z}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}

and

(4.12)

A_{y,z}\equiv B_{x,y}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(z)\}+V}

if
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
are linearly independent tuples.

By (
4.10
) and the induction hypothesis, since
d\geq s+\dim(U_{k})+2\dim(V)+8=(s-1)+(\dim(U_{k})+1)+2\dim(V)+8
,
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples,
there exists
G_{x}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
such that
A_{x,y}\equiv G_{x}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(y)\}+V}
for all
y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V),1\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples. Similarly, by (
4.11
), there exists
G^{\prime}_{y}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
such that
B_{x,y}\equiv G^{\prime}_{y}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}+V}
for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples.

Fix
y\in H
with
(\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples. By (
4.12
),
for any
x,z\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples, we have that

G_{y}-G^{\prime}_{y}\equiv A_{y,z}-B_{x,y}\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(z)\}+V}
.
By Lemma
4.9
, if
d\geq\max\{\dim(V)+s+2,\dim(U_{k})+\dim(V)+3,2\dim(V)+9\}
, then
G_{y}\equiv G^{\prime}_{y}\mod J^{M}_{V}.

Now we have that

F(x)-F(y)\equiv L_{x}A_{x,y}-L_{y}B_{x,y}\equiv L_{x}G_{x}-L_{y}G^{\prime}_{y}\equiv L_{x}G_{x}-L_{y}G_{y}\mod J^{M}_{V}

and so

F^{\prime}(x)\equiv F^{\prime}(y)\mod J^{M}_{V}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples, where
F^{\prime}(x):=F(x)-L_{x}G_{x}
.
So for all
x,x^{\prime}\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),(\iota(x^{\prime}),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples, if
d\geq 2+\dim(U_{k})+\dim(V)
, then there exists
y\in H
such that
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V),(\iota(x^{\prime}),\iota(y),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
are linearly independent tuples (to see this, note that
|H|>\delta K^{d}
and that the number of
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that this condition fails is at most
O_{k}(K^{\dim(U_{k})+\dim(V)+1})
). So

F^{\prime}(x)\equiv F^{\prime}(y)\equiv F^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\mod J^{M}_{V}.

In other words, there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),0\leq i\leq k
being linearly independent tuples,

F(x)\equiv L_{x}G_{x}+G\mod J^{M}_{V}
and thus
F(x)-G\in J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x)\}+V}
.
∎

As an immediate corollary of Proposition
4.12
, we have

Corollary 4.13
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N},s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d,k}1
be a prime dividing
K
,
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
U_{1},\dots,U_{k},V\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be (possibly trivial) subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
U_{1},\dots,U_{k}
having the same dimension such that
(U_{i},V)
is a linearly independent pair for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
For convenience denote
U_{0}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
.
Let
F,F^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be maps such that

F(x)\equiv F^{\prime}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V)
being a linearly independent tuple for all
0\leq i\leq k
. If
d\geq s+\dim(U_{k})+2\dim(V)+11
, then there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

F(x)\equiv F^{\prime}(x)\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x)\}+V}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V)
being a linearly independent tuple for all
0\leq i\leq k
.

Proof.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d,k}1
.
Fix any
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),(\iota(y),

U_{i},V)
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. For any
z\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),U_{i},V)
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
, by assumption

(4.13)

F(x)\equiv F^{\prime}(z)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z)\}+V}.

By Proposition
4.9
, since
d\geq\max\{\dim(V)+s+3,\dim(U_{k})+\dim(V)+3,2\dim(V)+11\}
, we have that

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),(\iota(y),U_{i},V)
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. By Proposition
4.12
, there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that
F(x)\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x)\}+V}
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i},V),
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. Similarly, there exists
G^{\prime}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that
F^{\prime}(y)\equiv G^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(y)\}+V}
for all
y\in H
with
(\iota(y),U_{i},V),
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. Fix any
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i},V)
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
, we have that

G\equiv F(x)\equiv F^{\prime}(y)\equiv G^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}.

By Proposition
4.9
, since
d\geq\max\{\dim(V)+s+2,\dim(U_{k})+\dim(V)+2,2\dim(V)+9\}
, we have that
G\equiv G^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V}
and we are done.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

It is worth summarizing the idea of the proof of Corollary
4.13
briefly.
Ideally, we wish to arrive at the conclusion that

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y)\}+V}.

directly from the hypothesis in the statement. However, due to a loss of information, we were only able to deduce the weaker property (
4.13
) in the first place, where we need to modulo an additional module
J^{M}_{z}
. In order to overcome this difficulty, we apply (
4.13
) for many different
z
, and then intersect all the modules
J^{M}_{{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z)\}+V}}
to recover the lost information. For convenience we refer to this method as the
intersection approach
, which will be frequently used in this paper.

We now move from (
4.8
) to the following slightly different equation:

(4.14)

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)},

in which case we can obtain a better description of the solutions than (
4.8
). In order to state this result, we need to introduce the following notion:

Definition 4.15

(Super polynomials)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
We say that a map
F\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)^{k}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
is a
super polynomial
if for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
with
|i|\leq s
, there exists

C_{i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-|i|)
such that

F(f_{1},\dots,f_{k})=\sum_{i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k},|i|\leq s}C_{i}f_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdot\dots\cdot f_{k}^{i_{k}}

for all
f_{1},\dots,f_{k}\in\text{HP}_{d}(1)
.
We say that
F
is
symmetric
if
F(f_{1},\dots,f_{k})=F(f_{\sigma(1)},\dots,f_{\sigma(k)})
for all permutation
\sigma\colon\{1,\dots,k\}\to\{1,\dots,k\}
.
The
degree
of
F
(denoted as
\deg(F)
) is the largest integer
t\in\mathbb{N}
such that
C_{i}\neq 0
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
with
|i|=t
(it is clear that
\deg(F)\leq s
). For convenience we define the constant zero function to have degree
s
for any
s\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We say that
F
is
homogeneous
if
C_{i}\neq 0
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
with
|i|\neq\deg(F)
.

Since
L_{x}-L_{y}=L_{x-y}\in J^{M}_{x-y}
,
it is not hard to see that
F(x)=G(L_{x})
is a solution to (
4.14
) for any super polynomial
G\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
. The next proposition shows that this is the only possibility.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k\in\mathbb{N},s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d,k}1
be a prime dividing
K
.
Let
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
U_{1},\dots,U_{k}
be (possibly trivial) subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of same dimensions. For convenience denote
U_{0}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
. Let
F\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a map such that

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. If
d\geq s+\dim(U_{k})+8
, then there exists a super polynomial
G\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

F(x)\equiv G(L_{x})\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
.

Proof.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d,k}1
.
By Proposition
4.12
, since
d\geq s+\dim(U_{k})+8
, there exists
G_{0}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that
F(x)\equiv G_{0}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. By Proposition
4.10
, since
d\geq 5
, we may write

F(x)\equiv G_{0}+L_{x}F_{1}(x)\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
for some
F_{1}(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
.

Suppose we have shown that for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there
exist
G_{i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-i),0\leq i\leq k
and some map
F_{k+1}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
such that

F(x)\equiv L_{x}^{k+1}F_{k+1}(x)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}L_{x}^{i}G_{i}\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. We show that the same conclusion holds for
k+1
.
Since
F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
, we have that

L_{x}^{k+1}F_{k+1}(x)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}L_{x}^{i}G_{i}\equiv L_{y}^{k+1}F_{k+1}(y)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}L_{y}^{i}G_{i}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}.

Since
L_{x}-L_{y}=L_{x-y}
, this implies that

L_{x}^{k+1}(F_{k+1}(x)-F_{k+1}(y))\in J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
. Since
d\geq 7
, by repeatedly using Lemma
4.8
, we have that
F_{k+1}(x)-F_{k+1}(y)\in J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}\subseteq J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}
for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. By
Proposition
4.12
, there exists
G_{k+1}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
such that
F_{k+1}(x)\equiv G_{k+1}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
.
By Proposition
4.10
, we may write

F_{k+1}(x)\equiv G_{k+1}+L_{x}F_{k+2}(x)\mod J^{M}

for
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
for some
F_{k+2}(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-2)
.
Then

F(x)\equiv L_{x}^{k+1}F_{k+1}(x)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}L_{x}^{i}G_{i}\equiv L_{x}^{k+2}F_{k+2}(x)+\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}L_{x}^{i}G_{i}\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
.

So by induction,
there
exist
G_{i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-i),0\leq i\leq s
and some map
F_{s+1}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(-1)
(meaning that
F_{s+1}\equiv 0
by Convention
1.3
) such that

F(x)\equiv L_{x}^{s+1}F_{s+1}(x)+\sum_{i=0}^{s}L_{x}^{i}G_{i}\equiv\sum_{i=0}^{s}L_{x}^{i}G_{i}\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U_{i})
being linearly independent tuples for all
0\leq i\leq k
. This completes the proof by the definition of super polynomials.
∎

4.4.
Properties for super polynomials

Before moving from equation (
4.14
) to more sophisticated equations, we prove some properties for super polynomials for later uses.

By Lemma
A.4
, if a polynomial
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
takes value zero on a large subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, then
P\equiv 0
. A similar result holds for super polynomials:

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
d,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
.
Let
U
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form, and
F\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)^{k}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a super polynomial given by

F(f_{1},\dots,f_{k})=\sum_{i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k},|i|\leq s}C_{i}f_{1}^{i_{1}}\cdot\ldots\cdot f_{k}^{i_{k}}.

Suppose that
F(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k}})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}
for all
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\in H
with
(\iota(x_{1}),\dots,\iota(x_{k}),U)
being a linearly independent tuple. If
d\geq\max\{s+1,\dim(U)+k,7\}
, then

C_{i}\equiv 0\mod J^{M}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{k},|i|\leq s
.

Proof.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
.
It suffices to prove Proposition
4.17
under the assumption that the conclusion holds for
k-1
(when
k=1
, then we prove Proposition
4.17
without any induction hypothesis).

We may rewrite
F
as

F(f_{1},\dots,f_{k})=\sum_{0\leq i\leq s}F_{i}(f_{1},\dots,f_{k-1})f_{k}^{i}

for some super polynomial
F_{i}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)^{k-1}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-i)
(where
F_{i}
is regarded as a constant if
k=1
).
For any
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\in H
with
(\iota(x_{1}),\dots,\iota(x_{k}),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, since
F(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k}})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}
, we have that
F_{0}(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k-1}})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x_{k})}
.
Note that the set of
x_{k}\in H
with
(\iota(x_{1}),\dots,\iota(x_{k}),U)
not being a linearly independent tuple is of cardinality
O(K^{\dim(U)+k-1})
.
By Proposition
4.9
(setting
m=1
,
V=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, and
U_{1}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x_{1}),\dots,\iota(x_{k-1})\}+U
), since
d\geq\max\{s+1,\dim(U)+k,7\}
, we have that
F_{0}(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k-1}})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}
for any
x_{1},\dots,x_{k-1}\in H
with
(\iota(x_{1}),\dots,\iota(x_{k-1}),U)
being a linearly independent tuple.
By induction hypothesis, we have that
F_{0}
takes values in
J^{M}
. So for any
x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\in H
with
(\iota(x_{1}),\dots,\iota(x_{k}),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, the fact that
F(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k}})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}
implies that
L_{x_{k}}F^{\prime}(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k}})\in J^{M}
, where

F^{\prime}(f_{1},\dots,f_{k})=\sum_{1\leq i\leq s}F_{i}(f_{1},\dots,f_{k-1})f_{k}^{i-1}.

By Lemma
4.8
, since
d\geq 5
, we have that
F^{\prime}(L_{x_{1}},\dots,L_{x_{k}})\in J^{M}
. We may then repeat the previous discussion to inductively show that all of
F_{0},\dots,F_{s}
takes values in
J^{M}
. The conclusion now follows from the induction hypothesis.
∎

The following technical proposition will be used in the next subsection.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
.
Let
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form, and
G_{x}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a super polynomial for all
x\in H
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
such that

(4.15)

\begin{split}G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv G_{y}(L_{x})\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
. If
d\geq s+11
, then there exists a symmetric super polynomial
Q\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\times\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv Q(L_{x},L_{y})\mod J^{M}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
.

Before proving Proposition
4.18
, we need a lemma:

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k,s\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\leq s
, and
p\gg_{d,s}1
be a prime.
Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
x,y\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be linearly independent.
Suppose that

(4.16)

\sum_{i=0}^{k}C_{i}L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k-i}\equiv PL_{x}^{k+1}+QL_{y}^{k+1}\mod J^{M}

for some
C_{i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k)
and some
P,Q\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
. If
d\geq 7
, then we have that
C_{i}\in J^{M}_{x,y}
for all
0\leq i\leq k
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{d,s}1
.
If
k=0
, then then it follows from (
4.16
) that
C_{0}\equiv PL_{x}+QL_{y}\mod J^{M}
and thus
C_{0}\in J^{M}_{x,y}
. If
k=1
, then it follows from (
4.16
) that

C_{0}L_{y}+C_{1}L_{x}\equiv PL_{x}^{2}+QL_{y}^{2}\mod J^{M}.

It follows that
C_{0}L_{y}\equiv QL_{y}^{2}\mod J^{M}_{x}
. By Lemma
4.8
, since
d\geq 7
, we have that
C_{0}\equiv QL_{y}\mod J^{M}_{x}
and thus
C_{0}\in J^{M}_{x,y}
. Similarly, we have
C_{1}\in J^{M}_{x,y}
. So the conclusion holds.

Now assume that the conclusion holds for
k-2
for some
2\leq k\leq s
. We prove that the conclusion holds for
k
.
It follows from (
4.16
) that
C_{0}L_{y}^{k}\equiv QL_{y}^{k+1}\mod J^{M}_{x}
.
Since
d\geq 7
, by repeatedly using Lemma
4.8
,
we have that
C_{0}\equiv QL_{y}\mod J^{M}_{x}
and thus
C_{0}\in J^{M}_{x,y}
. By Proposition
4.10
, since
d\geq 7
, there exist
P^{\prime},Q^{\prime}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
such that
C_{0}\equiv L_{x}P^{\prime}+L_{y}Q^{\prime}\mod J^{M}
. Similarly, there exist
P^{\prime\prime},Q^{\prime\prime}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
such that
C_{k}\equiv L_{x}P^{\prime\prime}+L_{y}Q^{\prime\prime}\mod J^{M}
. Substituting the expressions of
C_{0}
and
C_{k}
back to (
4.16
), we have that

(4.17)

L_{x}L_{y}(\sum_{i=0}^{k-2}C_{i+1}L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k-2-i})\equiv(P-P^{\prime\prime})L_{x}^{k+1}+(Q-Q^{\prime})L_{y}^{k+1}-P^{\prime}L_{x}L_{y}^{k}-Q^{\prime\prime}L_{x}^{k}L_{y}\mod J^{M}.

In particular, we have that
(P-P^{\prime\prime})L_{x}^{k+1}\in J^{M}_{y}
. By Lemma
4.8
, we have that
P-P^{\prime\prime}\mod J^{M}_{y}
. By Proposition
4.10
, there exists
P^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-2)
such that
P-P^{\prime\prime}\equiv L_{y}P^{\prime\prime\prime}\mod J^{M}
. Similarly, there exists
Q^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-2)
such that
Q-Q^{\prime}\equiv L_{x}Q^{\prime\prime\prime}\mod J^{M}
. Substituting these back to (
4.17
), we have that

L_{x}L_{y}(\sum_{i=0}^{k-2}C_{i+1}L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k-2-i})\equiv L_{x}L_{y}(P^{\prime\prime\prime}L_{x}^{k}+Q^{\prime\prime\prime}L_{y}^{k}-P^{\prime}L_{y}^{k-1}-Q^{\prime\prime}L_{x}^{k-1})\mod J^{M}.

By repeatedly using Lemma
4.8
, we have that

\sum_{i=0}^{k-2}C_{i+1}L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k-2-i}\equiv(P^{\prime\prime\prime}L_{x}-Q^{\prime\prime})L_{x}^{k-1}+(Q^{\prime\prime\prime}L_{y}-P^{\prime})L_{y}^{k-1}\mod J^{M}.

By induction hypothesis, we have that
C_{i}\in J^{M}_{x,y}
for all
1\leq i\leq k-1
and thus for all
0\leq i\leq k
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.18
.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
.
For
0\leq k\leq s+1
, we say that
Property
k
holds if there exist a symmetric super polynomial
Q_{k}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\times\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
, for each
x\in H
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
a homogeneous super polynomial
Q_{k,x}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\times\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
of degree
k
and a super polynomial
G_{k,x}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
such that

(4.18)

\begin{split}G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv Q_{k}(L_{x},L_{y})+Q_{k,x}(L_{x},L_{y})+L_{y}^{k+1}G_{k,x}(L_{y})\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
, where when
k=s+1
, (
4.18
) is understood as

G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv Q_{s+1}(L_{x},L_{y})\mod J^{M}.

Assume that

G_{x}(f)=\sum_{i=0}^{s}C_{i,x}f^{i}
for some
C_{x,i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-i)
for all
x\in H
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
.
We have that Property 0 holds by setting
Q_{0}\equiv 0
,
Q_{0,x}(f,g)\equiv C_{i,0}
, and
G_{0,x}(f):=\sum_{i=1}^{s}C_{i,x}f^{i-1}
.
Now assume that Property
k
holds for some
0\leq k\leq s
. We show that Property
k+1
holds.
By (
4.15
) and (
4.18
), we have that

(4.19)

\begin{split}Q_{k,x}(L_{x},L_{y})+L_{y}^{k+1}G_{k,x}(L_{y})\equiv Q_{k,y}(L_{y},L_{x})+L_{x}^{k+1}G_{k,y}(L_{x})\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
.
Assume that

(4.20)

\begin{split}Q_{k,x}(f,g)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}A_{x,i}f^{i}g^{k-i}\end{split}

for some
A_{x,i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k)
. It follows from (
4.19
) that

(4.21)

\begin{split}\sum_{i=0}^{k}(A_{x,i}-A_{y,k-i})L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k-i}\equiv L_{x}^{k+1}G_{k,y}(L_{x})-L_{y}^{k+1}G_{k,x}(L_{y})\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
.
By Lemma
4.19
, since
d\geq 7
, we have that

A_{x,i}\equiv A_{y,k-i}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}
for all
0\leq i\leq k
and
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
. By Corollary
4.13
, since
d\geq s-k+11
, for all
0\leq i\leq k
, there exists
A_{i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k)
such that

A_{x,i}\equiv A_{i}\equiv A_{x,k-i}\equiv A_{k-i}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}

for all
x\in H
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
.
We may assume without loss of generality that
A_{i}=A_{k-i}
.
By Proposition
4.10
, since
d\geq 5
, we may write

(4.22)

\begin{split}A_{x,i}\equiv A_{i}+L_{x}A^{\prime}_{x,i}\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for some
A^{\prime}_{x,i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
.
Note that we may write
G_{k,x}
as

(4.23)

\begin{split}G_{k,x}(f)=P_{x}+fG_{k+1,x}(f)\end{split}

for some
P_{x}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-1)
and super polynomial
G_{k+1,x}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-k-2)
. By (
4.18
), (
4.20
), (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), we have that

\begin{split}G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv Q_{k+1}(L_{x},L_{y})+Q_{k+1,x}(L_{x},L_{y})+L_{y}^{k+2}G_{k+1,x}(L_{y})\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
,
where

Q_{k+1}(f,g):=Q_{k}(f,g)+\sum_{i=0}^{k}A_{i}f^{i}g^{k-i}

and

Q_{k+1,x}(f,g):=P_{x}g^{k+1}+\sum_{i=0}^{k}A^{\prime}_{x,i}f^{i+1}g^{k-i}.

Since
Q_{k}
is symmetric and
A_{i}=A_{k-i}
, we have that
Q_{k+1}
is also symmetric. On the other hand, it is clear that
Q_{k+1,x}
is a homogeneous super polynomial of degree
k+1
. So Property
k+1
holds.

By induction, we have that Property
s+1
holds, meaning that there exist a symmetric super polynomial
Q_{s+1}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\times\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv Q_{s+1}(L_{x},L_{y})\mod J^{M}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in H
.
We are done.
∎

4.5.
Solutions to some special equations

Next, we move forward from equation (
4.6
) to the following more advanced cocycle equation:

(4.24)

C(x,y)+C(y,z)\equiv C(x,z)\mod\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all $x,y,z\in G$},

where
C
is a map from
G\times G
to
\mathbb{R}
and
G
is an abelian group. Setting
z
to be an arbitrary element in
G
, it is obvious that the solution to (
4.24
) is
C(x,y)\equiv F(x)-F(y)\mod\mathbb{Z}
for some map
F\colon G\to\mathbb{R}
for all
x,y\in G
.
In the setting of shifted modules, one can study a similar equation:

(4.25)

C(x,y)+C(y,z)\equiv C(x,z)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y),\iota(y-z)}\text{ for all $x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}$},

where
C
is a map from
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
to
\text{HP}_{d}(s)
. Comparing (
4.24
) with (
4.25
), it is natural to conjecture that the solution to (
4.25
) is
C(x,y)\equiv F(x)-F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
for some
F\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
. This is indeed the case at least when
x,y
are
p
-linearly independent.

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
p\gg_{d,s}1
be a prime dividing
K
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
C\colon(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{2}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a map such that (
4.25
) holds. If
d\gg_{s}1
,
then there exists a map
F\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that
C(x,y)\equiv F(x)-F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.

Again, instead of proving
Proposition
4.20
, we prove the following more general result:

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
, and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
,
U
be a (possibly trivial) subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, and

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
For all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U)
being a linearly independent tuple,
let
C(x,y)
be an element in
\text{HP}_{d}(s)
. Suppose that for all
x,y,z\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),U)
being a linearly independent tuple,
we have

(4.26)

C(x,y)+C(y,z)\equiv C(x,z)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y),\iota(y-z)}.

If

d\geq s+\dim(U)+9
, then there exist
\phi(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U)
being a linearly independent tuple
such that

C(x,y)\equiv\phi(x)-\phi(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U)
being a linearly independent tuple.

Proof.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
.
Suppose that either
s=0
, or
s\geq 1

and we have shown the conclusion holds for
s-1
. We prove that the conclusion holds for
s
.

Applying Proposition
4.10
to
J^{M}_{\iota(x-y),\iota(y-z)}
, we have that if
d\geq 7
, then for all
x,y,b\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, there exists
P_{x,y,b}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
such that

(4.27)

C(x,y)\equiv(C(x,b)-C(y,b))+L_{b-x}P_{x,y,b}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}.

For all
x,y,z,b\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple,
since
C(x,y)+C(y,z)\equiv C(x,z)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y),\iota(y-z)}
,
it follows from (
4.27
) that

L_{b-x}(P_{x,y,b}+P_{y,z,b}-P_{x,z,b})\equiv L_{b-x}P_{x,y,b}+L_{b-y}P_{y,z,b}-L_{b-x}P_{x,z,b}\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y),\iota(y-z)}.

Since
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(z),\iota(b),U)
is a linearly independent tuple, by Lemma
4.8
, if
d\geq 9
,
then

(4.28)

P_{x,y,b}+P_{y,z,b}-P_{x,z,b}\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y),\iota(y-z)}.

We arrive at an equation similar to (
4.26
), except that
P_{x,y,b}
are elements in
\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
instead of
\text{HP}_{d}(s)
. Therefore, we may use the induction hypothesis to obtain a description for
P_{x,y,b}
.

Claim 1.
For any
b\in H
with
(\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, there exist
\psi_{b}(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple such that

C(x,y)\equiv\psi_{b}(x)-\psi_{b}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple.

If
s=0
, then
P_{x,y,b}\equiv 0
by Convention
1.3
. Setting
\psi_{b}(x):=C(x,b)
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, it follows from (
4.27
) that
C(x,y)=\psi_{b}(x)-\psi_{b}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
whenever
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
is a linearly independent tuple.

If
s\geq 1
, then
since
d\geq s+\dim(U)+9=(s-1)+(\dim(U)+1)+9
, we may apply
the induction hypothesis to (
4.28
) and
P_{x,y,b}
to conclude that for all
b\in H
with
(\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, there exist
\psi_{b}(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple such that

P_{x,y,b}\equiv\psi_{b}(x)-\psi_{b}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple. Setting
\psi_{b}(x):=C(x,b)+L_{b-x}\psi_{b}(x)
, it follows from (
4.27
) that

C(x,y)\equiv(C(x,b)-C(y,b))+L_{b-x}P_{x,y,b}\equiv\psi_{b}(x)-\psi_{b}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple. We have thus proved Claim 1 in both cases.

In order to complete the proof of Proposition
4.21
, we need to remove the restriction that
x,y,b
are
p
-linearly independent in the claim. Our strategy is to apply the claim for many linearly independent
b
and to obtain many different maps
\psi_{b}
. Then by studying the interactions of these
\psi_{b}
, we extend them to a universal map
\phi
such that
C(x,y)\equiv\phi(x)-\phi(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
holds for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U)
being a linearly independent tuple.

By Claim 1, for all
x,y,b,b^{\prime}\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
and
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b^{\prime}),U)
being linearly independent tuples, we have that

(\psi_{b}-\psi_{b^{\prime}})(x)-(\psi_{b}-\psi_{b^{\prime}})(y)\equiv C(x,y)-C(x,y)=0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}.

By Proposition
4.16
, if
d\geq s+\dim(U)+9
, then for all
b,b^{\prime}\in H
with
(\iota(b),U)
and
(\iota(b^{\prime}),U)
being linearly independent tuples, there exists a super polynomial
G_{b,b^{\prime}}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

(\psi_{b}-\psi_{b^{\prime}})(x)\equiv G_{b,b^{\prime}}(L_{x})\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(b),U)
and
(\iota(x),\iota(b^{\prime}),U)
being linearly independent tuples.
So for all
x,b,b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(b),U)
,
(\iota(x),\iota(b^{\prime}),U)
and
(\iota(x),\iota(b^{\prime\prime}),U)
being linearly independent tuples, we have that

G_{b,b^{\prime}}(L_{x})-G_{b,b^{\prime\prime}}(L_{x})\equiv(\psi_{b}-\psi_{b^{\prime}})(x)-(\psi_{b}-\psi_{b^{\prime\prime}})(x)\equiv G_{b^{\prime\prime},b^{\prime}}(L_{x})\mod J^{M}.

Let

B
be a set of
d-\dim(U)
vectors in
H
such that
\iota(B)
completes
U
into a basis of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
(such
B
exists since
|H|>\delta K^{d}
).
For
b\in B
, let
U_{b}
be the set of
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple. Fix some
b_{0}\in B
. For
b\in B
and
x\in U_{b}
, define
\phi_{b_{0}}(x):=\psi_{b_{0}}(x)
and
\phi_{b}(x):=\psi_{b}(x)-G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{x})
if
b\neq b_{0}
.

Claim 2.
that
\phi_{b}(x)\equiv\phi_{b^{\prime}}(x)\mod J^{M}
for all
b,b^{\prime}\in B
and
x\in U_{b}\cap U_{b^{\prime}}
.

Indeed, we may assume without loss of generality that
b\neq b^{\prime}
and
b\neq b_{0}
. If
b^{\prime}=b_{0}
, then

\phi_{b}(x)-\phi_{b^{\prime}}(x)\equiv\psi_{b}(x)-G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{x})-\psi_{b_{0}}(x)\equiv 0\mod J^{M}.

If
b^{\prime}\neq b_{0}
, then

\begin{split}&\quad\phi_{b}(x)-\phi_{b^{\prime}}(x)\equiv(\psi_{b}(x)-G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{x}))-(\psi_{b^{\prime}}(x)-G_{b^{\prime},b_{0}}(L_{x}))\\
&\equiv G_{b,b^{\prime}}(L_{x})-G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{x})+G_{b^{\prime},b_{0}}(L_{x})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}.\end{split}

This proves Claim 2.

By Claim 2, there exists
\phi(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
for all
x\in\cup_{b\in B}U_{b}
such that

\phi(x)\equiv\phi_{b}(x)\mod J^{M}

for all
b\in B
with
x\in U_{b}
. Note that for all
x,y\in H
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, since
d\geq\dim(U)+3
, there exists
b\in B
with
(\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(b),U)
being a linearly independent tuple. So
x,y\in U_{b}
and thus

\phi(x)-\phi(y)\equiv\phi_{b}(x)-\phi_{b}(y)\equiv\psi_{b_{0}}(x)-\psi_{b_{0}}(y)\equiv C(x,y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

if
b=b_{0}
, and

\phi(x)-\phi(y)\equiv\phi_{b}(x)-\phi_{b}(y)\equiv(\psi_{b}(x)-G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{x}))-(\psi_{b}(y)-G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{y}))\equiv C(x,y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

if
b\neq b_{0}
, where we used the fact that
G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{x})\equiv G_{b,b_{0}}(L_{y})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}
since
G_{b,b_{0}}
is a super polynomial.
Finally, since
\cup_{b\in B}U_{b}
consists of all
x\in H
with
(\iota(x),U)
being a linearly independent tuple, we are done.
∎

We need to explore the solutions to one more equation before proving Theorem
4.7
.

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
d,D,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
.
Let

P=\{\ell_{1}v_{1}+\dots+\ell_{D}v_{D}\colon\ell_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-L_{i},L_{i})\text{ for all }1\leq i\leq D\}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}

be a proper and homogeneous generalized arithmetic progression of dimension
D
with
|P|>\delta K^{d}
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
F\colon P\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
be a map such that

(4.29)

F(x)+F(y)\equiv F(x+y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}\cap J^{M}_{\iota(y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{2})
. If
d\geq s+9
, then there exist a super polynomial
G\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
and a locally linear map
T\colon P(\frac{1}{100})\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such
that

F(x)\equiv L_{x}^{2}G(L_{x})+T(x)\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in P(\frac{1}{100})
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
.

Proof.

By Convention
1.3
, there is nothing to prove when
s<0
. So we assume that
s\in\mathbb{N}
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
, and we implicitly use (
4.5
) for the estimate the cardinality of sets of the form
P(c)
.
We start with a reformulation of (
4.29
).
Fix any
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{2})
. Since
F(x)+F(y)\equiv F(x+y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}
and
d\geq 5
, by Proposition
4.10
, we have that

F(x)+F(y)\equiv F(x+y)+L_{x}R^{\prime}(x,y)\mod J^{M}
for some
R^{\prime}(x,y)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
. Since
F(x)+F(y)\equiv F(x+y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y)}
, we have that
L_{x}R^{\prime}(x,y)\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y)}
. By Lemma
4.8
, since
d\geq 7
, we have that
R^{\prime}(x,y)\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y)}
. So by Proposition
4.10
, we may write

R^{\prime}(x,y)\equiv L_{y}R(x,y)\mod J^{M}
for some
R(x,y)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
. In conclusion, we have that

(4.30)

F(x)+F(y)\equiv F(x+y)+L_{x}L_{y}R(x,y)\mod J^{M}

for some
R(x,y)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{2})
.

We first assume that
s\geq 2
.
By symmetry, (
4.30
) implies that
L_{x}L_{y}(R(x,y)-R(y,x))\in J^{M}
for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{2})
. By Lemma
4.8
, since
d\geq 5
, this implies that

(4.31)

\begin{split}R(x,y)\equiv R(y,x)\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{2})
.

Fix any
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z\in P(\frac{1}{4})
. Then (
4.30
) implies that

(4.32)

\begin{split}&\quad F(x)+F(y)+F(z)-F(x+y+z)\\
&=(F(y)+F(z)-F(y+z))+(F(x)+F(y+z)-F(x+y+z))\\
&\equiv L_{y}L_{z}R(y,z)+L_{x}L_{y+z}R(x,y+z)\mod J^{M}.\end{split}

By symmetry, we also have that

(4.33)

\begin{split}F(x)+F(y)+F(z)-F(x+y+z)\equiv L_{x}L_{z}R(x,z)+L_{y}L_{x+z}R(y,x+z)\mod J^{M}.\end{split}

Comparing (
4.32
) and (
4.33
), we have that

(4.34)

\begin{split}&\quad L_{y}L_{z}(R(y,z)-R(y,x+z))+L_{x}L_{y}(R(x,y+z)-R(y,x+z))\\
&+L_{x}L_{z}(R(x,y+z)-R(x,z))\equiv 0\mod J^{M}.\end{split}

In particular,
L_{y}L_{z}(R(y,z)-R(y,x+z))\in J^{M}_{\iota(x)}
. By Lemma
4.8
, since
d\geq 7
, we have that
R(y,z)\equiv R(y,x+z)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}
. In other words, for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z\in P(\frac{1}{8})
, we have that
R(y,x)\equiv R(y,z)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-z)}
. By Proposition
4.16
, since
d\geq s+7
, for all
y\in P(\frac{1}{8})
with
\iota(y)\neq\mathbb{0}
, there exists a super polynomial
G_{y}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
such that

R(y,x)\equiv G_{y}(L_{x})\mod J^{M}

for all
x\in P(\frac{1}{8})
with
x,y
being
p
-linearly independent.
By (
4.31
), we have that

\begin{split}G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv G_{y}(L_{x})\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{8})
.
By Proposition
4.18
, since
d\geq s+9
,
there exists a symmetric super polynomial
Q\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\times\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
such that

R(x,y)=G_{x}(L_{y})\equiv Q(L_{x},L_{y})\mod J^{M}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{8})
.
We may then write
Q(f,g)=\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}Q_{k}(f,g)
for some symmetric homogeneous super polynomials
Q_{k}\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\times\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
of degree
k
. Write

Q_{k}(f,g)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}C_{k,i}f^{i}g^{k-i}

for some
C_{k,i}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2-k)
. For convenience write
C_{k,-1}=0
. Then we may rewrite (
4.34
) as

(4.35)

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}\sum_{0\leq a,b,c\leq k,a+b+c=k+2}L_{x}^{a}L_{y}^{b}L_{z}^{c}W(a,b,c)\equiv 0\mod J^{M},\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&\quad W(a,b,c):=-C_{k,b-1}\binom{k-(b-1)}{a}\mathbb{1}_{a\neq 0}+C_{k,a-1}\binom{k-(a-1)}{b-1}\\
&\qquad-C_{k,b-1}\binom{k-(b-1)}{a-1}+C_{k,a-1}\binom{k-(a-1)}{b}\mathbb{1}_{b\neq 0}.\end{split}

Note that
W(a,b,c)=0
if
a=0
or
b=0
. If
a,b\neq 0
, then

W(a,b,c)=C_{k,a-1}\binom{k+2-a}{b}-C_{k,b-1}\binom{k+2-b}{a}.

So by (
4.35
) and Proposition
4.17
, since

d\geq\max\{s+1,7\}
,
we have that

W(a+1,b+1,c)=C_{k,a}\binom{k+1-a}{b+1}-C_{k,b}\binom{k+1-b}{a+1}\equiv 0\mod J^{M}

for all
a,b,c\in\mathbb{N}
with
a+b+c=k
. From this it is not hard to see that there exists
C_{k}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2-k)
such that

C_{k,a}\equiv\binom{k+2}{a+1}C_{k}\mod J^{M}
for all
0\leq a\leq k
. Therefore,

\begin{split}&\quad L_{x}L_{y}R(x,y)\equiv L_{x}L_{y}\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}\sum_{i=0}^{k}\binom{k+2}{i+1}C_{k}L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k-i}\\
&=\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}\binom{k+2}{i}C_{k}L_{x}^{i}L_{y}^{k+2-i}=\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}C_{k}(L_{x+y}^{k+2}-L_{x}^{k+2}-L_{y}^{k+2})\mod J^{M}.\end{split}

Let
Z
denote the set of
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
\iota(x)=\mathbb{0}
.
Then for all
x\in P(\frac{1}{8})\backslash Z
, writing
F^{\prime}(x):=\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}C_{k}L_{x}^{k+2}
,
G(f):=\sum_{k=0}^{s-2}C_{k}f^{k}
, and
T(x):=F(x)-F^{\prime}(x)
, we have that
F^{\prime}(x)=L_{x}^{2}G(L_{x})\in J^{M}
and

(4.36)

T(x)+T(y)\equiv T(x+y)\mod J^{M}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{8})
.

Now if
s<2
, then
R(x,y)\equiv 0
. Setting
G\equiv 0
and
T:=F
, by (
4.30
) , (
4.36
) still holds for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{8})
.

Now for all
x\in P(\frac{1}{32})\cap Z
, define

T_{y}(x):=T(x+y)-T(y)
for any
y\in P(\frac{1}{16})\backslash Z
(which exists since
|Z|\leq(K/p)^{d}
). We claim that
T:=T_{y}\mod J^{M}
is independent of the choices of
y
. Indeed, for any
y,y^{\prime}\in P(\frac{1}{16})\backslash Z
, if
y
and
y^{\prime}
are
p
-linearly independent, then it follows from (
4.36
) that

T(x+y)+T(y^{\prime})\equiv T(x+y+y^{\prime})\equiv T(x+y^{\prime})+T(y)\mod J^{M}

and so
T_{y}\equiv T_{y^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}
. If
y
and
y^{\prime}
are not
p
-linearly independent, then there exists
z\in P(\frac{1}{16})\backslash Z
with
(y,z)
and
(y^{\prime},z)
being
p
-linearly independent pairs. Then

T_{y}\equiv T_{z}\equiv T_{y^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}.

So
T\mod J^{M}
is well defined.

Claim.
We have that (
4.36
) holds for all (not necessarily
p
-linearly independent)
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{100})
.

We first show that (
4.36
) holds for all
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{100})\backslash Z
with
x+y\notin Z
.
In fact, since
d\geq 2
, there exists
z\in P(\frac{1}{16})
such that
(x,z),(x+y,z)
and
(y,x+z)
are
p
-linearly independent pairs. So it follows from (
4.36
) that

(T(x)+T(z))+(T(y)+T(x+z))\equiv T(x+z)+T(x+y+z)\equiv T(x+z)+(T(x+y)+T(z))\mod J^{M},

which implies that (
4.36
) holds.

We next show that (
4.36
) holds for all
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{100})\backslash Z
with
x+y\in Z
.
Pick any
z\in P(\frac{1}{100})
such that
(x,z)
and
(y,x+z)
are
p
-linearly independent pairs. So it follows from (
4.36
) that

T(x)+T(y)\equiv T(x+z)+T(y)-T(z)\equiv T(x+y+z)-T(z)\equiv T(x+y)\mod J^{M}.

We finally show that (
4.36
) holds for all
x,y\in P(\frac{1}{100})
with at least one of
x,y
in
Z
.
If both
x
and
y
belong to
Z
, then pick any
p
-linearly independent
z,w\in P(\frac{1}{100})
. So it follows from (
4.36
) and the previous case that

T(x)+T(y)\equiv T(x+z)-T(z)+T(y+w)-T(w)\equiv T(x+z-w)+T(y+w-z)\equiv T(x+y)\mod J^{M}.

If
x\in Z
and
y\notin Z
, then pick any
z\in P(\frac{1}{100})
with
(x+z,y),(z,x+y)
being
p
-linearly independent pairs. It follows from (
4.36
) that

T(x)+T(y)\equiv T(x+z)-T(z)+T(y)\equiv T(x+y+z)-T(z)\equiv T(x+y)\mod J^{M}.

This completes the proof of the claim.

Since
\text{HP}_{d}(s)/(J^{M}\cap\text{HP}_{d}(s))
is an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-vector space, it is not hard to see from the claim that
T\mod J^{M}
is determined by its values on the generators of
P
. So there exist
f_{1},\dots,f_{D}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

T(\ell_{1}v_{1}+\dots+\ell_{D}v_{D})\equiv\ell_{1}f_{1}+\dots+\ell_{D}f_{D}\mod J^{M}

for all
\ell_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-L_{i}/100,L_{i}/100)
.
So
T
is equivalent mod
J^{M}
to a genial locally linear map on
P(\frac{1}{100})
and we are done.
∎

4.6.
Proof of Theorem
4.7

We are now ready to prove Theorem
4.7
.
Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
, and we implicitly use (
4.5
) for the estimate the cardinality of sets of the form
P(c)
.
It is not hard to see that

(4.37)

P(c)+P(c^{\prime})\subseteq P(c+c^{\prime})\text{ for all $0<c,c^{\prime}<1$ with $c+c^{\prime}\leq 1$.}

In order to prove Theorem
4.7
, we first solve
\xi
for
h\in P(c)
with
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
, and then remove the additional condition
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
. To this end, we start by showing the following:

Proposition 4.23
.

If
\xi\colon P\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
is an
almost Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 4
in the sense that

\xi(h_{1})+\xi(h_{2})\equiv\xi(h_{3})+\xi(h_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})}

for all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}\in P
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
and with
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
being
p
-linearly independent, there exist a constant
0<c\leq 1
depending only on
s

and a locally linear map
T\colon P(c)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

\xi(h)\equiv T(h)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}
for all
h\in P(c)
with
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
.

Step 1: a preliminary description for
\xi(x)-\xi(y)
.

We first show that
\xi(x)-\xi(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}
depends only on the difference
x-y
.
For
t\in P(\frac{1}{2})
, denote
\xi_{t}(x):=\xi(x+t)-\xi(x)
. By assumption, we have that

\xi_{t}(x)=\xi_{t}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y),\iota(t)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,t\in P(\frac{1}{2})
(which ensures that
x+t,y+t\in P
by (
4.37
)).
By Proposition
4.12
, since
d\geq s+10
, there exists
G_{t}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

\xi_{t}(x)\equiv G_{t}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(t)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,t\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
In other words,

\xi(x)-\xi(y)\equiv G_{x-y}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
(which enforces
x-y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
by (
4.37
)).

Applying Proposition
4.10
to
J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}
,
since

d\geq 7
, then we may write

(4.38)

\xi(x)-\xi(y)\equiv L_{x}A_{x,y}-L_{y}B_{x,y}+G_{x-y}\mod J^{M}

for some
A_{x,y},B_{x,y}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

Step 2: solutions to
A_{x,y}
and
B_{x,y}
.

Our next step is to use a cocycle approach to solve
A_{x,y}
and
B_{x,y}
from (
4.38
).
Using (
4.38
) and the identity
\xi(x)-\xi(z)=(\xi(x)-\xi(y))+(\xi(y)-\xi(z))
, we have that

(4.39)

L_{x}(A_{x,y}-A_{x,z})+L_{y}(A_{y,z}-B_{x,y})+L_{z}(B_{x,z}-B_{y,z})+(G_{x-y}+G_{y-z}-G_{x-z})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. For convenience denote

\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}:=A_{x+t,y+t}-A_{x,y}\text{ and }\Delta_{t}B_{x,y}:=B_{x+t,y+t}-B_{x,y}.

Replacing
x,y,z
by
x+t,y+t,z+t
in (
4.39
) to cancel the term
G_{x-y}+G_{y-z}-G_{x-z}
, we deduce that

(4.40)

\begin{split}&\quad L_{x}(\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}-\Delta_{t}A_{x,z})+L_{y}(\Delta_{t}A_{y,z}-\Delta_{t}B_{x,y})+L_{z}(\Delta_{t}B_{x,z}-\Delta_{t}B_{y,z})\\
&\equiv-L_{t}((A_{x+t,y+t}+A_{y+t,z+t}-A_{x+t,z+t})-(B_{x+t,y+t}+B_{y+t,z+t}-B_{x+t,z+t}))\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z,t\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. In particular,
L_{x}(\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}-\Delta_{t}A_{x,z})\in J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(z),\iota(t)}
. Since
x,y,z,t
are
p
-linearly independent and
d\geq 11
,
by Lemma
4.8
,
we have that

\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}\equiv\Delta_{t}A_{x,z}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(z),\iota(t)}.

By Proposition
4.12
, since
d\geq s+10
, there exists
C_{x,t}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
such that

(4.41)

\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}\equiv C_{x,t}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(t)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,t\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

We solve
C_{x,t}
before solving
A_{x,y}
.
Since
\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}+\Delta_{s}A_{x+t,y+t}=\Delta_{t+s}A_{x,y}
for all
x,y,t,s\in P(O(1)^{-1})
, writing
C^{\prime}_{x,y}:=C_{x,y-x}
,
a=x,b=x+t,c=x+s+t
, it follows from (
4.41
) that

C^{\prime}_{a,b}+C^{\prime}_{b,c}\equiv C^{\prime}_{a,c}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(a-b),\iota(b-c),\iota(y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
a,b,c,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. By Proposition
4.9
(setting
k=1,\dim(U_{1})=1,\dim(V)=2
and
m=1
), since

d\geq\max\{s+2,11\}
, we have

C^{\prime}_{a,b}+C^{\prime}_{b,c}\equiv C^{\prime}_{a,c}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(a-b),\iota(b-v)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
a,b,c\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
By Proposition
4.21
, since
d\geq s+8
, there exist
\phi(x)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
for all
x\in P(O(1)^{-1})
such that

C^{\prime}_{x,y}\equiv\phi(y)-\phi(x)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x-y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

We now use the solution of
C^{\prime}_{x,y}
to solve
A_{x,y}
. By (
4.41
),

A_{x+t,y+t}-A_{x,y}=\Delta_{t}A_{x,y}\equiv C_{x,t}=C^{\prime}_{x,x+t}\equiv\phi(x+t)-\phi(x)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(t)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,t\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
Writing
y^{\prime}=y+t
,
z=x-y
and
A^{\prime}_{z,y}:=A_{y+z,y}-\phi(y+z)
, we have that

A^{\prime}_{z,y}\equiv A^{\prime}_{z,y^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(y^{\prime})}

for all
p
-linearly independent
y,y^{\prime},z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
By Proposition
4.12
, since
d\geq s+8
, there exist
f(z)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
for all
z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
such that

A^{\prime}_{z,y}\equiv f(z)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
y,z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. So

(4.42)

A_{x,y}\equiv\phi(x)+f(x-y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

We may solve
B_{x,y}
in a similar way and conclude that there exist
\psi(y)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
for all
y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
and
g(z)\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
for all
z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
such that

(4.43)

B_{x,y}\equiv\psi(y)+g(x-y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

Step 3: a description for
\phi-\psi
.

We next determine the connection between
\phi
and
\psi
.
Applying Lemma
4.8
to the term
L_{y}(\Delta_{t}A_{y,z}-\Delta_{t}B_{x,y})
in (
4.40
), we have that

\Delta_{t}A_{y,z}\equiv\Delta_{t}B_{x,y}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(z),\iota(t)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z,t\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. This implies that

\phi(y)-\psi(y)\equiv\phi(y+t)-\psi(y+t)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(z),\iota(t)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z,t\in P(O(1)^{-1})
, which implies that

\phi(y)-\psi(y)\equiv\phi(y^{\prime})-\psi(y^{\prime})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(z),\iota(y-y^{\prime})}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,y^{\prime},z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. By
Proposition
4.9
(setting
k=1,\dim(U_{1})=1,\dim(V)=1
and
m=2
),
since
d\geq\max\{s+2,11\}
, we have that

\phi(y)-\psi(y)\equiv\phi(y^{\prime})-\psi(y^{\prime})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y-y^{\prime})}

for all
p
-linearly independent
y,y^{\prime}\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

By Proposition
4.16
, since
d\geq s+7
, there exists a super polynomial
Q\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s-1)
such that
\phi(y)-\psi(y)\equiv Q(L_{y})\mod J^{M}
for all
y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
with
\iota(y)\neq\mathbb{0}
.

We now substitute the expression of
\phi(y)-\psi(y)
to the previous equations.
Note that

\psi(y)+g(x-y)-\phi(y)=Q(L_{y})+g(x-y)\equiv Q(L_{x-y})+g(x-y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}.

So
replacing
g(x-y)
by
Q(L_{x-y})+g(x-y)
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that
Q\equiv 0
and
\phi(x)=\psi(x)
for all
x\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. By Proposition
4.10
, (
4.38
), (
4.42
) and (
4.43
),

\begin{split}&\quad\xi(x)-\xi(y)\equiv L_{x}A_{x,y}-L_{y}B_{x,y}+G_{x-y}\\
&\equiv L_{x}\phi(x)-L_{y}\phi(y)+L_{x}f(x-y)-L_{y}g(x-y)+G_{x-y}+L_{x}L_{y}R_{x,y}\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for some
R_{x,y}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. Writing

(4.44)

\begin{split}\xi^{\prime}(x):=\xi(x)-L_{x}\phi(x),\end{split}

F=f-g
and
G^{\prime}_{z}:=G_{z}+L_{z}f(z)
, we have that

(4.45)

\begin{split}\xi^{\prime}(x)-\xi^{\prime}(y)\equiv L_{y}F(x-y)+G^{\prime}_{x-y}+L_{x}L_{y}R_{x,y}\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

Step 4: the elimination of
G^{\prime}_{x-y}
.

Next we deal with the term
G^{\prime}_{x-y}
by using Proposition
4.22
. Note that (
4.45
) implies that

\xi^{\prime}(x)-\xi^{\prime}(y)\equiv G^{\prime}_{x-y}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
Using the identity
\xi^{\prime}(x)-\xi^{\prime}(z)=(\xi^{\prime}(x)-\xi^{\prime}(y))-(\xi^{\prime}(y)-\xi^{\prime}(z))
, we have that

G^{\prime}_{x-y}+G^{\prime}_{y-z}\equiv G^{\prime}_{x-z}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(z)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y,z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. Writing
a=x-y
and
b=y-z
, we have that

G^{\prime}_{a}+G^{\prime}_{b}\equiv G^{\prime}_{a+b}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(b),\iota(z)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
z,a,b\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. By Proposition
4.9
(setting
k=m=\dim(U_{1})=1,\dim(V)=1
), since
d\geq\max\{s+2,9\}
, we have that

G^{\prime}_{a}+G^{\prime}_{b}\equiv G^{\prime}_{a+b}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(b)}.

Similarly,
G^{\prime}_{a}+G^{\prime}_{b}\equiv G^{\prime}_{a+b}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(a)}
and so

G^{\prime}_{a}+G^{\prime}_{b}\equiv G^{\prime}_{a+b}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(a)}\cap J^{M}_{\iota(b)}

for all
p
-linearly independent
a,b\in P(O(1)^{-1})
. By Proposition
4.22
, since
d\geq s+9
, there exist a super polynomial
R\colon\text{HP}_{d}(1)\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
with
R(0)=0
and a locally linear map
T\colon P(O(1)^{-1})\to\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

(4.46)

\begin{split}G^{\prime}_{a}\equiv R(L_{a})+T(a)\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all

a\in P(O(1)^{-1})\backslash Z
, where
Z
is the set of
a\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that
\iota(a)=\mathbb{0}
.
Since
R
is a super polynomial with
R(0)=0
,
it is not hard to see that

(4.47)

\begin{split}R(L_{a})+R(L_{b})\equiv R(L_{a+b})+L_{a}L_{b}R^{\prime}_{a,b}\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for some
R^{\prime}_{a,b}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.

We now substitute the expression of
G^{\prime}_{x-y}
to the previous equations.
By (
4.45
), (
4.46
) and (
4.47
), writing

(4.48)

\begin{split}\xi^{\prime\prime}(x):=\xi^{\prime}(x)-T(x)-R(L_{x}),\end{split}

we have that

(4.49)

\begin{split}\xi^{\prime\prime}(x)-\xi^{\prime\prime}(y)\equiv L_{y}F(x-y)+L_{x}^{2}R_{1,x,y}+L_{x}L_{y}R_{2,x,y}+L^{2}_{y}R_{3,x,y}\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for some
R_{1,x,y},R_{2,x,y},R_{3,x,y}\in\text{HP}_{d}(s-2)

for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.

Step 5: completion of the proof.

It follows from (
4.49
) that

\xi^{\prime\prime}(x)\equiv\xi^{\prime\prime}(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}
for all
p
-linearly independent
x,y\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
Since
d\geq s+8
, by Proposition
4.12
, there exists
C\in\text{HP}_{d}(s)
such that

(4.50)

\begin{split}\xi^{\prime\prime}(x)\equiv C\mod J^{M}\end{split}

for all
x\in P(O(1)^{-1})
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
.
Combining (
4.44
), (
4.48
) and (
4.50
), we have that

\xi(x)\equiv T(x)+C+R(L_{x})+L_{x}\phi(x)\mod J^{M}

and thus

(4.51)

\begin{split}\xi(x)\equiv T(x)+C\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}\end{split}

for all
x\in P(O(1)^{-1})
with
\iota(x)\neq\mathbb{0}
(since
R(0)=0
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
4.23
.

We now continue to prove Theorem
4.7
based on the construction given by (
4.51
).
Note that for all
x\in P(O(1)^{-1})
with
\iota(x)=\mathbb{0}
, and for any
p
-linearly independent
y,z\in P(O(1)^{-1})
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad\xi(x)+T(y+z)+C\equiv\xi(x)+\xi(y+z)\\
&\equiv\xi(x+y)+\xi(z)\equiv T(x+y)+T(z)+2C\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(z)},\end{split}

which implies that

\xi(x)\equiv T(x)+C\mod J^{M}_{\iota(y),\iota(z)}

since
T
is locally linear.
By Proposition
4.9
, since

d\geq\max\{s+2,9\}
,
we have

\xi(x)\equiv T(x)+C\mod J^{M}

and thus
\xi(x)\equiv T(x)+C\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}
for all
x\in P(O(1)^{-1})
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
4.7
.

4.7.
Lifting Theorem
4.7
by the
p
-expansion trick

In this section, we use Theorem
4.7
and the
p
-expansion trick to deduce Theorem
4.3
.

If
r=0
, then
\xi(P)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z},d}(s)\subseteq J^{M}
and so we can simply take
T\equiv 0
. Now suppose that the conclusion of Theorem
4.3
holds if
\xi(P)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
, we show that the same conclusion holds if
\xi(P)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r+1},d}(s)
.

Note that if
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 4, then so is
p\xi
.
So by induction hypothesis, there exists a locally linear map
T\colon P(c)\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
such that

p\xi(h)\equiv T(h)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in P(c)
.
Let
A
be the matrix associated with
M
,
and
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be the quadratic form given by
\tilde{M}(n):=\frac{1}{p}((n\tau(A))\cdot n)
. Denote
\tilde{L}_{h}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(n\tau(A))\cdot h
for all
n,h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Since
d\geq 5
, by Proposition
A.9
, for all
h\in P(c)
, we may write

Q_{0}(p\xi(h)-T(h))=\sum_{i=(i_{0},i_{1})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s,i_{0}+i_{1}\leq r}R_{h,i}\tilde{M}^{i_{0}}\tilde{L}_{h}^{i_{1}}

for some
Q_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q_{0}\leq O_{d}(1)
and some
R_{h,i}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z},d}(s-(2i_{0}+i_{1}))
, where we slight abuse the notation to treat
h
as an element in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
in the expression
\tilde{L}_{h}
.
So

(4.52)

\begin{split}Q_{0}\xi(h)=\frac{T(h)}{p}+\sum_{i=(i_{0},i_{1})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s,i_{0}+i_{1}\leq r}R^{\prime}_{h,i}\tilde{M}^{i_{0}}\tilde{L}_{h}^{i_{1}},\end{split}

where
R^{\prime}_{h,i}:=\frac{R_{h,i}}{p}
. Denote

V(h_{1},\dots,h_{k}):=\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h_{1})\dots,\iota(h_{k})\}^{\perp_{M}}

for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.
Fix any
h_{1},\dots,h_{4}\in P(c)
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
. Since
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 4, for all
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}))
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, since
n+pm\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}))
, we have that

(4.53)

\begin{split}&\quad 0\equiv Q_{0}(\xi(h_{1})+\xi(h_{2})-\xi(h_{3})-\xi(h_{4}))(n+pm)\\
&\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{4}c_{j}\sum_{i=(i_{0},i_{1})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s}(R^{\prime}_{h_{j},i}\tilde{M}^{i_{0}}\tilde{L}_{h_{j}}^{i_{1}})(n+pm)\mod\mathbb{Z},\end{split}

where
c_{1}=c_{2}:=1
and
c_{3}=c_{4}:=-1
. Note that

\tilde{M}(n+pm)\equiv\tilde{M}(n)+2(n\tau(A))\cdot m\mod p\mathbb{Z}

and

\tilde{L}_{h_{j}}(n+pm)\equiv\tilde{L}_{h_{j}}(n)+(h_{j}\tau(A))\cdot m\mod p\mathbb{Z}

for all
1\leq j\leq 4
.
Since
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}))=V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}))
and since
R_{h_{j},i}
has integer coefficients, it follows from (
4.53
) that

(4.54)

\begin{split}\sum_{j=1}^{4}c_{j}\sum_{i=(i_{0},i_{1})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s}R^{\prime}_{h_{j},i}(n)(\tilde{M}(n)+2(n\tau(A))\cdot m)^{i_{0}}(\tilde{L}_{h_{j}}(n)+(h_{j}\tau(A))\cdot m)^{i_{1}}\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Since
\tilde{M}
is non-degenerate,
if
n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
are
p
-linearly independent, then the map

m\mapsto(2(n\tau(A))\cdot m,(h_{1}\tau(A))\cdot m,\dots,(h_{4}\tau(A))\cdot m)\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{5}

is an injection from
[p]^{d}
to
\{(x_{0},\dots,x_{4})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{5}\colon x_{1}+x_{2}=x_{3}+x_{4}\}
.
Since the left hand side of (
4.54
) is a
\mathbb{Z}/p
-coefficient polynomial in
m
, we have that

\begin{split}\sum_{j=1}^{4}c_{j}\sum_{i=(i_{0},i_{1})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s}R^{\prime}_{h_{j},i}(n)x_{0}^{i_{0}}x_{j}^{i_{1}}\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all
x_{0},\dots,x_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
x_{1}+x_{2}\equiv x_{3}+x_{4}\mod p\mathbb{Z}
.

We claim that
for all
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}))
with
n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
being
p
-linearly independent, we have that

(1)

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},i_{1})}(n),\dots,R^{\prime}_{h_{4},(i_{0},i_{1})}(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
i_{1}\geq 2
;

(2)

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},1)}(n)\equiv\dots\equiv R^{\prime}_{h_{4},(i_{0},1)}(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
for all
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
;

(3)

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},0)}(n)+R^{\prime}_{h_{2},(i_{0},0)}(n)\equiv R^{\prime}_{h_{3},(i_{0},0)}(n)+R^{\prime}_{h_{4},(i_{0},0)}(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
for all
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
.

It is convenient to prove the claim in the
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
setting. It suffices to show that for any
F\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{5}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
s
given by

F(x_{0},\dots,x_{4}):=\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{i=(i_{0},i_{1})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s}c_{i,j}x_{0}^{i_{0}}x_{j}^{i_{1}}

for some
c_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, if
F(x_{0},\dots,x_{4})=0
whenever
x_{1}+x_{2}=x_{3}+x_{4}
, we have that

•

c_{i,1}=\dots=c_{i,4}=0
for all
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
i_{1}\geq 2
;

•

c_{i,1}=c_{i,2}=-c_{i,3}=-c_{i,4}
for all
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
;

•

c_{i,1}+\dots+c_{i,4}=0.
for all
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Indeed, separating the variable
x_{0}
, we see that for any
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

F_{i_{0}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{4}):=\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{i_{1}\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}\leq s}c_{i,j}x_{j}^{i_{1}}

is equal to zero whenever
x_{1}+x_{2}=x_{3}+x_{4}
. So
F_{i_{0}}
is divisible by
x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3}-x_{4}
. Comparing the terms of different degrees, we have that

F_{i_{0},i_{1}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{4}):=\sum_{j=1}^{4}c_{i,j}x_{j}^{i_{1}}

is divisible by
x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3}-x_{4}
for all
i_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
. This is equivalent of saying that

(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\mapsto c_{i,1}x_{1}^{i_{1}}+c_{i,2}x_{2}^{i_{1}}+c_{i,3}x_{3}^{i_{1}}+c_{i,4}(x_{1}+x_{2}-x_{3})^{i_{1}}

is the trivial polynomial, which is already impossible if
i_{1}\geq 2
unless
c_{i,1}=\dots=c_{i,4}=0
. If
i_{1}=1
, then this is only possible if
c_{i,1}=c_{i,2}=-c_{i,3}=-c_{i,4}
. If
i_{1}=0
, then this is only possible if
c_{i,1}+\dots+c_{i,4}=0.
This completes the proof of the claim.

Note that if
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
are
p
-linearly independent, then the number of
n\in[p]^{d}
with
n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
being
p
-linearly dependent is
p^{3}
. On the other hand, the cardinality of the set
V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}))\cap[p]^{d}
is
O(p^{d-4})
by Lemma
A.6
(translated into the
\mathbb{Z}
-setting). Since
d\geq 8
, it follows from Proposition
A.8
that conditions (i)–(iii) hold for all
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}))
for all
p
-linearly independent
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in P(c)
.

Note that if
i_{1}\geq 2
, then Condition (i) implies that

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},i_{1})}\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})}
for all
p
-linearly independent
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in P(c)
. Since
d\geq\max\{s+3,11\}
, it follows from Proposition
3.10
(setting
k=0,m=2,\dim(V)=1
) that

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},i_{1})}\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1})}

for all
h_{1}\in P(c)
with
\iota(h_{1})\neq\mathbb{0}
. Condition (ii) implies that

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},1)}\equiv R^{\prime}_{h_{2},(i_{0},1)}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})}

for all
p
-linearly independent
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in P(c)
.
Since
d\geq\max\{s+3,11\}
, it follows from Proposition
3.10
(setting
k=0,m=1,\dim(V)=2
) that

R^{\prime}_{h_{1},(i_{0},1)}\equiv R^{\prime}_{h_{2},(i_{0},1)}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2})}

for all
p
-linearly independent
h_{1},h_{2}\in P(c)
. So identifying
R^{\prime}_{h_{j},i}
as elements in
\text{HP}_{d}(s)
, it follows from Proposition
4.12
(since
d\geq s+8
) that
there exists
G_{i_{0}}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p,d}(s)
such that

R^{\prime}_{h,(i_{0},1)}\equiv G_{i_{0}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in P(c)
with
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
.
Finally, by identifying
R^{\prime}_{h_{j},i}
as elements in
\text{HP}_{d}(s)
,
by condition (iii), and by using Proposition
4.23
, we have that there exist
0<c^{\prime}:=c^{\prime}(c,s)<1
(which eventually depends only on
r
and
s
) and a
locally linear map
T_{i_{0}}\colon P(c^{\prime})\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p,d}(s)
such that

R^{\prime}_{h,(i_{0},0)}\equiv T_{i_{0}}(h)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in P(c^{\prime})
with
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
.
It then follows from (
4.52
) that for all
h\in P(c^{\prime})
with
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
and
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap\iota^{-1}(V(h))
,

\begin{split}&\quad\xi(h)(n)\equiv Q^{\ast}Q_{0}\xi(h)(n)\\
&\equiv Q^{\ast}\Bigl(\frac{T(h)(n)}{p}+\sum_{i=0}^{\min\{\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\rfloor,r\}}\tilde{M}(n)^{i}T_{i}(h)(n)+\sum_{i=0}^{\min\{\lfloor\frac{s-1}{2}\rfloor,r-1\}}\tilde{M}(n)^{i}G_{i}(n)\tilde{L}_{h}(n)\Bigr)\mod\mathbb{Z},\end{split}

where
Q^{\ast}
is any integer with
Q^{\ast}Q_{0}\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
.
In other words,

\xi(h)\equiv T^{\prime}(h):=Q^{\ast}\Bigl(\frac{T(h)}{p}+\sum_{i=0}^{\min\{\lfloor\frac{s}{2}\rfloor,r\}}\tilde{M}^{i}T_{i}(h)+\sum_{i=0}^{\min\{\lfloor\frac{s-1}{2}\rfloor,r-1\}}\tilde{M}^{i}G_{i}\tilde{L}_{h}\Bigr)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in P(c^{\prime})
with
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
, where
T^{\prime}
clearly is a locally linear map from
P(c^{\prime})
to
\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r+1},d}(s)
.

Finally,
similar to the argument in Step 5 of Section
4.6
, we have that

\xi(h)\equiv T^{\prime}(h)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}
for all
h\in P(c^{\prime})
.
This completes the proof of Theorem
4.3
.

It is an interesting question to ask that in Theorem
4.3
, whether one can remove the dependence on
r
for the constant
c
. However, we do not need such an improvement in this series.

We conclude this section with a generalization of Proposition
4.12
, which will be used in
[
8
]
.

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d,r}1
be a prime dividing
K
,
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
,
\mathbb{0}\notin\iota(H)
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
Let
F\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
be a map such that

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}

for all
x,y\in H
. If
d\geq s+8
, then there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
such that

F(x)\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}

for all
x\in H
.

The approach to deduce Proposition
4.24
from Proposition
4.12
is very similar to the approach we use to deduce Theorem
4.3
from Proposition
4.23
. We leave the details to the interested readers.

5.
Structures for sets with small density dependence numbers

In this section, we extend some important results in additive combinatorics for shifted modules.

5.1.
Two notions of equivalence classes

Although
\sim
is not an equivalence relation
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\times\mathcal{J}(M)
, it is still possible to talk about equivalence classes in the following way:

Definition 5.1

(Equivalence classes)
.

We say that a subset
X
of
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
is an
equivalence class
(or a
weak equivalence class
) if
x\sim x^{\prime}
for all
x,x^{\prime}\in X
. Clearly,
\pi(x)
is a constant for all
x\in X
, and so we denote this constant by
\pi(X)
when there is no confusion.
We say that an equivalence class
X\subseteq\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
is a
strong equivalence class
if there exists
u\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u\sim x
for all
x\in X
.

Example 5.2
.

Assume that
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
M(n)=n\cdot n
. For
x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, let
L_{x}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(1)
be the map given by
L_{x}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(\tau(x)\cdot n)
. Let
e_{1},\dots,e_{d}
be the standard unit vectors of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Let
x_{1}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{e_{1}})
,
x_{2}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{e_{2}})
and
x_{3}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{e_{1}+e_{2}}+L_{e_{1}})
. It is not hard to check that
\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}
is a weak equivalence class in
\Gamma^{1}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
. On the other hand, suppose that there exists
x\in\Gamma^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
x\sim x_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq 3
. Since
x\in\Gamma^{1}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, we may write
x=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}+L_{y})
for some
y\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Since
x\sim x_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq 3
, we have that
y,y
and
y-e_{1}
are scalar multiples of
e_{1},e_{2}
and
e_{1}+e_{2}
respectively, which is not possible. So
\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}
is not a strong equivalence class.

Example 5.3
.

Let the notations be the same as Example
5.2
. Let
x_{4}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{e_{1}-e_{2}})
. It is not hard to see that
\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{4}\}
is a strong equivalence class since
(\mathbb{0},J^{M})\sim x_{i}
for all
i=1,2,4
. If we add
x_{3}
into this set, then it is not hard to see that
\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}\}
is a weak equivalence class. However, by Example
5.2
,
\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4}\}
is no longer a strong equivalence class.

In conclusion, Example
5.2
shows that there exists a weak equivalence class
X
which is not a strong equivalence class. Example
5.3
shows that there is a strong equivalent class
X
and some
u\in\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
x\sim u
for all
x\in X
, but the set
X\cup\{u\}
is no longer a strong equivalent class. In this paper, such equivalence classes
X
are considered as abnormal. In fact, the following proposition shows that “generic” equivalence classes do not have the properties mentioned above.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
d,k,k^{\prime},K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},k\geq 2
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\gg_{d}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
X\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be a finite set with
\pi(X)=\{h\}
for some
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.
Denote
t=2
if
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
and
t=0
otherwise. Then

(1)

if
d\geq 2k+2(s+1)(k-2)+5+t
and
X
is a weak equivalence class, then either
X
is a strong equivalence class or
there exists a subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(s+k-1)(k-1)
such that
V\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
for all
(h,J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
.

(2)

if
d\geq 2k^{\prime}+2(s+1)(k-2)+5+t
and
X
is a strong equivalence class, then either there exists a subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(s+k^{\prime}-1)(k-1)
such that
V\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
for all
(h,J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
, or for all
u\in\Gamma^{s}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u\sim a
for all
a\in X
, we have that
X\cup\{u\}
is also a strong equivalence class.

Before proving Proposition
5.4
, we need the following lemma from linear algebra:

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
d,k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime,
U
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k^{\prime}
(possibly the trivial subspace), and
\mathcal{U}
be a collection of subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
k
containing
U
.
Then either there
exist
V_{i}\in\mathcal{U},1\leq i\leq m
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{m}
are linearly independent modulo
U
, or there exists a subspace
W
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
k^{\prime}+(m-1)(k-k^{\prime})
containing
U
such that
W\cap V\neq U
for all
V\in\mathcal{U}
.

Proof.

Pick any
V_{1}\in\mathcal{U}
.
Suppose that we have chosen
V_{i}\in\mathcal{U},1\leq i\leq r
for some
1\leq r\leq m-1
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{r}
are linearly independent modulo
U
.
If for all
V\in\mathcal{U}
,
V\cap(V_{1}\dots+V_{r})\neq U
, then we are done since
\dim(V_{1}\dots+V_{r})=k^{\prime}+r(k-k^{\prime})\leq k^{\prime}+(m-1)(k-k^{\prime})
. If not, then we may find
V_{r+1}\in\mathcal{U}
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{r+1}
are linearly independent modulo
U
.

In conclusion, either we are done or there
exist
V_{i}\in\mathcal{U},1\leq i\leq m
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{m}
are linearly independent modulo
U
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.4
.

Since
X
is finite, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
for all
(h,J_{V}+f)\in X
.
We first consider the case
\iota(h)=\mathbb{0}
. For convenience we assume that
h=\mathbb{0}
.
We start with Part (i). Denote
K:=s+k
.
By Lemma
5.5
, either
there exists a subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(K-1)(k-1)
such that
V\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
for all
(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
, or there
exist
u_{i}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V_{i}}+f_{i})\in X,1\leq i\leq K
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent.
If it is the former case then we are done. We now assume that it is the latter case.

Obviously
(\mathbb{0},J^{M}+f_{1})\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
is equivalent to
u_{1}
.
Suppose that for some
1\leq n\leq s
, there exists
u\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
. We show that there exists
u^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u^{\prime}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n+1
.
Assume that
u=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}+f)
.
Then for all
1\leq i\leq m
, since
X
is an equivalence class, we have that
u\sim u_{i}\sim u_{n+1}
. So
f\equiv f_{i}\equiv f_{n+1}\mod J^{M}_{V_{n+1}+V_{i}}

and thus

f\equiv f_{n+1}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{n}J^{M}_{V_{n+1}+V_{i}}.

Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{n+1}
are linearly independent,
and
\pi(X)=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, the dimensions of
V_{1},\dots,V_{n+1}
are at most
k-1
.
Since
d\geq 2k+2s(k-2)+5
, by Lemma
3.9
(setting
V=V_{n+1}
,
m=k-1
,
r=k-1
, and
N=n
), we may
assume that
f-f_{n+1}=g+g^{\prime}
for some
g\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V_{n+1}}
and
g^{\prime}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap\cap_{i=1}^{n}J^{M}_{V^{\prime}_{i}}
, and set
u^{\prime}:=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}+(f-g^{\prime}))
.
Then
(f-g^{\prime})-f_{n+1}=g\in J^{M}_{V_{n+1}}
. On the other hand, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,

(f-g^{\prime})-f_{i}=(f-f_{i})-g^{\prime}\in J^{M}_{V_{i}}
. This means that
u^{\prime}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n+1
.

Inductively, we may find some
u_{0}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u_{0}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+1
.

Let
s+2\leq i\leq K
. Since
u_{0}\sim u_{j}\sim u_{i}
for
1\leq j\leq s+1
, we have that

f_{0}\equiv f_{j}\equiv f_{i}\mod J^{M}_{V_{j}+V_{i}}
and so
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\mod\cap_{j=1}^{s+1}J^{M}_{V_{j}+V_{i}}
. Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{s+1},V_{i}
are linearly independent and of dimensions at most
k-1
, and since
d\geq 2k+2(s+1)(k-2)+5
,
by Proposition
3.7
(setting
m=k-1
and
r=k-1
), we have that
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\mod J^{M}_{V_{i}}
and so
u_{0}\sim u_{i}
.
In other words,
u_{0}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq K
.

Now pick any
u^{\prime}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V^{\prime}}+f^{\prime})\in X
. Since
u_{0}\sim u_{i}\sim u^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq K
, we have that
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V_{i}+V^{\prime}}
. So
f_{0}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{K}J^{M}_{V_{i}+V^{\prime}}
. Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent and of dimensions at most
k-1
, and since
d\geq 2k+2(s+1)(k-2)+5
, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=k-1
and
r=k-1
), we have that
f_{0}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V}
. So
u_{0}\sim u^{\prime}
and thus
X
is a strong equivalence class.

We now prove Part (ii). Denote
K:=s+k^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
5.5
, either
there exists a subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(K-1)(k-1)
such that
V\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
for all
(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
, or there
exist
u_{i}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V_{i}}+f_{i})\in X,1\leq i\leq K
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent.
If it is the former case then we are done. We now assume that it is the latter case.

Let
u_{0}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}+f_{0})\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be such that
u_{0}\sim a
for all
a\in X
. It suffices to show that for all
u=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in\Gamma^{s}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, if
u\sim a
for all
a\in X
, then
u_{0}\sim u
. Since
u_{0}\sim u_{i}\sim u
for all
1\leq i\leq K
, we have that
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\equiv f\mod J^{M}_{V_{i}+V}
and
\pi(u_{0})=\pi(u)=\mathbb{0}
. So
V
is of dimension at most
k^{\prime}-1
and

f_{0}\equiv f\mod\cap_{i=1}^{K}J^{M}_{V_{i}+V}
. Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent and of dimension at most
k-1
, and since
d\geq 2k^{\prime}+2(s+1)(k-2)+5
, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=k^{\prime}-1
and
r=k-1
), we have that
f_{0}\equiv f\mod J^{M}_{V}
. So
u_{0}\sim u
and we are done.

We next consider the case
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
. The proof is very similar to the case
\iota(h)=\mathbb{0}
, except that the dimensions of relevant subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
appearing in the proof need to be changed slightly. We provide the details of the proof for completeness.

We start with Part (i). Denote
K:=s+k+1
.
By Lemma
5.5
, either
there exists a subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(K-1)(k-1)+1
containing
h
such that
V\cap W\neq\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
for all
(h,J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
, or there
exist
u_{i}=(h,J^{M}_{V_{i}}+f_{i})\in X,1\leq i\leq K
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent modulo
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
.
If it is the former case then we are done. We now assume that it is the latter case.

Obviously
(h,J^{M}_{h}+f_{1})\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
is equivalent to
u_{1}
.
Suppose that for some
1\leq n\leq s
, there exists
u\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n
. We show that there exists
u^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u^{\prime}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n+1
.
Assume that
u=(h,J^{M}_{h}+f)
.
Then for all
1\leq i\leq m
, since
X
is an equivalence class, we have that
u\sim u_{i}\sim u_{n+1}
. So
f\equiv f_{i}\equiv f_{n+1}\mod J^{M}_{V_{n+1}+V_{i}}

and thus

f\equiv f_{n+1}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{n}J^{M}_{V_{n+1}+V_{i}}.

Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{n+1}
are linearly independent modulo
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{h\}
,
we may write
V_{n+1}+V_{i}=V_{n+1}+V^{\prime}_{i},1\leq i\leq n
for some subspaces
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{n}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

of dimensions at most
k-1
such that
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{n},V_{n+1}
are linearly independent. Since
d\geq 2k+2s(k-2)+7
,
by Lemma
3.9
(setting
V=V_{n+1}
,
m=k
,
r=k-1
and
N=n
), we may
assume that
f-f_{n+1}=g+g^{\prime}
for some
g\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V_{n+1}}
and
g^{\prime}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap\cap_{i=1}^{n}J^{M}_{V^{\prime}_{i}}
. Set
u^{\prime}:=(h,J^{M}_{h}+(f-g^{\prime}))
.
Then
(f-g^{\prime})-f_{n+1}=g\in J^{M}_{V_{n+1}}
. On the other hand, for all
1\leq i\leq n
,

(f-g^{\prime})-f_{i}=(f-f_{i})-g^{\prime}\in J^{M}_{V_{i}}
. This implies that
u^{\prime}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq n+1
.

Inductively, may find some
u_{0}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
u_{0}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+1
.

Let
s+2\leq i\leq K
. Since
u_{0}\sim u_{j}\sim u_{i}
for
1\leq j\leq s+1
, we have that

f_{0}\equiv f_{j}\equiv f_{i}\mod J^{M}_{V_{j}+V_{i}}
and so
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\mod\cap_{j=1}^{s+1}J^{M}_{V_{j}+V_{i}}
. Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{s+1},V_{i}
are linearly independent modulo
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
, we may write
V_{j}+V_{i}=V^{\prime}_{j}+V_{i},1\leq j\leq s+1
for some subspaces
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{s+1}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

of dimensions at most
k-1
such that
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{s+1},V_{i}
are linearly independent. Since
d\geq 2k+2(s+1)(k-2)+7
,
by Proposition
3.7
(setting
m=k
and
r=k-1
), we have that
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\mod J^{M}_{V_{i}}
and so
u_{0}\sim u_{i}
.
In other words,
u_{0}\sim u_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq K
.

Now pick any
u^{\prime}=(h,J^{M}_{V^{\prime}}+f^{\prime})\in X
. Since
u_{0}\sim u_{i}\sim u^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq K
, we have that
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V_{i}+V^{\prime}}
. So
f_{0}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{K}J^{M}_{V_{i}+V^{\prime}}
. Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent modulo
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
, we may write
V_{i}+V^{\prime}=V^{\prime}_{i}+V^{\prime},1\leq i\leq K
for some subspaces
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{K}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

of dimensions at most
k-1
such that
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{K}
are linearly independent. Since
d\geq 2k+2(s+1)(k-2)+7
, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=k
and
r=k-1
), we have that
f_{0}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V}
. So
u_{0}\sim u^{\prime}
and thus
X
is a strong equivalence class.

We now prove Part (ii). Denote
K:=s+k^{\prime}+1
.
By Lemma
5.5
, either
there exists a subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(K-1)(k-1)+1
containing
h
such that
V\cap W\neq\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
for all
(h,J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
, or there
exist
u_{i}=(h,J^{M}_{V_{i}}+f_{i})\in X,1\leq i\leq K
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent modulo
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
.
If it is the former case then we are done. We now assume that it is the latter case.

Let
u_{0}=(h,J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+f_{0})\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be such that
u_{0}\sim a
for all
a\in X
. It suffices to show that for all
u=(h,J^{M}_{V}+f)\in\Gamma^{s}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, if
u\sim a
for all
