a\in X
, then
u_{0}\sim u
. Since
u_{0}\sim u_{i}\sim u
for all
1\leq i\leq K
, we have that
f_{0}\equiv f_{i}\equiv f\mod J^{M}_{V_{i}+V}
. So

f_{0}\equiv f\mod\cap_{i=1}^{K}J^{M}_{V_{i}+V}
. Since
V_{1},\dots,V_{K}
are linearly independent modulo
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
,
we may write
V_{i}+V=V^{\prime}_{i}+V,1\leq i\leq K
for some subspace
V^{\prime}_{i}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
k-1
such that
V^{\prime}_{1},\dots,V^{\prime}_{K}
are linearly independent. Since
d\geq 2k^{\prime}+2(s+1)(k-2)+7
,
by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=k^{\prime}
and
r=k-1
), we have that
f_{0}\equiv f\mod J^{M}_{V}
. So
u_{0}\sim u
and we are done.
∎

5.2.
The
M
-energy graph

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form dividing
K
,
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, and

\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
be maps.
For
h\in H
, denote
\tilde{\xi}_{i}(h):=(h,J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+\xi_{i}(h))\in\Gamma_{1}^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
. Consider the directed graph
(H^{2},E)
where
((h_{1},h_{2}),(h_{3},h_{4}))\in E
if and only if
\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h_{1})\hat{-}\tilde{\xi}_{2}(h_{2})\sim\tilde{\xi}_{3}(h_{3})\hat{-}\tilde{\xi}_{4}(h_{4})
. We call
(H^{2},E)
the
M
-energy graph
of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
, and
|E|
the
M
-energy
of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
.
For convenience we define the
M
-energy graph/
M
-energy
of
\xi_{1}
to be the

M
-energy graph/
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{1},\xi_{1},\xi_{1})
(in which case the energy graph is undirected).

In this section, we prove the following Cauchy-Schwartz inequality for
M
-energy:

Theorem 5.6

(Cauchy-Schwartz inequality)
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\geq\max\{s+4,13\}
,
\delta>0
, and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
. If the
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
is at least
\delta K^{3d}
, then the
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
is
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{3d}
.

We start with the following technical proposition.

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq\max\{s+4,13\}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, and
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
. If the
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
is at least
\delta K^{3d}
, then there exist
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
and a set
C_{h}\subseteq H^{\prime}\times H^{\prime}
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
such that for all
h\in H^{\prime}

•

|C_{h}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
;

•

h_{1}-h_{2}=h
for all
(h_{1},h_{2})\in C_{h}
;

•

\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h_{1})\hat{-}\tilde{\xi}_{2}(h_{2})\sim\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h^{\prime}_{1})\hat{-}\tilde{\xi}_{2}(h^{\prime}_{2})
for all
(h_{1},h_{2}),(h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2})\in C_{h}
.

Similarly, there exist
H^{\prime\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
and a set
C^{\prime}_{h}\subseteq H^{\prime\prime}\times H^{\prime\prime}
for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
such that for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}

•

|C^{\prime}_{h}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
;

•

h_{1}+h_{4}=h
for all
(h_{1},h_{4})\in C^{\prime}_{h}
;

•

\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}_{4}(h_{4})\sim\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h^{\prime}_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}_{4}(h^{\prime}_{4})
for all
(h_{1},h_{4}),(h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{4})\in C^{\prime}_{h}
.

Proof.

Let
G:=(H^{2},E)
be the
M
-energy graph of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
.
For
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, let
U_{h}
be the set of all vertices
(h_{1},h_{2})\in H^{2}
such that
h_{1}+h_{2}=h
.
Clearly,
E
does not contain any edge connecting vertices from two different
U_{h}
. So
G
has a disjoint partition
G_{h}:=(U_{h},E_{h}),h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
. Since
\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}|E_{h}|=|E|\geq\delta K^{3d}
, and
|E_{h}|\leq K^{2d}
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, we have that there exists
H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H^{\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
such that
|E_{h}|\geq\delta K^{2d}/2
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
.

Fix
h\in H^{\prime}
and consider the graph
G_{h}=(U_{h},E_{h})
. Enumerate
U_{h}:=\{n_{1},\dots,n_{|U_{h}|}\}
, and let
a_{i}
denote the number of edges in
G_{h}
with one of the vertices being
n_{i}
.
We say that
w=(v_{1},\dots,v_{s+4})\in U_{h}^{s+4}
is
good
if writing
v_{i}=(u_{i,1},u_{i,2})=(u_{i,1},u_{i,1}-h)
for
1\leq i\leq s+4
,
we have that

u_{i,1},1\leq i\leq s+4
are
p
-linearly independent.
The number of tuples
(v_{1},\dots,v_{s+4})\in U_{h}^{s+4}
is at most

|U_{h}|^{s+4}
. On the other hand, the number of tuples
(v,v_{1},\dots,v_{s+4})\in U_{h}^{s+5}
such that
(v,v_{1}),\dots,(v,v_{s+4})\in E_{h}
is at least

\sum_{i=1}^{|U_{h}|}a^{s+4}_{i}\geq\frac{(\sum_{i=1}^{|U_{h}|}a_{i})^{s+4}}{|U_{h}|^{s+3}}=\frac{|E_{h}|^{s+4}}{|U_{h}|^{s+3}}\gg_{\delta,s}K^{(s+5)d}.

For each
w=(v_{1},\dots,v_{s+4})\in U_{h}^{s+4}
, let
W_{w}
denote the set
v\in U_{h}
such that
(v,v_{1}),\dots,(v,

v_{s+4})\in E_{h}
. Since
|W_{w}|\leq K^{d}
for all
w
, the number of
w\in U_{h}^{s+4}
such that
|W_{w}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
is
\gg_{\delta,s}K^{(s+4)d}
. Since
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
, we may choose such a
w
which is also good (in fact, it is not hard to see from the fact
u_{i,1}-u_{i,2}=h
and Lemma
A.3
that the number
w\in U^{s+4}_{h}
which is not good is at most
O_{\delta,d}(K^{(s+4)d}/p^{d-(s+3)})
since
d\geq s+4
). Fix such a choice of

w=(v_{1},\dots,v_{s+4})
, with
v_{i}=(u_{i,1},u_{i,2})
for
1\leq i\leq s+4
.
Pick
(h_{1},h_{2}),(h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2})\in W_{w}
. Since
((h_{1},h_{2}),v_{i}),((h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2}),v_{i})\in E
, we have that

(5.1)

\xi_{1}(h_{1})-\xi_{2}(h_{2})\equiv\xi_{3}(u_{i,1})-\xi_{4}(u_{i,2})\equiv\xi_{1}(h^{\prime}_{1})-\xi_{2}(h^{\prime}_{2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{2}),\iota(u_{i,1}),\iota(u_{i,2})}

for
1\leq i\leq s+4
.
Therefore,

(5.2)

\xi_{1}(h_{1})-\xi_{2}(h_{2})\equiv\xi_{1}(h^{\prime}_{1})-\xi_{2}(h^{\prime}_{2})\mod\bigcap_{i=1}^{s+4}J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{2}),\iota(u_{i,1}),\iota(u_{i,2})}.

By the definition of
U_{h}
, we have that
\bigcap_{i=1}^{s+4}J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{2}),\iota(u_{i,1}),\iota(u_{i,2})}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{s+4}J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(u_{i,1}),\iota(h)}
and
J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h)}=J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{2})}.

Since
u_{i,1},1\leq i\leq s+4
are
p
-linearly independent and
d\geq 13
, it follows from (
5.2
) and Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=3
and
r=1
) that

(5.3)

\xi_{1}(h_{1})-\xi_{2}(h_{2})\equiv\xi_{1}(h^{\prime}_{1})-\xi_{2}(h^{\prime}_{2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{2})}.

Since
h_{1}-h_{2}=h
for all
(h_{1},h_{2})\in W_{w}
, we are done by setting
C_{h}=W_{w}
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
.

The existence of
H^{\prime\prime}
and
C^{\prime}_{h}
can be proved in a similar way, and we leave the details to the interested readers.
∎

Remark 5.8
.

We briefly explain the reason why we consider the set
U_{h}^{s+4}
in the proof of Proposition
5.7
. If we used a similar argument with
w=v_{1}\in U_{h}
instead of
w=(v_{1},\dots,v_{s+4})\in U_{h}^{s+4}
, we would arrive at (
5.1
) with
i=1
, which is insufficient to deduce (
5.3
). This is because there is a loss of information in (
5.1
) where we need to modulo an additional module
J^{M}_{\iota(u_{1,1}),\iota(u_{1,2})}
. In order to overcome this difficulty, we use the intersection approach to find
s+4
different
v_{i}
in general positions, and then intersect all the modules
J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(h^{\prime}_{2}),\iota(u_{i,1}),\iota(u_{i,2})}
to recover the lost information (the number
s+4
is determined by the intersection properties for
M
-modules proved in Section
3
). Similar approaches will be frequently used in this paper.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
5.6
.

Proof of Theorem
5.6
.

By Proposition
5.7
, there exist
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
and a set
C_{h}\subseteq H^{\prime}\times H^{\prime}
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
such that for all
h\in H^{\prime}
,

•

|C_{h}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
;

•

h_{1}-h_{2}=h
for all
(h_{1},h_{2})\in C_{h}
;

•

\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h_{1})\hat{-}\tilde{\xi}_{2}(h_{2})\sim\tilde{\xi}_{1}(h^{\prime}_{1})\hat{-}\tilde{\xi}_{2}(h^{\prime}_{2})
for all
(h_{1},h_{2}),(h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2})\in C_{h}
.

This means that the
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
is at least

\sum_{h\in H^{\prime}}|C_{h}|^{2}\gg_{\delta,d}K^{3d}.

∎

5.3.
The Balog-Gowers-Szemerédi theorem

Let
G
be a discrete abelian group and
H
be a subset of
G
. The Balog-Gowers-Szemerédi Theorem says that if
H
has a large additive energy, then there exists a large subset
H^{\prime}
of
H
with a small doubling constant
\frac{|H^{\prime}+H^{\prime}|}{|H^{\prime}|}
(see for example
[
10
]
). We extend this result to shifted modules in this section.

For
A,B\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, we use the notation
A\hat{+}B
to denote the set of elements of the form
a\hat{+}b
for some
a\in A
and
b\in B
, and we define
A\hat{-}B
in a similar way. We refer the readers to Section
2.2
to recall the definitions we use in graph theory.

Theorem 5.9

(Balog-Gowers-Szemerédi Theorem for
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\geq 2s+15
,
\delta>0
and

p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
\xi\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
. If the
M
-energy of
\xi
is at least
\delta K^{3d}
, then
there exists
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}))=O_{\delta,d}(1)
.

Note that in our setting, given a subset
X
of
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, there is no such a concept as the “doubling constant” of
X
. So in Theorem
5.9
, we use the density dependence number of the relation graph of
X
to replace the doubling constant in the conventional additive combinatorics. It is helpful for the readers to informally interpret the conclusion
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}))=O_{\delta,d}(1)
as
\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})
having a “doubling constant”
O_{\delta,d}(1)
.

Remark 5.10
.

Intuitively, compared with the density dependence number, the clique covering number is a more natural replacement for the cardinality of subsets of
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
. The reason we use the density dependence number in Theorem
5.9
(as well as in other results in Section
5
) is because it is easier to handle.
In Section
6
, we will explain how to pass from the density dependence number to the clique covering number.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
5.9
.
Our method to prove Theorem
5.9
is inspired by the work of Ballog and Szemerédi
[
1
]
.
The approach in
[
1
]
relies heavily on the analysis of the cardinality of the level set, i.e. a set of the form
\{(h_{1},h_{2})\in G\times G\colon h_{1}+h_{2}=c\}
for some abelian group
G
and
c\in G
. In our setting,
for any
c\in\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, we may also define the level set at
c
as
S(c):=\{(h_{1},h_{2})\in H^{2}\colon\tilde{\xi}(h_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{2})\sim c\}
. We have the following basic properties:

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
a,a^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
c,c^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
.

(i) If
(a,a^{\prime}),(a,a^{\prime\prime})\in S(c)
, then
a^{\prime}=a^{\prime\prime}
.

(ii) If
(a,a^{\prime})\in S(c)\cap S(c^{\prime})
, then
\pi(c)=\pi(c^{\prime})
.

Proof.

For Part (i), if
(a,a^{\prime}),(a,a^{\prime\prime})\in S(c)
, then
a+a^{\prime}=a+a^{\prime\prime}=\pi(c)
, and so
a^{\prime}=a^{\prime\prime}
.
For Part (ii), we have
a+a^{\prime}=\pi(c)=\pi(c^{\prime})
.
∎

The first step is to show that there exist many
c\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that the level set
S(c)
has a large cardinality.

Proposition 5.12
.

Suppose that
d\geq\max\{s+3,13\}
and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
.
There exist
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
and for each
h\in H^{\prime}
some
c_{h}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
with
\pi(c_{h})=h
such that
S(c_{h})
is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
(in particular
S(c_{h}),h\in H^{\prime}
are disjoint).

Proof.

By Proposition
5.7
, there exist
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
and a set
C^{\prime}_{h}\subseteq H_{1}\times H_{1}
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
such that for all
h\in H^{\prime}
,

•

|C^{\prime}_{h}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
;

•

h_{1}+h_{2}=h
for all
(h_{1},h_{2})\in C^{\prime}_{h}
;

•

\tilde{\xi}(h_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{2})\sim\tilde{\xi}(h^{\prime}_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h^{\prime}_{2})
for all
(h_{1},h_{2}),(h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2})\in C^{\prime}_{h}
.

Let
C_{h}:=\{\tilde{\xi}(h_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{2})\colon(h_{1},h_{2})\in C^{\prime}_{h}\}
.
Then
C_{h}
is an equivalence class.
By Lemma
2.4
,
C_{h}\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
.
Since
|C^{\prime}_{h}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
,
for any
h\in H^{\prime}
and subspace
Y
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
s+2
and containing
\iota(h)
, since
d\geq s+3
,
it is impossible to have
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2})\}\cap Y\neq\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
for all
(h_{1},h_{2})\in C^{\prime}_{h}
.
By Proposition
5.4
, since
d\geq 11
, we have that
C_{h}
is a strong equivalence class. So there exist
c_{h}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
such that
\tilde{\xi}(h_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{2})\sim c_{h}
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
and
(h_{1},h_{2})\in C^{\prime}_{h}
. So we have that
|S(c_{h})|\geq|C^{\prime}_{h}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
, Since
\pi(c_{h})=h
, we are done.
∎

Let
H^{\prime}
and
c_{h},h\in H^{\prime}
satisfy the requirement of Proposition
5.12
and set
\mathcal{X}:=\{c_{h}\colon h\in H^{\prime}\}
.
The second step is to show that there exists a large subset
\mathcal{X^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{X}
such that the relation graph of
\mathcal{X^{\prime}}\hat{+}\mathcal{X^{\prime}}
has a small density dependence number.
Consider the graph
G_{1}=(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},E_{1})
, where
E_{1}=\cup_{c\in\mathcal{X}}S(c)
.
We say that an edge in
E_{1}
has
color

c
if it belongs to
S(c)
. By Proposition
5.12
,

(5.4)

|E_{1}|=\sum_{c\in\mathcal{X}}|S(c)|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{2d}.

For
W_{1},W_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, let
E(W_{1},W_{2})
denote the set of edges in
E_{1}
joining a vertex in
W_{1}
and a vertex in
W_{2}
. For
W\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
c\in\mathcal{X}
, let
W(c)
denote the set of all
x\in W
such that
(x,y)\in S(c)
for some
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.
Following the method similar to pages 265–266 of
[
1
]
, there exist
W_{1},W_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
such that

|\mathcal{X}^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,s}K^{d}
, and that for all
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
,
|E(W_{1}(c_{1}),W_{2}(c_{2}))|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{2d}
. Moreover, for all
c\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
,
E(W_{1}(c_{1}),W_{2}(c_{2}))
contains at least one edge of color
c
.

Consider the bipartite graph
G_{2}=(X_{2},Y_{2},E_{2})
, where
X_{2}=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
,
Y_{2}=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, and
\{(c_{1},c_{2}),(c,h_{1},h_{2})\}\in E_{2}
if
there exists
(a_{1},a_{2})\in E(W_{1}(c_{1}),W_{2}(c_{2}))
such that
(a_{1},h_{1})\in S(c_{1})
,
(a_{2},h_{2})\in S(c_{2})
and
(a_{1},a_{2})\in S(c)
. For
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, let
\Xi(c_{1},c_{2})
denote the set of all edges in
E_{2}
with
(c_{1},c_{2})
as one of the vertices.

Lemma 5.13
.

We have that
|\Xi(c_{1},c_{2})|=|E(W_{1}(c_{1}),W_{2}(c_{2}))|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{2d}
.

Proof.

Fix
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and let
(a_{1},a_{2})\in E(W_{1}(c_{1}),W_{2}(c_{2}))
. By definition and Lemma
5.11
, there exist unique
h_{1},h_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that
(a_{1},h_{1})\in S(c_{1})
and
(a_{2},h_{2})\in S(c_{2})
. By the definition of
E_{1}
, we have that
(a_{1},a_{2})\in S(c)
for some unique
c\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
. Therefore,
\lambda\colon(a_{1},a_{2})\to((c_{1},c_{2}),(c,h_{1},h_{2}))
is a well defined map from
E(W_{1}(c_{1}),W_{2}(c_{2}))
to
\Xi(c_{1},c_{2})
.

It suffices to show that
\lambda
is a bijection. Suppose that both
\lambda(a_{1},a_{2})
and
\lambda(a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2})
equal to
((c_{1},c_{2}),(c,h_{1},h_{2}))
. Then
(a_{1},h_{1}),(a^{\prime}_{1},h_{1})\in S(c)
. By Lemma
5.11
,
a_{1}=a^{\prime}_{1}
. Similarly,
a_{2}=a^{\prime}_{2}
. So
\lambda
is injective. On the other hand, by the definition of
E_{2}
,
\lambda
is subjective. This finishes the proof.
∎

For
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, denote

X_{2,h}:=\{c_{1}\hat{+}c_{2}\colon(c_{1},c_{2})\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\pi(c_{1})+\pi(c_{2})=h\}

and

Y_{2,h}:=\{(c,h_{1},h_{2})\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\times\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\colon\pi(c)+h_{1}+h_{2}=h\}.

It is easy to see that there is no edge in
E_{2}
connecting
X_{2,h}
and
Y_{2,h^{\prime}}
if
h\neq h^{\prime}
. Therefore,
G_{2}
has a disjoint partition
G_{2,h}=(X_{2,h},Y_{2,h},E_{2,h}),h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.

Let
G^{\prime}_{2,h}=(X_{2,h},E^{\prime}_{2,h})
be the graph given by
(c_{1}\hat{+}c_{2},c^{\prime}_{1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{2})\in E^{\prime}_{2,h}
if
c_{1}\hat{+}c_{2}\sim c^{\prime}_{1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{2}
.
The goal of the second step is equivalent of showing that
G^{\prime}_{2,h}
has density dependence number
O_{\delta,s}(1)
.
We use the intersection method.
We say that

((D_{1},h_{1,1},h_{1,2}),\dots,(D_{s+4},h_{s+4,1},h_{s+4,2}))\in Y^{s+4}_{2,h}

is
good
if
h_{i,1},h_{i,2},1\leq i\leq s+4
are
p
-linearly independent.
Let
Z_{2,h}
denote the set of all good elements in
Y^{s+4}_{2,h}
.
Consider the bipartite graph
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}=(X_{2,h},Z_{2,h},E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h})
such that
(t,(r_{1},\dots,r_{s+4}))\in E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
if
(t,r_{1}),\dots,(t,r_{s+4})\in E_{2,h}
, where
r_{i}\in Y_{2,h}
.

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
G^{\prime}_{2,h}
and
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
be defined as above. If
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
and
d\geq 2s+15
, then
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
is an
O_{\delta,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense auxiliary graph of
G^{\prime}_{2,h}
. Therefore,
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G^{\prime}_{2,h})=O_{\delta,s}(1)
.

Proof.

We first show that
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
is an auxiliary graph of
G^{\prime}_{2,h}
.
Let
t_{1}=c_{1}\hat{+}c_{2}
,
t_{2}=c^{\prime}_{1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{2}
and

w=(r_{1}=(D_{1},h_{1,1},h_{1,2}),\dots,r_{s+4}=(D_{s+4},h_{s+4,1},h_{s+4,2}))\in Z_{2,h}

be such that
(t_{1},w),(t_{2},w)\in E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
. Our goal is to show that
(t_{1},t_{2})\in E^{\prime}_{2,h}
. Assume that
c_{j}=(\pi(c_{j}),J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(c_{j}))}+f_{j})
and
c^{\prime}_{j}=(\pi(c_{j}),J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(c_{j}))}+f_{j^{\prime}})
for
j=1,2
.
For each
1\leq i\leq s+4
,
since
((c_{1},c_{2}),r_{i})\in E_{2,h}
, by definition, there exist
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that
(a_{1},h_{i,1})\in S(c_{1}),(a_{2},h_{i,2})\in S(c_{2})
and
(a_{1},a_{2})\in S(D_{i})
.
So

\begin{split}&\quad f_{1}+f_{2}\equiv(\xi(a_{1})+\xi(h_{i,1}))+(\xi(a_{2})+\xi(h_{i,2}))\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(c_{1})),\iota(\pi(c_{2})),\iota(a_{1}),\iota(a_{2}),\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2})}.\end{split}

Since
\pi(c_{1})=a_{1}+h_{i,1}
,
\pi(c_{2})=a_{2}+h_{i,2}
and
\pi(c_{1})+\pi(c_{2})=h
, we have that

(5.5)

\begin{split}f_{1}+f_{2}\equiv\xi(a_{1})+\xi(a_{2})+\xi(h_{i,1})+\xi(h_{i,2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(\pi(c_{1})),\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2})}.\end{split}

Since
((c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2}),r_{i})
belong to
E_{2,h}
, a symmetric argument shows that

(5.6)

\begin{split}f^{\prime}_{1}+f^{\prime}_{2}\equiv\xi(a^{\prime}_{1})+\xi(a^{\prime}_{2})+\xi(h_{i,1})+\xi(h_{i,2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(\pi(c^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2})}\end{split}

for some
(a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2})\in S(D_{i})
.
Since
(a_{1},a_{2}),(a^{\prime}_{1},a^{\prime}_{2})\in S(D_{i})
, we have that

(5.7)

\begin{split}\xi(a_{1})+\xi(a_{2})\equiv D_{i}\equiv\xi(a^{\prime}_{1})+\xi(a^{\prime}_{2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(a_{1}),\iota(a_{2}),\iota(a^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(a^{\prime}_{2})}.\end{split}

Combining (
5.5
), (
5.6
) and (
5.7
), we have that

\begin{split}f_{1}+f_{2}\equiv f^{\prime}_{1}+f^{\prime}_{2}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(\pi(c_{1})),\iota(\pi(c^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2})}\end{split}

for all
1\leq i\leq s+4
.
Therefore,

(5.8)

\begin{split}f_{1}+f_{2}\equiv f^{\prime}_{1}+f^{\prime}_{2}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{s+4}J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(\pi(c_{1})),\iota(\pi(c^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2})}.\end{split}

Since
w
is good, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=3
and
r=2
), if
p\gg_{d}1
and
d\geq 2s+15
, then (
5.8
) implies that

\begin{split}f_{1}+f_{2}\equiv f^{\prime}_{1}+f^{\prime}_{2}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(\pi(c_{1})),\iota(\pi(c^{\prime}_{1}))}.\end{split}

So
c_{1}\hat{+}c_{2}\sim c^{\prime}_{1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{2}
. In other words,
(t_{1},t_{2})\in E^{\prime}_{2,h}
.

We next show that
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
is
O_{\delta,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense. Let
t\in X_{2,h}
. By Lemma
5.13
, the number of tuples
w=(r_{1},\dots,r_{s+4})\in Y^{s+4}_{2,h}
such that
(t,r_{1}),\dots,(t,r_{s+4})\in E_{2,h}
is
\gg_{\delta,s}K^{(2s+8)d}
. Since
|Z_{2,h}|\leq K^{(2s+8)d}
and there are at most
(2s+8)K^{(2s+8)d}/p^{d-2s-7}
many
(r_{1},\dots,r_{s+4})\in Y^{s+4}_{2,h}
which are not good by Lemma
A.3
, we have that
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}
is of density
\gg_{\delta,s}1
provided that
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
and
d\geq 2s+8
. ∎

We are now ready to complete the proof of Theorem
5.9
.
Consider the graph
G_{3}=(H,E_{3})
, where
(h,h^{\prime})\in E_{3}
if and only if
(h,h^{\prime})\in S(c)
for some
c\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
. By Proposition
5.12
,
|E_{3}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{2d}
.
Again we use the intersection method.
Let
a_{h}
denote the number of edges in
G_{3}
with one of the vertices being
h
. The number of tuples
(h,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+4})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{s+5}
such that
(h,h_{1}),\dots,(h,h_{s+4})\in E_{3}
is at least

\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}a^{s+4}_{h}\geq\frac{(\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}a_{h})^{s+4}}{K^{(s+3)d}}=\frac{(2|E_{3}|)^{s+4}}{K^{(s+3)d}}\gg_{\delta,s}K^{(s+5)d}.

For each
w=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+4})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{s+4}
, let
W_{w}
denote the set
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that
(h,h_{1}),\dots,

(h,h_{s+4})\in E_{3}
. Since
|W_{w}|\leq K^{d}
for all
w
, the number of
w\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{s+4}
such that
|W_{w}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
is
\gg_{\delta,s}K^{(s+4)d}
.

We say that
w=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+4})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{s+4}
is
good
if
h_{1},\dots,h_{s+4}
are
p
-linearly independent.
Since the number of
w\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{s+4}
which is not good is at most
(s+4)K^{(s+4)d}/p^{d-s-3}

by Lemma
A.3
,
if
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
and
d\geq s+4
, then we may choose a good
w\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{s+4}
such that
|W_{w}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
.

Fix such a
w=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+4})
and let
H^{\prime}=W_{w}
. Then
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,s}K^{d}
. It remains to show that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}))=O_{\delta,s}(1)
. Let
G_{4}=(\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}),E_{4})
be the relation graph of
\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})
. Clear, we have a partition

G_{4}=\cup_{h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}(V_{4,h},E_{4,h})
, where
V_{4,h}:=\pi^{-1}(h)\cap(\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}))
.

For all
x_{1},x_{2}\in H^{\prime}
with
x_{1}+x_{2}=h
, by definition, for
1\leq i\leq s+4
,
(x_{1},h_{i})\in S(c_{i,1})
and
(x_{2},h_{i})\in S(c_{i,2})
for some
c_{i,1},c_{i,2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.
Then
\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\sim c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2}
. Since
\pi(c_{i,1})+\pi(c_{i,2})=x_{1}+x_{2}-2h_{i}=h-2h_{i}
, we have that
c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2}\in X_{2,h-2h_{i}}
.

Let
G^{\prime}_{4,h}=(V_{4,h},Z_{2,h-2h_{1}}\times\dots\times Z_{2,h-2h_{s+4}},E^{\prime}_{4,h})
be the bipartite graph such that
(u,(z_{1},\dots,

z_{s+4}))\in E^{\prime}_{4,h}
if and only if there exist
x_{1},x_{2}\in H^{\prime}
with
x_{1}+x_{2}=h
,
c_{i,1},c_{i,2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime},1\leq i\leq s+4
such that
u=\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2})
,
(x_{1},h_{i})\in S(c_{i,1})
,
(x_{2},h_{i})\in S(c_{i,2})
,
(c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2},z_{i})\in E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h-2h_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+4
. For convenience for say that
c_{i,1},c_{i,2},1\leq i\leq s+4
are the
parameters
associated to
(u,(z_{1},\dots,z_{s+4}))
. Note that for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in H^{\prime}
with
x_{1}+x_{2}=h
, by the construction of
H^{\prime}
,

(x_{1},h_{i})\in S(c_{i,1})
,
(x_{2},h_{i})\in S(c_{i,2})
for some
c_{i,1},c_{i,2}\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+4
.
On the other hand, the number of choices for
z_{i}
with
(c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2},z_{i})\in E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h-2h_{i}}
is
\gg_{\delta,s}|Z_{2,h-2h_{i}}|
since
G^{\prime\prime}_{2,h}=(X_{2,h},Z_{2,h},E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h})
is an
O_{\delta,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense auxiliary graph of
G^{\prime}_{2,h}
by Proposition
5.14
.
This means that
G^{\prime}_{4,h}
is an
O_{\delta,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense graph.

So in order to show that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}))=O_{\delta,s}(1)
, it suffices to show that
G^{\prime}_{4,h}
is an auxiliary graph of
(V_{4,h},E_{4,h})
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
. Let
x_{1},x_{2},x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2}\in H^{\prime}
with
x_{1}+x_{2}=x^{\prime}_{1}+x^{\prime}_{2}=h
and
\mathbb{z}:=(z_{1},\dots,z_{s+4}),z_{i}\in Z_{2,h-2h_{i}},1\leq i\leq s+4
be such that
(\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2}),\mathbb{z}),(\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2}),\mathbb{z})\in E^{\prime}_{4,h}
.
Let
c_{i,1},c_{i,2},1\leq i\leq s+4
be the parameters associated to
(\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2}),\mathbb{z})
, and
c^{\prime}_{i,1},c^{\prime}_{i,2},1\leq i\leq s+4
be the parameters associated to
(\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{2}),\mathbb{z})
.
For all
1\leq i\leq s+4
and
j=1,2
, since
\tilde{\xi}(x_{j})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\sim c_{i,j}
and
c_{i,j}\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, we have that

\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\sim c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2}

and

\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{2})+\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\sim c^{\prime}_{i,1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{i,2}.

Since
(c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2},z_{i}),(c^{\prime}_{i,1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{i,2},z_{i})
both belong to
E^{\prime\prime}_{2,h-2h_{i}}
, by Proposition
5.14
, we have that
c_{i,1}\hat{+}c_{i,2}\sim c^{\prime}_{i,1}\hat{+}c^{\prime}_{i,2}
and so

\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\sim\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{2})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(h_{i}).

So

\xi(x_{1})+\xi(x_{2})\equiv\xi(x^{\prime}_{1})+\xi(x^{\prime}_{2})\mod\cap_{i=1}^{s+4}J^{M}_{\iota(x_{1}),\iota(x_{2}),\iota(x^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(x^{\prime}_{2}),\iota(h_{i})}.

Since
w=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+4})
is good and
\dim(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(x_{1}),\iota(x_{2}),\iota(x^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(x^{\prime}_{2})\})\leq 3
(note that
x_{1}+x_{2}=x^{\prime}_{1}+x^{\prime}_{2}=\pi(v_{1})-2h_{1}
), by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=3
and
r=1
), if
d\geq 13
, then

\xi(x_{1})+\xi(x_{2})\equiv\xi(x^{\prime}_{1})+\xi(x^{\prime}_{2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x_{1}),\iota(x_{2}),\iota(x^{\prime}_{1}),\iota(x^{\prime}_{2})}.

So by Lemma
2.5
,

\tilde{\xi}(x_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x_{2})\sim\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(x^{\prime}_{2})

and thus
G^{\prime}_{4,h}
is an auxiliary graph of
V_{4,h}
. This completes the proof of Theorem
5.9
.

5.4.
Ruzsa’s triangle inequality

Ruzsa’s triangle inequality is an important tool in additive combinatorics, which says that for all subsets
A,B,C
of an abelian group
G
, we have that

|A-C|\leq\frac{|A-B|\cdot|B-C|}{|B|},

or equivalently,
d(A,C)\leq d(A,B)+d(B,C)
where the “distance function” is defined as
d(A,B):=\log\frac{|A-B|}{|A|^{1/2}|B|^{1/2}}
(see for example Lemma 2.6 of
[
10
]
). It is natural to ask if Ruzsa’s triangle inequality also applies to shifted modules. In our setting, the “distance function”
d(A,B)
is replaced by the density dependence number of the set
A\hat{-}B
.

Proposition 5.15

(Ruzsa’s quasi triangle inequality for
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)
.

Let
d,D,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
A,C\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
and
B\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
with
|B|\geq\delta K^{d}
such that
\pi\colon B\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is an injection. Suppose that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}B)
,
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(B\hat{-}C)\leq D
. If
d\geq\max\{4k+s,8k+5\}
, then we have
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}C)=O_{\delta,D,k,s}(1)
.

Note that Proposition
5.15
does not imply an inequality of the form
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}B)+{\#}_{\text{dd}}(B\hat{-}C)

\geq{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}C)
, but it provides us with a quasi triangle inequality in the sense that the smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}B)
and
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(B\hat{-}C)
implies the smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}C)
.

We remark that the set
B
in Proposition
5.15
is very restrictive compared with the sets
A
and
C
. It seems that our method can be adapted to prove Proposition
5.15
with less restrictions imposed on the set
B
. However, we do not pursuit this improvement in this paper since Proposition
5.15
is good enough for our purposes.

The original Ruzsa triangle inequality is a direct consequence of the injectivity of the map
(A-C)\times B\mapsto(A-B)\times(B-C),(a-c,b)\mapsto(a-b,b-c)
. However, this method does not apply to Proposition
5.15
and so we need to use a different method.

Proof of Proposition
5.15
.

For convenience denote
X_{h}:=\pi(h)^{-1}\cap X
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
X\subseteq\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
. Since
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}B)
,
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(B\hat{-}C)\leq D
, the relation graph of each
(A\hat{-}B)_{h}
has a
D^{-1}
-dense auxiliary graph
G_{A,h}=((A\hat{-}B)_{h},Y_{A,h},E_{A,h})
, and the relation graph of each
(B\hat{-}C)_{h}
has a
D^{-1}
-dense auxiliary graph
G_{C,h}=((B\hat{-}C)_{h},Y_{C,h},E_{C,h})
.
By Lemma
2.15
, we may assume without loss of generality that all of
Y_{A,h},Y_{C,h},h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
have the same cardinality, which we denote by
L
.

We need to use the intersection method.
We say that
(b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s})\in B^{4k+s}
is
good
if
\pi(b_{1}),\dots,\pi(b_{4k+s})

p
-are linearly independent. Consider the bipartite graph

G^{\prime}_{h}=((A\hat{-}C)_{h},(\sqcup_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}Y_{A,h^{\prime}}\times Y_{C,h-h^{\prime}})^{4k+s},E^{\prime}_{h})

defined in the following way:
for any
u\in(A\hat{-}C)_{h}
,
h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
,

y_{i}=(y_{i,1},y_{i,2})\in Y_{A,h_{i}}\times Y_{C,h-h_{i}},1\leq i\leq 4k+s
,
set
(u,(y_{1},\dots,y_{4k+s}))\in E^{\prime}_{h}
if there exist
a\in A,c\in C
, and some good
(b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s})\in B^{4k+s}
such that
u=a\hat{-}c
(which enforces
\pi(a)-\pi(c)=h
) and that
(a\hat{-}b_{i},y_{i,1})\in E_{A,h_{i}}
and
(b_{i}\hat{-}c,y_{i,2})\in E_{C,h-h_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq 4k+s
(which enforces
\pi(b_{i})=\pi(a)-h_{i}
).
For convenience we say that
a,c,b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s}
are the
parameters
associated to the edge
(u,(y_{1},\dots,y_{4k+s}))
(note that the parameters of an edge is not necessarily unique).

Note that if
d\geq 4k+s
and
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
, then by Lemma
A.3
, the number of good
(b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s})\in B^{4k+s}
is at least
|B|^{4k+s}/2\geq\delta^{4k+s}K^{(4k+s)d}/2
.
On the other hand, for each
u=a\hat{-}c
in
(A\hat{-}C)_{h}
and good
(b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s})\in B^{4k+s}
, writing
h_{i}=\pi(a)-\pi(b_{i})
, there exist at least
D^{-2}|Y_{A,h_{i}}|\cdot|Y_{C,h-h_{i}}|
many
(y_{i,1},y_{i,2})\in Y_{A,h_{i}}\times Y_{C,h-h_{i}}
such that
(a\hat{-}b_{i},y_{i,1})\in E_{A,h_{i}}
and
(b_{i}\hat{-}c,y_{i,2})\in E_{C,h-h_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq 4k+s
. Since
\pi\colon B\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is an injection, the tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{4k+s})
associated with different
(b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s})
are different. This means that every
u\in(A\hat{-}C)_{h}
is the vertices of at least
(\delta^{4k+s}K^{(4k+s)d}/2)\cdot(D^{-2}L^{2})^{4k+s}
edges in
E^{\prime}_{h}
. Since the set
(\sqcup_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}Y_{A,h^{\prime}}\times Y_{C,h-h^{\prime}})^{4k+s}
is of cardinality
(K^{d}L^{2})^{4k+s}
, we have that
G^{\prime}_{h}
is
O_{\delta,D,k,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense.
To show that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}C)=O_{\delta,D,k,s}(1)
, it suffices to show that
G^{\prime}_{h}
is an auxiliary graph of the relation graph of
(A\hat{-}C)_{h}
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.

Let
u,u^{\prime}\in(A\hat{-}C)_{h}
,
h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, and

y_{i}=(y_{i,1},y_{i,2})\in Y_{A,h_{i}}\times Y_{C,h-h_{i}},1\leq i\leq 4k+s
be such that
(u,(y_{1},\dots,y_{4k+s}))
,
(u^{\prime},(y_{1},\dots,y_{4k+s}))\in E^{\prime}_{h}
, with parameters
a,c,b_{1},\dots,b_{4k+s}
and
a^{\prime},c^{\prime},b^{\prime}_{1},\dots,b^{\prime}_{4k+s}
respectively.
For
y=a,c,a^{\prime}
and
c^{\prime}
, assume that
y=(\pi(y),J^{M}_{V_{y}}+f_{y})
.
Since
(a\hat{-}b_{i},y_{i,1}),(a^{\prime}\hat{-}b^{\prime}_{i},y_{i,1})\in E_{A,h_{i}}
and
(b_{i}\hat{-}c,y_{i,1}),(b^{\prime}_{i}\hat{-}c^{\prime},y^{\prime}_{i,1})\in E_{C,h-h_{i}}
, we have that

f_{a}-f_{c}\equiv(f_{a}-f_{b_{i}})+(f_{b_{i}}-f_{c})\equiv(f_{a^{\prime}}-f_{b^{\prime}_{i}})+(f_{b^{\prime}_{i}}-f_{c^{\prime}})\equiv f_{a^{\prime}}-f_{c^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}_{V+\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{i})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{i}))\}},

where
V:=V_{a}+V_{c}+V_{a^{\prime}}+V_{c^{\prime}}
.
Since
\pi(a)-\pi(b_{i})=\pi(a^{\prime})-\pi(b^{\prime}_{i})=h_{i}
, we have that
\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{i}))\in V_{a}+V_{a^{\prime}}\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{i}))\}
. So

f_{a}-f_{c}\equiv f_{a^{\prime}}-f_{c^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}_{V+\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{i}))\}}.

Ranging
i
over
1,\dots,4k+s
, we have that

(5.9)

f_{a}-f_{c}\equiv f_{a^{\prime}}-f_{c^{\prime}}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{4k+s}J^{M}_{V+\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{i}))\}}.

Since
h\in(V)
, we have that

\dim(V)\leq 4k-1
. Since
\pi(b_{1}),\dots,\pi(b_{4k+1})
are
p
-linearly independent, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=4k-1
and
r=1
), since
d\geq 8k+5
and
p\gg_{d}1
,
we deduce from (
5.9
) that

a\hat{-}c\sim a^{\prime}\hat{-}c^{\prime}
. So
G^{\prime}_{h}
is an auxiliary graph of the relation graph of
(A\hat{-}C)_{h}
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and we are done.
∎

5.5.
Plünnecke-Rusza theorem

Let
A
be a subset of an abelian group
G
. The Plünnecke-Rusza theorem says that for any fixed
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the smallness of
|A+A|/|A|
implies the smallness of
|kA-\ell A|/|A|

(see for example Corollary 6.29
[
10
]
).
In our setting,
for
A\in\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
and
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
with
(k,\ell)\neq(0,0)
, we use the notation
kA\hat{-}\ell A
to denote the set

(A\hat{+}A\hat{+}\dots\hat{+}A)\hat{-}(A\hat{+}A\hat{+}\dots\hat{+}A),

where there are
k
copies of
A
in the first bracket and
\ell
copies of
A
in the second bracket.
It is natural to ask whether the smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)
implies the smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(kA\hat{-}\ell A)
.

Conjecture 5.16

(Plünnecke-Rusza theorem for
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)
.

Let
d,D,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k,\ell,s\in\mathbb{N}
with
(k,\ell)\neq(0,0)
,
\delta>0
, and
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
A\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be such that
|A|\geq\delta K^{d}
and that the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is injective. Suppose that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)\leq D
. If
d\gg_{k,\ell,s}1
, then
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(kA\hat{-}\ell A)=O_{\delta,d,D}(1)
.

Thanks to Rusza’s quasi triangle inequality Proposition
5.15
, to prove Conjecture
5.16
, it suffices to show that the smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)
implies the smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}A\hat{+}A)
(which is known as the Green-Ruzsa inequality):

Conjecture 5.17

(Green–Ruzsa inequality for
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)
.

Let
d,D,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
Let
A\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be such that
|A|\geq\delta K^{d}
and that the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is injective. Suppose that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)\leq D
. If
d\gg_{s}1
, then
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{-}A\hat{+}A)=O_{\delta,d,D}(1)
.

Conjecture
5.16
can be proved by a simple induction argument combining the Rusza’s quasi triangle inequality (Proposition
5.15
) and Conjecture
5.17
(see the proof of Proposition
5.19
for details).

Unfortunately, we were unable to prove Conjecture
5.17
. However, we have the following weaker version of Conjecture
5.17
, which turns out to be good enough for the purposes of this paper.

Proposition 5.18

(Weak Green–Ruzsa inequality for
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)
.

Let
d,D,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
Let
A\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be such that
|A|\geq\delta K^{d}
and that the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is injective. Suppose that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)\leq D
. If
d\geq\max\{s+7,19\}
, then there exists
A^{\prime}\subseteq A
with
|A^{\prime}|\geq|A|/2
such that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A^{\prime}\hat{-}A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
.

By Propositions
5.15
and
5.18
, we have the following weak version of Plünnecke-Rusza theorem:

Proposition 5.19

(Weak Plünnecke-Rusza theorem for
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)
.

Let
d,D,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k,\ell,s\in\mathbb{N}
with
(k,\ell)\neq(0,0)
,
\delta>0
, and
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
A\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
be such that
|A|\geq\delta K^{d}
and that the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is injective. Suppose that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)\leq D
. If

d\geq\max\{s+7,19,4k+s,8k+5,4\ell+s,8\ell+5\},

then there exists
A^{\prime}\subseteq A
with
|A^{\prime}|\geq\delta K^{d}/2
such that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(kA^{\prime}\hat{-}\ell A)\leq O_{\delta,D,k,\ell,s}(1)
.

Proof of Proposition
5.19
assuming Proposition
5.18
.

By Proposition
5.18
, there exists
A^{\prime}\subseteq A
with
|A^{\prime}|\geq\delta p^{d}/2
such that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A^{\prime}\hat{-}A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
.
Since
3A^{\prime}=(A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime})\hat{-}(\hat{-}A^{\prime})
, and both
(A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime})\hat{-}A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}\hat{-}A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime}
and
A^{\prime}\hat{-}(\hat{-}A^{\prime})=A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime}
has density dependence number
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
,
by Proposition
5.15
,
3A^{\prime}
has density dependence number
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
.

It is obvious that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A^{\prime})=1
.
Suppose that we have shown
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(iA^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,i,s}(1)
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Since
(i+1)A^{\prime}=(i-1)A^{\prime}\hat{-}(\hat{-}2A^{\prime})
, and both
(i-1)A^{\prime}\hat{-}(\hat{-}A^{\prime})=iA^{\prime}
and
2A^{\prime}\hat{-}(\hat{-}A^{\prime})=3A^{\prime}
have density dependence number
O_{\delta,D,i,s}(1)
by induction hypothesis, by Proposition
5.15
, we have
{\#}_{\text{dd}}((i+1)A^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,i,s}(1)
. So we have that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(kA^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,k,s}(1)
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Finally, for all
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, since
kA^{\prime}\hat{-}(\hat{-}A^{\prime})=(k+1)A^{\prime}
and
(\hat{-}A^{\prime})\hat{-}\ell A^{\prime}=\hat{-}(\ell+1)A^{\prime}
have density dependence numbers
O_{\delta,d,D,k,s}(1)
and
O_{\delta,D,\ell,s}(1)
respectively, by Proposition
5.15
, we have
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(kA^{\prime}\hat{-}\ell A^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,k,\ell,s}(1)
.
∎

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
5.18
.

For every
B\subseteq A
,
we construct a subset
X_{B}\subseteq B
as follows. We start with
X_{B}
being the empty set. If there exists some
b\in B
such that the number of
a\in B
for which
a\hat{+}b\sim u
for some
u\in X_{B}\hat{+}B
is at most
|B|/2
,
then we add one such
b
in
X_{B}
and repeat this process. If such
b
does not exist, we then terminate the construction. The construction of the set
X_{B}
is inspired by the proof of Lemma 2.17 of
[
10
]
. However, as we shall see later, the set
X_{B}
in our setting is much more difficult to handle compared with Lemma 2.17 of
[
10
]
.

Suppose that we added
b\in B
into
X_{B}
at some step. Let
Q
denote the set of
a\in B
such that
a\hat{+}b\not\sim u
for any
u\in X_{B}\hat{+}B
(here
X_{B}
denotes the set before adding
b
into it).
Then by definition
|Q|\geq|B|/2
.
Since the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is injective, we have

(\pi^{-1}(\pi(a\hat{+}b))\cap((X_{B}\cup\{b\})\hat{+}B))\backslash(\pi^{-1}(\pi(a\hat{+}b))\cap(X_{B}\hat{+}B))=\{a\hat{+}b\}

for all

a\in Q
.
By Lemma
2.16
, we have

{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\pi^{-1}(\pi(a\hat{+}b))\cap((X_{B}\cup\{b\})\hat{+}B))\geq{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\pi^{-1}(\pi(a\hat{+}b))\cap(X_{B}\hat{+}B))+1

for all

a\in Q
.
Since the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
is injective and
|Q|\geq|B|
/2, we have that

\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\pi^{-1}(h)\cap((X_{B}\cup\{b\})\hat{+}B))-\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\pi^{-1}(h)\cap(X_{B}\hat{+}B))\geq|B|/2.

In other words, each time we add some
b\in B
into the set
X_{B}
, the quantity

\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\pi^{-1}(h)\cap(X_{B}\hat{+}B))

is increased by at least
|B|/2
. Since
\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\pi^{-1}(h)\cap(A\hat{+}A))\leq DK^{d}
, we have that

(5.10)

|X_{B}|\leq 2DK^{d}/|B|.

Finally by the construction of
X_{B}
, for all
b\in B
, the set

Q_{B}(b):=\{a\in B\colon a\hat{+}b\sim c\text{ for some }c\in X_{B}\hat{+}B\}

is of cardinality at least
|B|/2
.

For every
B\subseteq A
,
let
G_{B}=(B\times A^{2},X_{B}\times(A\hat{+}A)^{2},E_{B})
be the bipartite graph such that
((b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),(x,c,c^{\prime}))\in E_{B}
if

there exist
a,a^{\prime}\in A
such that
a\hat{+}b_{1}\sim a^{\prime}\hat{+}x
,
b_{2}\hat{+}a=c
and
b_{3}\hat{+}a^{\prime}=c^{\prime}
.

We say that
x\in X_{B}
is a
major
of some
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in B\times A^{2}
in the graph
G_{B}
if the number of edges in
E_{B}
of the form
((b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),(x,c,c^{\prime}))
is at least
|B|/2|X_{B}|
.

For
(x,c,c^{\prime})\in X_{B}\times(A\hat{+}A)^{2}
, denote
\tilde{\pi}(x,c,c^{\prime}):=\pi(c)
.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let
B\subseteq A
.

(1)

For every
\mathbb{b}\in B\times A^{2}
,
there exists a subset
R_{\mathbb{b}}
of
X_{B}\times(A\hat{+}A)^{2}
of cardinality at least
|B|/2
such that
(\mathbb{b},r)\in E_{B}
for all
r\in R_{\mathbb{b}}
. Moreover, we may require that
\tilde{\pi}(r)\neq\tilde{\pi}(r^{\prime})
for all distinct
r,r^{\prime}\in R_{\mathbb{b}}
.

(2)

For every
\mathbb{b}\in B\times A^{2}
, there exists
x\in X_{B}
which is a major of
\mathbb{b}
in the graph
G_{B}
.

(3)

If
((b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),(x,c,c^{\prime}))\in E_{B}
, then writing
z=(\pi(z),J^{M}_{V_{z}}+f_{z})
for
z=x,b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c,
and
c^{\prime}
, we have that
V_{z}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(z))\}
for
z=x,b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}
,
V_{c}=\text{span}_{p}\{\iota(\pi(c)),

\iota(\pi(b_{2}))\}
,
V_{c^{\prime}}=\text{span}_{p}\{\iota(\pi(c^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{3}))\}
and

f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\equiv f_{x}-f_{c}+f_{c^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(c)),\iota(\pi(x))}

(in particular
b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\sim x\hat{-}c\hat{+}c^{\prime}
).

Proof.

We start with Part (i). Fix
\mathbb{b}=(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in B\times A^{2}
and let
a\in Q_{B}(b_{1})
. By definition, there exists
c_{0}=a^{\prime}\hat{+}x\in B\hat{+}X_{B}
for some
a^{\prime}\in B
and
x\in X_{B}
such that
a\hat{+}b_{1}\sim c_{0}
. Set
c=b_{2}\hat{+}a
and
c^{\prime}=b_{3}\hat{+}a^{\prime}
. In this way, we may define a map
f_{\mathbb{b}}\colon Q_{B}(b_{1})\to X_{B}\times(A\hat{+}A)^{2}
given by
f_{\mathbb{b}}(a)=(x,b_{2}\hat{+}a,b_{3}\hat{+}a^{\prime})
.
Let
R_{\mathbb{b}}:=f_{\mathbb{b}}(Q_{B}(b_{1}))
.

Since
|Q_{B}(b_{1})|\geq|B|/2
, to show that
R_{\mathbb{b}}
is of cardinality at least
|B|/2
, it suffices to show that
f_{\mathbb{b}}
is injective. Suppose that
f_{\mathbb{b}}(a)=f_{\mathbb{b}}(\tilde{a})=(x,c,c^{\prime})
. Then
\pi(a)=\pi(c)-\pi(b_{2})=\pi(\tilde{a})
. Since the map
\pi\colon A\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is injective, we have that
a=\tilde{a}
. So
f_{\mathbb{b}}
is injective.

On the other hand, for each
(x,c,c^{\prime})\in R_{\mathbb{b}}
,
\pi(c)=\pi(a)+\pi(b_{2})
is uniquely determined by the choice of
a
. So
\pi(c)\neq\pi(\tilde{c})
for all distinct
(x,c,c^{\prime}),(\tilde{x},\tilde{c},\tilde{c}^{\prime})\in R_{\mathbb{b}}
.
This completes the proof of Part (i).
Part (ii) Follows from Part (i) and the Pigeonhole Principle.

We now prove Part (iii). Write also
z=(\pi(z),J^{M}_{V_{z}}+f_{z})
for
z=a,a^{\prime}
. For
z=a,a^{\prime},x,b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}
, since
z\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
, we have
V_{z}=\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(z))\}
.
Since
b_{2}\hat{+}a=c
and
b_{3}\hat{+}a^{\prime}=c^{\prime}
,
we have that

(\pi(c),J^{M}_{V_{c}}+f_{c})=(\pi(a)+\pi(b_{2}),J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a)),\iota(\pi(b_{2}))}+f_{a}+f_{b_{2}})

and

(\pi(c^{\prime}),J^{M}_{V_{c^{\prime}}}+f_{c^{\prime}})=(\pi(a^{\prime})+\pi(b_{3}),J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{3}))}+f_{a^{\prime}}+f_{b_{3}}).

In particular,
\pi(c)=\pi(a)+\pi(b_{2})
and
\pi(c^{\prime})=\pi(a^{\prime})+\pi(b_{3})
.
So
J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a)),\iota(\pi(b_{2}))}=J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(c)),\iota(\pi(b_{2}))}
and
J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{3}))}=J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(c^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{3}))}
.

Since
a\hat{+}b_{1}\sim a^{\prime}\hat{+}x
, we have that
\pi(b_{1})+\pi(a)=\pi(a^{\prime})+\pi(x)
.
So
\pi(b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3})=\pi(x\hat{-}c\hat{+}c^{\prime})

and

J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(x))\}+V_{c}+V_{c^{\prime}}}=J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x)),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(a)),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(a^{\prime}))}=J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(c)),\iota(\pi(x))}.

Finally,
a\hat{+}b_{1}\sim a^{\prime}\hat{+}x
implies that
f_{b_{1}}+f_{a}\equiv f_{a^{\prime}}+f_{x}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a)),\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(a^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{x}))}
. Therefore,

f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\equiv f_{x}-f_{c}+f_{c^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a)),\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(a^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{x}))}
.
We are done since
J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(a)),\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(a^{\prime})),\iota(\pi(b_{x}))}\subseteq J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(c)),\iota(\pi(x))}=J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(x))\}+V_{c}+V_{c^{\prime}}}
.
∎

Let
B\subseteq A
. Clearly, the graph
G_{B}
has a disjoint partition
G_{B,h}=(U_{B,h},V_{B,h},E_{B,h}),h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, where

U_{B,h}=\{(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in B\times A^{2}\colon\pi(b_{1})-\pi(b_{2})+\pi(b_{3})=h\}

and

V_{B,h}=\{(x,c,c^{\prime})\in X_{B}\times(A\hat{+}A)^{2}\colon\pi(x)-\pi(c)+\pi(c^{\prime})=h\}.

For each
x\in X_{B}
, let
U_{B,h,x}
denote the set of all
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{B,h}
having
x
as a major (by Lemma
5.20
Part (ii), we have that
U_{B,h}=\cup_{x\in X_{B}}U_{B,h,x}
), and let
V_{B,h,x}
be the set of elements in
V_{B,h}
whose first entry is
x
.
Let

\hat{U}_{B,h,x}:=\{b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\pi(x)})\colon(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{B,h,x}\}.

Proposition 5.21
.

Denote
\rho:=|B|/p^{d}
.
If
p\gg_{d,D,s,\rho}1
and
d\geq\max\{s+7,19\}
, then
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\hat{U}_{B,h,x})=O_{D,s,\rho}(1)
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.

Proof.

Fix
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
denote
(A\hat{+}A)_{h^{\prime}}:=\pi^{-1}(h^{\prime})\cap(A\hat{+}A)
for all
h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
.
Since
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A\hat{+}A)\leq D
, the relation graph of
(A\hat{+}A)_{h^{\prime}}
admits a
D^{-1}
-dense auxiliary graph
G^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}}=((A\hat{+}A)_{h^{\prime}},Y_{h^{\prime}},E^{\prime}_{h^{\prime}})
.
By Lemma
2.15
, we may assume without loss of generality that all of
Y_{h^{\prime}},h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
have the same cardinality, which we denote by
L
.

We need to use the intersection method.
Consider the bipartite graph

G_{B,h,x}=(\hat{U}_{B,h,x},(\sqcup_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}Y_{h^{\prime}}\times Y_{\pi(x)-h-h^{\prime}})^{s+7},E_{B,h,x})

defined in the following way. For all
u\in\hat{U}_{B,h,x}
,
h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
,
y_{i}=(y_{i,1},y_{i,2})\in Y_{h_{i}}\times Y_{\pi(x)-h-h_{i}},1\leq i\leq s+7
,

(u,(y_{1},\dots,y_{s+7}))\in E_{B,h,x}
if and only if there exist
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{B,h,x}
,
c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i}\in A\hat{+}A,1\leq i\leq s+7
such that the following holds:

•

u=b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x))})
;

•

\pi(c_{1}),\dots,\pi(c_{s+7})
are
p
-linearly independent;

•

((b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),(x,c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i}))\in E_{B,h}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+7
;

•

(c_{i},y_{i,1})\in Y_{h_{i}}
,
(c_{i},y_{i,2})\in Y_{\pi(x)-h-h_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+7
(this enforces
c_{i}\in(A\hat{+}A)_{h_{i}}
and
c^{\prime}_{i}\in(A\hat{+}A)_{\pi(x)-h-h_{i}}
).

For convenience we say that
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i},1\leq i\leq s+7
are the
parameters
associated to the edge
(u,(y_{1},\dots,y_{s+7}))
(note that the parameters of an edge is not necessarily unique).

Fix
u=b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\pi(x)})
for some
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{B,h,x}
.
Let
W
be the set of

\mathbb{w}=((x,c_{1},c^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x,c_{s+7},c^{\prime}_{s+7}))\in V_{B,h,x}^{s+7}
such that
((b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),(x,c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i}))\in E_{B,h}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+7
.
By Lemma
5.20
(ii),

|W|\geq(|B|/2|X_{B}|)^{s+7}\gg_{D,s,\rho}K^{(s+7)d}
. By Lemma
5.20
(i), the tuple
(\pi(c_{1}),\dots,\pi(c_{s+7}))
is different for all
\mathbb{w}\in W
. By Lemma
A.3
, if
p\gg_{d,D,s,\rho}1
and
d\geq s+7
, then the set
W^{\prime}
of
\mathbb{w}\in W
such that
\pi(c_{1}),\dots,\pi(c_{s+7})
are
p
-linearly independent is of cardinality
\gg_{D,s,\rho}K^{(s+7)d}
.
On the other hand, for every
\mathbb{w}=((x,c_{1},c^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,(x,c_{s+7},c^{\prime}_{s+7}))\in W^{\prime}
,
the number of
(y_{i,1},y_{i,2})\in Y_{\pi(c_{i})}\times Y_{\pi(x)-h-\pi(c_{i})}
with
(c_{i},y_{i,1})\in Y_{h_{i}}
,
(c_{i},y_{i,2})\in Y_{\pi(x)-h-h_{i}}
is at least
D^{-2}L^{2}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+7
. So there are
\gg_{D,s,\rho}p^{(s+7)d}\cdot(D^{-2}K^{2})^{s+7}
many edges in
G_{B,h,x}
with
u
as a vertex. Since
|(\sqcup_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}Y_{h^{\prime}}\times Y_{\pi(x)-h-h^{\prime}})^{s+7}|=(L^{2}p^{d})^{s+7}
,
we have that
G_{B,h,x}
is
O_{D,s,\rho}(1)^{-1}
-dense.
To show that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\hat{U}_{B,h,x})=O_{D,s,\rho}(1)
, it suffices to show that
G_{B,h,x}
is an auxiliary graph of the relation graph of
\hat{U}_{B,h,x}
.

Let
u,u^{\prime}\in\hat{U}_{B,h,x}
,
h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
y_{i}=(y_{i,1},y_{i,2})\in Y_{h_{i}}\times Y_{\pi(x)-h-h_{i}},1\leq i\leq s+7
be such that
(u,(y_{1},\dots,y_{s+7}))
and
(u^{\prime},(y_{1},\dots,y_{s+7}))
are edges in
E_{B,h,x}
with parameters
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i},1\leq i\leq s+7
and
b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},b^{\prime}_{3},\tilde{c}_{i},\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{i},1\leq i\leq s+7
respectively. Clearly,
\pi(c_{i})=\pi(\tilde{c}_{i})=h_{i}
.
For convenience write
z=(\pi(z),J^{M}_{V_{z}}+f_{z})
for
z=x,b_{j},b^{\prime}_{j},c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i},\tilde{c}_{i}
and
\tilde{c}_{i^{\prime}}
.
Since
((b_{1},b_{2},b_{3}),(x,c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i}))\in E_{B,h}
, by Lemma
5.20
(iii),

f_{c^{\prime}_{i}}-f_{c_{i}}+f_{x}\equiv f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(x)),\iota(h_{i})}.

Similarly,

f_{\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{i}}-f_{\tilde{c}_{i}}+f_{x}\equiv f_{b^{\prime}_{1}}-f_{b^{\prime}_{2}}+f_{b^{\prime}_{3}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{3})),\iota(\pi(x)),\iota(h_{i})}.

Since
(c_{i},y_{i,1}),(\tilde{c}_{i},y_{i,1})\in Y_{h_{i}}
,
(c^{\prime}_{i},y_{i,2}),(\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{i},y_{i,2})\in Y_{\pi(x)-h-h_{i}}
, we have that

c^{\prime}_{i}\hat{-}c_{i}\sim\tilde{c}^{\prime}_{i}\hat{-}\tilde{c}_{i}.

Combining the above equations and the description of
V_{z}
in Lemma
5.20
(iii) for
z=c_{i},c^{\prime}_{i},\tilde{c}_{i}
and
\tilde{c}_{i^{\prime}}
, we have that

f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\equiv f_{b^{\prime}_{1}}-f_{b^{\prime}_{2}}+f_{b^{\prime}_{3}}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{s+7}J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{3})),\iota(\pi(x)),\iota(h_{i})}.

Since
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{i})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{i})),\iota(\pi(x))\colon 1\leq i\leq 3\}
is of dimension at most 6 (note that
\pi(b_{1})-\pi(b_{2})+\pi(b_{3})=h=\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})-\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})+\pi(b^{\prime}_{3})
),
h_{i}=\pi(c_{i}),1\leq i\leq s+7
are
p
-linearly independent, and
d\geq 19
, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=6
and
r=1
), we have that

f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\equiv f_{b^{\prime}_{1}}-f_{b^{\prime}_{2}}+f_{b^{\prime}_{3}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{3})),\iota(\pi(x))},

or equivalently,

b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x))})\sim b^{\prime}_{1}\hat{-}b^{\prime}_{2}\hat{+}b^{\prime}_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x))}).

This means that
G_{B,h,x}
is an auxiliary graph of the relation graph of
\hat{U}_{B,h,x}
and we are done.
∎

We are now ready to complete the proof of Proposition
5.18
.
It follows from Proposition
5.21
that for each
x\in X_{A}
, the set
\hat{U}_{A,h,x}
has a small density dependence number. However, this is insufficient to conclude that
A\hat{-}A\hat{+}A
has a small density dependence number because of the loss of information caused by modulo
J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x))}
in the set
\hat{U}_{A,h,x}
. To overcome this difficulty, we show that
\hat{U}_{B,h,x_{i}}
has a small density dependence number for some large subset
B\subseteq A
and for many
x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
in general positions, and then we use the intersection method to recover the loss of information.
Unfortunately, in the constructions we have to pass to a subset
B
of
A
(although the density of
B
can be arbitrarily close to
A
). This is the reason why we have to pass to a subset of
A
in the statement of Proposition
5.18
.

We now construct the series of special vectors
x_{i_{1},\dots,i_{r}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},1\leq r\leq s+6,i_{j}\in A
we need as follows.
For
x\in A
, we say that
x
is
admissible
if
x\in X_{A}
and
\iota(\pi(x))\neq\mathbb{0}
. For
2\leq r\leq s+6
, we say that a sequence
(x_{1},\dots,x_{r})\in A^{r}
is
admissible
if
(x_{1},\dots,x_{r-1})
is admissible and
x_{r}\in X_{A\backslash\pi^{-1}(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(x_{1})),\dots,\iota(\pi(x_{r-1}))\})}
. We call
r
the
length
of the tuple.
Since
d\geq s+6
and

|X_{A\backslash\pi^{-1}(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(x_{1})),\dots,\iota(\pi(x_{r-1}))\})}|\leq 2DK^{d}/(|A|-K^{s+5})\leq 2DK^{d}/(\delta K^{d}-K^{s+5})

for all
1\leq r\leq s+6
by (
5.10
), we have that there are in total
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
many admissible tuples of length
s+6
. Since
X_{B}
is non-empty for all non-empty
B
, there is at least one admissible tuple of length
s+6
.
Let

A^{\prime}:=A\backslash\pi^{-1}(\cup_{(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})\text{ is admissible}}\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(x_{1})),\dots,\iota(\pi(x_{s+6}))\}).

By (
5.10
), there are
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
many admissible tuples of length
s+6
. So we have that
|A^{\prime}|\geq|A|/2
if
p\gg_{\delta,d,D}1
. It then suffices to show that
A^{\prime}\hat{-}A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime}
has density dependence number
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
.

Fix
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and fix any
u\in U_{A^{\prime},h}
.
Since
u\in U_{A^{\prime},h}\subseteq U_{A,h}
, by Lemma
5.20
(ii), there exists
x_{1}\in X_{A^{\prime}}
which is a major of
u
. In other words,
u\in U_{A^{\prime},h,x_{1}}
. Suppose that we have found an admissible tuple
(x_{1},\dots,x_{r})\in A^{r}
for some
1\leq r\leq s+5
such that
u\in U_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
.
Denote
B:=A\backslash\pi^{-1}(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(x_{1})),\dots,\iota(\pi(x_{r}))\})
. Since
A^{\prime}\subseteq B
, we have

u\in U_{B,h}
. By Lemma
5.20
(ii), there exists
x_{r+1}\in X_{B}
which is a major of
u
. In other words,
u\in U_{A^{\prime},h,x_{r+1}}
. Since
x_{r+1}\in X_{B}
, we have that
(x_{1},\dots,x_{r+1})
is admissible. By induction, there exists an admissible tuple
(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})\in A^{s+6}
such that
u\in U_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
.

Let
J
be the set of all admissible tuples of length
s+6
. Recall that
|J|=O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
.
For any
\mathbb{x}:=(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})\in J
, since
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\hat{U}_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}})=O_{\delta,D,s}(1)

by Proposition
5.21
, the relation graph of
\hat{U}_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}}
has an
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense auxiliary graph
G^{\prime}_{h,x_{i}}=(\hat{U}_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}},Y_{h,x_{i}},E^{\prime}_{h,x_{i}})
for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
.
By Lemma
2.15
, we may assume without loss of generality that all of
Y_{h,x_{i}}
have the same cardinality, which we denote by
K
.

Let

\hat{U}_{h}=\{b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\colon(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{A^{\prime},h}\}
and

Y_{h}:=\sqcup_{(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})\in J}Y_{h,x_{1}}\times\dots\times Y_{h,x_{s+6}}.

Consider the bipartite graph
\tilde{G}_{h}=(\hat{U}_{h},Y_{h},\tilde{E}_{h})
given in the following way. For all
u\in\hat{U}_{h}
and
y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{s+6})\in Y_{h}
,
(u,y)\in\tilde{E}_{h}
if and only if there exist
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{A^{\prime},h}
and
\mathbb{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})\in J
such that

•

u=b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}
;

•

y\in Y_{h,x_{1}}\times\dots\times Y_{h,x_{s+6}}
;

•

(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}}
(which implies that
u\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))})\in\hat{U}_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}}
) for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
;

•

(u\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))}),y_{i})\in E^{\prime}_{h,x_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
.

For convenience we say that
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},\mathbb{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})
are the
parameters
associated to the edge
(u,y)
(note that the parameters of an edge is not necessarily unique).

For any
u\in\hat{U}_{h}
, by definition we may write
u=b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}
for some
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in U_{A^{\prime},h}
. By the previous discussions, there exists at least one of
\mathbb{x}\in J
with
(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})\in\cap_{i=1}^{s+6}U_{A^{\prime},h,x_{i}}
. For this
\mathbb{x}
, the number of
y\in Y_{h,x_{1}}\times\dots\times Y_{h,x_{s+6}}
such that
(u\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))}),y_{i})\in E^{\prime}_{h,x_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
is
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)^{-1}L^{s+6}
. So there are at least
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)^{-1}L^{s+6}
edges in
\tilde{E}_{h}
having
u
as a vertex. Since
|Y_{h}|=|J|K^{s+6}=O_{\delta,D,s}(L^{s+6})
, we have that

\tilde{G}_{h}
is
O_{\delta,D,s}(1)^{-1}
-dense.
To show that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(A^{\prime}\hat{-}A^{\prime}\hat{+}A^{\prime})=O_{\delta,D,s}(1)
, it suffices to show that for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
,
\tilde{G}_{h}
is an auxiliary graph of the relation graph of
\hat{U}_{h}
.

Let
u,u^{\prime}\in\hat{U}_{h}
and
y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{s+6})\in Y_{h}
be such that
(u,y),(u^{\prime},y)
are edges of
\tilde{E}_{h}
with parameters
b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},\mathbb{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{s+6})
and
b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},b^{\prime}_{3},\mathbb{x}^{\prime}=(x^{\prime}_{1},\dots,x^{\prime}_{s+6})
respectively. Then we must have
\mathbb{x}=\mathbb{x}^{\prime}
. Since
(u\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))}),y_{i}),(u^{\prime}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))}),y_{i})\in E^{\prime}_{h,x_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
,
we have that

u\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))})=b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))})\sim b^{\prime}_{1}\hat{-}b^{\prime}_{2}\hat{+}b^{\prime}_{3}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))})=u^{\prime}\hat{+}(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(x_{i}))}).

Writing
z=(\pi(z),J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(z))}+f_{z})
for
z=b_{j},b^{\prime}_{j},1\leq j\leq 3
, we have that

f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\equiv f_{b^{\prime}_{1}}-f_{b^{\prime}_{2}}+f_{b^{\prime}_{3}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{3})),\iota(\pi(x_{i}))}

for all
1\leq i\leq s+6
. Since
\mathbb{x}
is admissible,
\iota(\pi(x_{r}))\notin\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(x_{1})),\dots,\iota(\pi(x_{r-1}))\}
for all
1\leq r\leq s+6
. So
\pi(x_{1}),\dots,\pi(x_{s+6})
are
p
-linearly independent. Since
d\geq 17
and

\dim(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(\pi(b_{i})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{i}))\colon 1\leq i\leq 3\})\leq 5

(note that
\pi(b_{1})-\pi(b_{2})+\pi(b_{3})=\pi(u)=h=\pi(u^{\prime})=\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})-\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})+\pi(b^{\prime}_{3})
),
by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=5
and
r=1
), we have that

f_{b_{1}}-f_{b_{2}}+f_{b_{3}}\equiv f_{b^{\prime}_{1}}-f_{b^{\prime}_{2}}+f_{b^{\prime}_{3}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(\pi(b_{1})),\iota(\pi(b_{2})),\iota(\pi(b_{3})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{1})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{2})),\iota(\pi(b^{\prime}_{3}))},

or equivalently,

u=b_{1}\hat{-}b_{2}\hat{+}b_{3}\sim b^{\prime}_{1}\hat{-}b^{\prime}_{2}\hat{+}b^{\prime}_{3}=u^{\prime}
. This means that
\tilde{G}_{h}
is an auxiliary graph of the relation graph of
\hat{U}_{h}
and completes the proof of Proposition
5.18
.

6.
Decomposition results for relation graphs

As is mentioned in Remark
5.10
, it is more natural to relate the cardinality of additive sets to the clique covering numbers of the relation graphs rather than the density dependence numbers.
The purpose of this section is to study the connections between these two numbers.
The proof of Lemma
2.14
(i) implies that for any graph
G
, we have
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(G)\geq{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)
. On the other hand,
for a general graph
G
,
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(G)
can be much larger than
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)
.

Example 6.1
.

Let
G=(X,E)
be a graph. The
Mycielskian
of
G
is the graph
\mu(G)
whose vertices are
X\sqcup X^{\prime}\sqcup\{a\}
(where
X^{\prime}=\{x^{\prime}\colon x\in X\}
is an identity copy of
X
) and the set of edges of
\mu(G)
is
E\cup\{\{x,y^{\prime}\}\colon\{x,y\}\in E\}\cup\{\{y^{\prime},a\}\colon y^{\prime}\in V^{\prime}\}
. Let
M_{2}
be the graph with two points connected by an edge and
M_{i}:=\mu(M_{i-1})
for all
i\geq 3
.
It is known that
M_{i}=(X_{i},E_{i})
is triangle free, which implies that
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(M_{i})\geq\lceil\frac{|M_{i}|}{2}\rceil=3\cdot 2^{i-3}
if
i\geq 3
. On the other hand, it is known that the chromatic number of
M_{i}
is
i
(i.e. there exists a coloring of the vertices of
M_{i}
in
i
colors such that no two adjacent vertices have the same color), which implies that
M_{i}
admits an auxiliary graph of density
1/i
, meaning that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(M_{i})\leq i
. So we see that
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(M_{i})
is much larger than
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(M_{i})
if
i
is large.

Based on the intuition that the structure described in Example
6.1
should rarely occur for relation graphs for subsets of
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
,
it is natural to make the following conjecture:

Conjecture 6.2

(Smallness of
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)
implies smallness of
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(G)
)
.

Let
d,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime dividing
K
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
G
be the relation graph of a subset of
\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
. If
d\gg_{k,s}1
and
p\gg_{d}1
, then
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(G)=O_{d,{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)}(1)
.

If Conjecture
6.2
holds, then all the results in Section
5
still apply if we replace the density dependence numbers by the clique covering numbers (under a slight modification on relevant constants).
Unfortunately, we are unable to prove Conjecture
6.2
. Instead, we have the following weaker version of it.

Proposition 6.3

(Weak decomposition result for relation graphs)
.

Let
d,D,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
d\geq 2(k-2)s+6k-1
if
k\geq 2
,
p\gg_{d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. There exist
C:=C(d,D),N:=N(d,D)\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every subset
X
of
\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
with
\pi(X)=\{\mathbb{0}\}
and
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(X)\leq D
,
there exists
Y\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
which is the union of at most
N
subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(s+2k-2)(k-1)
such that
we can partition
X
into
X_{b}\cup X_{g}
such that

•

for all
(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X_{b}
, we have that
V\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
;

•

{\#}_{\text{cc}}(X_{g})\leq C
.
14
14

14

Note that Proposition
6.3
holds trivially if
d\leq(s+2k-1)(k-1)
.

Roughly speaking, Proposition
6.3
says that
for every relation graph of a subset
X
of
\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
with
\pi(X)=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, we may partition
X
into a “good” part
X_{g}
and a “bad” part
X_{b}
such that
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(X_{g})
is controlled by
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(X)
, and that the elements of
X_{b}
has nontrivial intersection with a low dimensional object
Y
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
In practice, we will use Proposition
6.3
for
X
of the form
kA\hat{-}\ell A
for some
A\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
.
Although
|X_{b}|
is much smaller than
|X|
, unfortunately it turns out that we can not get rid of the error set
X_{b}
by passing to a large subset of
A
.

Remark 6.4
.

Proposition
6.3
is the major reason why we need to impose a rather large lower bound for the dimension
d
in Theorem
1.5
. As we shall see in later sections, the dimension of the error set
X_{b}
continues to accumulate in the proof of Theorem
1.5
, which eventually ends up with a very large dimension. If one can prove Conjecture
6.2
(i.e. prove Proposition
6.3
with
X_{b}=\emptyset
), then the lower bound for
d
in Theorem
1.5
can be improved significantly.

Proof of Theorem
6.3
.

By Lemma
2.5
,
\sim
is an equivalent relation in
\Gamma_{1}^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
.
So if
k=1
, then the relation graph of
X
is a finite union of
t
complete graphs with disjoint vertices for some
t\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover, it is not hard to see that
t={\#}_{\text{cc}}(X)={\#}_{\text{dd}}(X)\leq D
.
So we are done by setting
C=D
and
N=0
. From now on we assume that
k\geq 2
.

If
D=1
, then
X
is an equivalence class by Lemma
2.14
(iv).
So we can simply take
C=1
,
N=0
and
X_{1}=X
.
Suppose that the conclusion holds for
D-1
for some
D\geq 2
, we show that the conclusion also holds for
D
.

For convenience denote
L:=s+2k-1
.
Pick an arbitrary
x_{1}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V_{1}}+f_{1})\in X
. Suppose that we have chosen
x_{i}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V_{i}}+f_{i})\in X
for
1\leq i\leq r
for some
1\leq r\leq L
such that
V_{1},\dots,V_{r}
are linearly independent, and that
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{r}\}
is an equivalence class. Let
B_{1}
be the set of all
x\in X
such that
x\sim x_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
, and set
B_{2}=X\backslash B_{1}
. If for all
x=J^{M}_{V}+f\in B_{1}
, we have that
(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})\cap V\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
. Then we stop the construction.
If
r=L
, then we also stop the construction.
Otherwise, if
r<L
and there exists
x=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in B_{1}
such that
(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})\cap V=\{\mathbb{0}\}
then we pick
x_{r+1}=x
. Since
x_{r+1}\in B_{1}
,
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{r+1}\}
is an equivalence class. Since
(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})\cap V=\{\mathbb{0}\}
,
V_{1},\dots,V_{r+1}
are linearly independent. Thus we can repeat the construction for
x_{1},\dots,x_{r+1}
.

After at most
L
inductions, we arrive at a sequence of elements
x_{i}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V_{i}}+f_{i})\in X
for
1\leq i\leq r
for some
1\leq r\leq L
such that writing
B_{1}
to be the set of all
x\in X
such that
x\sim x_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
, and
B_{2}=X\backslash B_{1}
, we have that either
r=L
or
(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})\cap V\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
for all
x=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in B_{1}
.

We may further partition the set
B_{2}
as the
\cup_{i=1}^{r}B_{2,i}
, where
x_{i}\not\sim x
for all
x\in B_{2,i}
. Since
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(B_{2,i}\cup\{x_{i}\})\leq{\#}_{\text{dd}}(X)\leq D
, by Lemma
2.16
,
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(B_{2,i})\leq D-1
.
Since
r\leq L
,
Applying the induction hypothesis to each of
B_{2,i}
, we have that
there exist
C^{\prime}:=C^{\prime}(d,D,L),N^{\prime}:=N^{\prime}(d,D,L)\in\mathbb{N}
, a set
Y^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
which is a union of at most
N^{\prime}
subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
(s+2k-2)(k-1)
such that
we can partition
B_{2}=\cup_{i=1}^{r}B_{2,i}
into
X^{\prime}_{g}\cup X^{\prime}_{b}
such that

•

for all
(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X^{\prime}_{b}
, we have that
V\cap Y^{\prime}\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
;

•

{\#}_{\text{cc}}(X^{\prime}_{g})\leq C^{\prime}
.

If
r<L
, then
(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})\cap V\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
for all
x=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in B_{1}
.
Since
\pi(X)=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, by the definition of
\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
,
V_{i}
is of dimension at most
k-1
and so

\dim(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})\leq r(k-1)\leq(s+2k-2)(k-1)
, the conclusion holds by setting
C=C^{\prime},N=N^{\prime}+1
,
Y=Y^{\prime}\cup(V_{1}+\dots+V_{r})
,
X_{g}=X^{\prime}_{g}
and
X_{b}=X^{\prime}_{b}\cup B_{1}
.

We now consider the case
r=L
. If
B_{1}
is an equivalence class, then the conclusion holds by setting
C=C^{\prime}+1,N=N^{\prime}
,
Y=Y^{\prime}
,
X_{b}=X^{\prime}_{b}
and
X_{g}=X^{\prime}_{g}\cup B_{1}
. So it suffices to show that
B_{1}
is an equivalence class. For all
x=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f),x^{\prime}=(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V^{\prime}}+f^{\prime})\in B_{1}
, by definition,
x\sim x_{i}\sim x^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq L
. So

f\equiv f_{i}\equiv f^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V+V^{\prime}+V_{i}}
. Thus

f\equiv f^{\prime}\mod\cap_{i=1}^{L}J^{M}_{V+V^{\prime}+V_{i}}.

Since
d\geq 2(k-2)s+6k-1
,
V_{1},\dots,V_{L}
are linearly independent and

\dim(V+V^{\prime})\leq 2(k-1)
,
by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=2(k-1)
and
r=k-1
),
we have that

f\equiv f^{\prime}\mod J^{M}_{V+V^{\prime}}
and so
x\sim x^{\prime}
.
So
B_{1}
is an equivalence class and we are done.
∎

We need a variation of Proposition
6.3
for later sections. Instead of requiring
X_{g}
to be covered by at most
C
complete subgraphs (or equivalence classes), sometimes we need to further require that each complete subgraph is also a strong equivalence class. For this purpose, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 6.5

(
(L,C,D)
-structure-obstacle pair and classification)
.

Let
C,D,K,L,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
p
be a prime dividing
K
. For

\mathcal{C}_{0}\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
and
Y\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, we say that
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
is a
(L,C,D)
-structure-obstacle pair
(or simply a
structure-obstacle pair
) if
\mathcal{C}_{0}
is of cardinality at most
L+1
and contains
0
with
f\not\equiv g\mod J^{M}
for all distinct
f,g\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, and
Y
is the union of at most
C
subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimensions at most
D
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
X
be a subset of
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
with
\pi(X)=\{\mathbb{0}\}
. We say that a
(L,C,D)
-structure-obstacle pair
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
is a
(L,C,D)
-classification
(or simply a
classification
) of
X
if for all
(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{V}+f)\in X
, either
V\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
or
f\equiv g\mod J^{M}_{V}
for some
g\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

Informally, if
X
admits a classification
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
, then the set
\mathcal{C}_{0}
describes the good part of
X
, which has the structure of unions of strong equivalent classes, while the set
Y
describes the bad part of
X
, which is the obstacle that prevent us from concluding
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(X)=O_{{\#}_{\text{dd}}(X)}(1)
in Proposition
6.3
.

Combining Propositions
5.4
(i) and
6.3
,
we have the following variation of Proposition
6.3
:

Proposition 6.6

(Strong decomposition result for relation graphs)
.

Let
d,D,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
d\geq 2(k-2)s+6k-1
if
k\geq 2
,
p\gg_{d,D}1
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Then every
X\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
with
\pi(X)=\{\mathbb{0}\}
and
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(X)\leq D
admits an
(O_{d,D}(1),O_{d,D}(1),(s+2k-2)(k-1))
-classification.

Remark 6.7
.

Using a similar method, one can generalize Propositions
6.3
and
6.6
for the case when
\pi(X)=\{h\}
for some
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\backslash\{\mathbb{0}\}
(with a slight different lower bound for the dimension
d
the dimension of the subspace
W
defined in the above mentioned propositions). However, we do not need them in this paper.

7.
Properties for Freiman
M
-homomorphisms of order 16

In this section, we provide details for Step 3 described in Section
2.3
.
Let
H
be a large subset of an abelian group
G
. It is known that the
2H-2H
has a rich structure in combinatorics.
To see this, let
S
be a subset of the Pontryagin dual
\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}
of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
\rho>0
. Denote

B(S,\rho):=\{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\colon\|\xi\cdot h\|_{\mathbb{T}}<\rho\text{ for all }\xi\in S\},

where
\|a\|_{\mathbb{T}}
denotes the distance between
a
and the closest integer.
For
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, denote

R(H,h):=\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in H\colon h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}-h_{4}=h\}.

We have the following structure theorem for
2H-2H
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},0<c\leq 1
,
\delta>0
,
p
be a prime dividing
K
, and
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
be such that
|H|\geq\delta K^{d}
. Then there exist
a set
S\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}
with
|S|\leq 2\delta^{-2}
and
a proper and homogeneous generalized arithmetic progression

P=\{x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D}\colon x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-L_{i},L_{i}),1\leq i\leq D\}

in
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
of rank
D
for some
D\leq|S|+d
and cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that the followings hold:

(1)

B(S,cD^{-2D}/4)\subseteq P(c)\subseteq P\subseteq B(S,1/4)\subseteq 2H-2H
;

(2)

|R(H,h)|\geq\delta^{4}K^{3d}/2
for all
h\in P
;

(3)

there exists
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{c,\delta,d}K^{d}
such that
2H^{\prime}-2H^{\prime}\subseteq P(c)
;

(4)

the vectors
(\{\alpha\cdot v_{i}\})_{\alpha\in S}\in(\frac{1}{K}\mathbb{Z})^{|S|}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{|S|},1\leq i\leq D
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
, where
\{x\}
is the embedding of
x
from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathbb{T}
in the fundamental domain
[0,1)
.

Proof.

For
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, denote
f\ast g(h):=\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}f(h^{\prime})g(h-h^{\prime})
for all functions
f,g\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
. It was proved in Lemma 6.3 of
[
3
]
(although not stated explicitly) that there exists
S\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}
with
|S|\leq D_{0}:=2\delta^{-2}
such that for all
h\in B(S,1/4)
,
we have that

\mathbb{1}_{H}\ast\mathbb{1}_{H}\ast\mathbb{1}_{-H}\ast\mathbb{1}_{-H}(h)\geq\delta^{4}/2,

This means that
|R(H,h)|\geq\delta^{4}K^{3d}/2
. In particular,
B(S,1/4)\subseteq 2H-2H
.

By Lemma 10.4 of
[
3
]
, there exists a proper generalized arithmetic progression
P^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of rank
D^{\prime}
for some
0\leq D^{\prime}\leq|S|
,
a subgroup
Y
of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that writing
P:=P^{\prime}+Y
, we have that
|P|=|P^{\prime}||Y|
(which implies that
P
is a proper generalized arithmetic progression in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of rank at most
D^{\prime}+d\leq 2\delta^{-2}+d
)
and

(7.1)

B(S,cD^{-2D}/4)\subseteq P(c)\subseteq B(S,c/4)\text{ and }B(S,D^{-2D}/4)\subseteq P\subseteq B(S,1/4).

Moreover, the vectors
(\{\alpha\cdot v_{i}\})_{\alpha\in S}\in\mathbb{R}^{|S|},1\leq i\leq D
can be made linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
(and so Property (iv) is satisfied).
It then follows from Lemma 8.1 of
[
3
]
that
|P|\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
.
So Property (i) is satisfied.

Since
P\subseteq B(S,1/4)
,
|R(H,h)|\geq\delta^{4}K^{3d}/2
for all
h\in P
.
This proves Property (ii).

We now prove Property (iii). Let
\rho=cD^{-2D}/16
.
Since
|H|\geq\delta K^{d}
, by Lemma 4.1 of
[
3
]
, there exists
x_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
such that

\mathbb{E}_{h\in B(S,\rho)}\mathbb{1}_{H}(x_{0}+h)\geq\mathbb{E}_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}\mathbb{1}_{H}(h)\geq\delta.

Set

H^{\prime}:=\{x\in H\colon x-x_{0}\in B(S,\rho)\}.

We have that
|H^{\prime}|\geq\delta|B(S,\rho)|\gg_{c,\delta,d}K^{d}
by Lemma 8.2 of
[
3
]
. Moreover,
2H^{\prime}-2H^{\prime}\subseteq B(S,4\rho)\subseteq P(c)
by (
7.1
). This finishes the proof.
∎

We may derive the following description for Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 using Proposition
7.1
(recall the definition in Section
4.1
):

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq\max\{4s+19,35\}
,
0<c\leq 1
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
be a prime dividing
K
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form, and
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
of cardinality at least
\delta K^{d}
. For any Freiman
M
-homomorphism
\xi\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
of order 16,
there exist
0<\rho=\rho(\delta,d)<1/4
,
a homogeneous proper generalized arithmetic progression

P=\{x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D}\colon x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-L_{i},L_{i}),1\leq i\leq D\}

in
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
of rank
O_{\delta,d}(1)
and cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
, a set
S\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}
of cardinality
O_{\delta}(1)
, and a map
\omega\colon P\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
such that the followings hold:

(1)

B(S,c\rho)\subseteq P(c)\subseteq P\subseteq B(S,1/4)\subseteq 2H-2H
;

(2)

For all
h\in P
, the set
R(H,h):=\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in H\colon h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}-h_{4}=h\}
is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta}K^{3d}
;

(3)

there exists
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{c,\delta,d}K^{d}
such that
2H^{\prime}-2H^{\prime}\subseteq P(c)
;

(4)

the vectors
(\{\alpha\cdot v_{i}\})_{\alpha\in S}\in\mathbb{R}^{|S|},1\leq i\leq D
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
;

(5)

for all
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\in H
with
u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4}\in P
, we have

\xi(u_{1})+\xi(u_{2})-\xi(u_{3})-\xi(u_{4})\equiv\omega(u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3}),\iota(u_{4})};

(6)

for all
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\in P
with
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}
, we have

\omega(u_{1})+\omega(u_{2})\equiv\omega(u_{3})+\omega(u_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})},

i.e. the map
\omega\colon P\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 4.

Proof.

We always assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d}1
in the proof.
By Proposition
7.1
, there exist
P,S
and
\rho
satisfying Properties (i)-(iv).
Denote
