\tilde{\xi}(h):=(h,J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+\xi(h))
for
h\in H
.
Since
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16,

\pi^{-1}(h)\cap(2\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}2\tilde{\xi}(H))
is an equivalence class for all
h\in P
.

We claim that
\pi^{-1}(h)\cap(2\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}2\tilde{\xi}(H))
is actually a strong equivalence class for all
h\in P
. Indeed, fix

h\in P
. Since
d\geq 4s+19
,
by Proposition
5.4
(setting
k=4
),
it suffices to show that for any subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
containing
\iota(h)
of dimension at most
3s+12
, there exists
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in R(H,h)
such that
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3}),\iota(h_{4})\}\cap Y\neq\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h)\}
. This is clearly true since
d\geq 3s+15
.

The claim implies that for all
h\in H
, there exists
\omega(h)\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
such that Property (v) holds.
Since
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16,
it follows from Property (v) that for all
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\in P
with
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}
and for all
(u_{i,1},u_{i,2},u_{i,3},u_{i,4})\in R(H,u_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq 4
, since
u_{i,4}=u_{i,1}+u_{i,2}-u_{i,3}
, we have

\omega(u_{1})+\omega(u_{2})\equiv\omega(u_{3})+\omega(u_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(u_{j}),\iota(u_{i,j})\colon 1\leq i\leq 4,1\leq j\leq 3\}}.

Since
\prod_{i=1}^{4}|R(H,u_{i})|\gg_{\delta}K^{12d}
and
d\geq\max\{15+s,35\}
, by Proposition
3.10
(setting
\dim(V)=3
,
m=12
,
k=0
,
\dim(U_{0}=0)
) and Lemma
A.3
, we have that

\omega(u_{1})+\omega(u_{2})\equiv\omega(u_{3})+\omega(u_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})}.

So Property (vi) holds.
∎

To conclude Step 3 described in Section
2.3
, we need the following result:

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
C,D,K,L,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\delta>0
,
p
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|\geq\delta K^{d}
,
\xi\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
be a map, and denote
\tilde{\xi}(h):=(h,J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+\xi(h))\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
for all
h\in H
. Suppose that
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H))
admits a
(L,C,D)
-classification
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
.
Then for any subspace
V
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
J^{M}_{V}\cap\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{0\}
and
V\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, and any subset
W\subseteq H\cap\iota^{-1}(V)
,
16
16

16

Here
\iota^{-1}(V)
is understood as the set of
v\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
\iota(v)\in V
.
there exists
W^{\prime}\subseteq W
with
|W^{\prime}|\geq|W|/80^{L}
such that

\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 on
W^{\prime}
.

Proposition
7.3
should be viewed as the analog of Lemma 9.2 of
[
3
]
. Unfortunately, we were unable to prove that Proposition
7.3
holds with
V
replaced
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
(we require the subspace
V
to “stay away” from
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
Y
). This incurs some additional difficulty in the final step of the proof of Theorem
1.5
, as we shall see in Section
9
.

Proof of Proposition
7.3
.

Since
\mathcal{C}_{0}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
are finite sets, there exists
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathcal{C}_{0},\xi(H)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
.
Note that
\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)/(\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})
is a finite abelian group, and is thus isomorphic to groups of the form
\mathbb{Z}/p^{a_{1}}\mathbb{Z}\times\dots\times\mathbb{Z}/p^{a_{t}}\mathbb{Z}
for some
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
1\leq a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\leq N
.
So there exist
1\leq m\leq\binom{d+s-1}{s}
(
\binom{d+s-1}{s}
is the number of monomials in
d
variables of degree
s
),
v_{1},\dots,v_{m}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
and some
1\leq a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\leq N
such that
p^{a_{i}}v_{i}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z},d}(s)
for all
1\leq i\leq m
, and that the map
\theta\colon G:=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{Z}/p^{a_{i}}\mathbb{Z}\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)/(\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})
induced by

\theta(x_{1},\dots,x_{m}):=x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{m}v_{m}

is a group isomorphism.

Enumerate
\mathcal{C}_{0}
as
J=\{c_{0}=\mathbb{0},c_{1},\dots,c_{L}\}
. We may assume without loss of generality that
c_{i}\notin J_{V}^{M}
for all
1\leq i\leq L
.
Then for all
1\leq i\leq L
, we have that

(7.2)

\theta^{-1}(c_{i}\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\neq\mathbb{0}.

Let
\phi_{i}\colon G\to\mathbb{T}
be the linear map given by

\phi_{i}(x_{1}\mod p^{a_{1}}\mathbb{Z},\dots,x_{m}\mod p^{a_{m}}\mathbb{Z}):=\frac{1}{p^{a_{1}}}b_{i,1}x_{1}+\dots+\frac{1}{p^{a_{m}}}b_{i,m}x_{m}\mod\mathbb{Z}

for some
b_{i,1},\dots,b_{i,m}\in\mathbb{Z}
. By (
7.2
), it is
not hard to find some appropriate
b_{i,1},\dots,b_{i,m}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\phi_{i}\circ\theta^{-1}(c_{i}\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\notin[-1/10,1/10].

Let
\Phi\colon G\to\mathbb{T}^{L}
be the linear map given by

\Phi(x)=(\phi_{1}(x),\dots,\phi_{L}(x))

for all
x\in G
. Then

\Phi\circ\theta^{-1}(c_{i}\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\notin[-1/10,1/10]^{L}

for all
1\leq i\leq L
.

We may cover
\mathbb{T}^{L}
by cubes of the form
Q=[\frac{j_{1}}{80},\frac{j_{1}+1}{80})\times\dots\times[\frac{j_{L}}{80},\frac{j_{L}+1}{80})\subseteq\mathbb{T}^{L}
, for some
0\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{L}\leq 79
, and cover
G
by sets of the form
\Phi^{-1}(Q)
.
By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a cube
Q
such that the set

W^{\prime}:=\{h\in W\colon\Phi\circ\theta^{-1}(\xi(h)\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\in Q\}

is of cardinality at least
|W|/80^{L}
.

It remains to show that
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 on
W^{\prime}
.
Let
h_{1},\dots,h_{16}\in W^{\prime}
with
h_{1}+\dots+h_{8}=h_{9}+\dots+h_{16}
. Denote

\omega:=(\xi(h_{1})+\dots+\xi(h_{8}))-(\xi(h_{9})+\dots+\xi(h_{16})).

It suffices to show that
\omega\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{16})}
.
Since
h_{1}+\dots+h_{8}=h_{9}+\dots+h_{16}
,
V\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
and
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
is a classification of
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{H}\hat{-}8\tilde{H})
, there exists
f\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
such that

\eta:=f-\omega\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{16})}\cap\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\subseteq J^{M}_{V}\cap\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
. Since

\Phi\circ\theta^{-1}(\xi(h_{j})\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\in Q

for all
1\leq j\leq 16
, we have that

\Phi\circ\theta^{-1}(\omega\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\in[-1/10,1/10]^{L}.

Since
\eta\in J^{M}_{V}\cap\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
, we have that

\Phi\circ\theta^{-1}(\eta\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})=\mathbb{0}.

Since
f\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, we have that

\Phi\circ\theta^{-1}(f\mod\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\cap J^{M}_{V})\notin[-1/10,1/10]^{L}.

unless
f=0
. Since
f-\omega=\eta
, we must have that
f=0
and thus
\omega=\eta\in J^{M}_{V}
. This completes the proof.
∎

8.
Structure-obstacle decomposition

Note that if one can further require the subspace
V
appearing in Proposition
7.3
to be
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, then Theorem
1.5
follows immediately from Theorem
4.7
, Propositions
7.2
and
7.3
. Unfortunately we do not know if such an improvement of Proposition
7.3
is possible.
To overcome this difficulty, we start by using Proposition
7.3
to study the Freiman
M
-homomorphism structure on certain subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
To this end, we need to introduce the following decomposition of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
which is suitable for our needs.

Definition 8.1

(Structure-obstacle decomposition)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime},s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d=d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}
, and
p
be a prime, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form.
Let
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)\times\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be a structure-obstacle pair. Let
T,U
be subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. We say that
(T,U)
is an
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y,M,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime})
-structure-obstacle decomposition
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
if the following conditions hold:

•

\text{rank}(M|_{U})=dim(U)=d^{\prime\prime}
;

•

J_{U}^{M}\cap\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{0\}
and
U\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
;

•

T=U^{\perp_{M}}
.

Roughly speaking, if
(T,U)
is a structure-obstacle decomposition, then
U
is a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
where Proposition
7.3
applies (and
M|_{U}
is non-degenerate). Treating
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
as the unions of
U+v,v\in T
, this allows us
describe the structure of
\xi|_{U+v}
using the results proved in previous sections.

It follows from Lemma
A.1
that for any structure-obstacle decomposition
(T,U)
,
M|_{U}
and
M|_{T}
are non-degenerate,
T\cap U=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, and
T+U=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

The rest of this section is devoted to proving the following proposition:

Proposition 8.2
.

Let
C,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime},D,L,s\in\mathbb{N},d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
d=d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,L}1
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|\geq\delta K^{d}
,
\xi\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
be a map, and denote
\tilde{\xi}(h):=(h,J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+\xi(h))\in\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
for
h\in H
. Suppose that
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H))
admits a
(L,C,D)
-classification
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
, and let
(T_{0},U_{0})
be a
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y,M,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime})
-structure-obstacle decomposition of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. If

d^{\prime}\geq\max\{s+1,D+3,7\}
and
d^{\prime\prime}\geq\max\{4s+23,35\}
, then there exist a subset
H^{\prime}
of
H
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d,L}K^{d}
such that for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{16}\in H^{\prime}
with
h_{1}+\dots+h_{8}=h_{9}+\dots+h_{16}
, we have that

\xi(h_{1})+\dots+\xi(h_{8})\equiv\xi(h_{9})+\dots+\xi(h_{16})\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{15})\}+T_{0}}.

We briefly explain the idea of the proof of Proposition
8.2
.
Roughly speaking, modulo
J^{M}_{T_{0}}
, Proposition
7.3
ensures that
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 on many layers of
U_{0}+v,v\in T_{0}
. We then apply Proposition
7.2
to compare the structure of
\xi
on different layers, and conclude that
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 on a large subset of
H
(again modulo
J^{M}_{T_{0}}
).
To be more precise, the proof of Proposition
8.2
is divided into the following steps.

Step 0. Preparisions.

In the rest of the section we assume that
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,L}1
. Let
A
be the matrix associated to
M
. We may assume without loss of generality that
M
is homogenous, i.e.
M(n)=(nA)\cdot n
.
Since
H
is a finite set, we may assume that
\xi(H)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Extending
\xi
periodically to the domain
\mathbb{Z}_{p^{N}K}^{d}
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that
f(n+Km)\equiv f(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}
for all
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
and
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. We may identify
\xi(h)
as polynomial map from the ring
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
to
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}
.

We first need to extend the decomposition
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}=T_{0}+U_{0}
to decompositions of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. To achieve this, we need to following lemma.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime and
v_{1},\dots,v_{d}
be a basis of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Then there exist
c_{1},\dots,c_{d}\in\{1,\dots,p-1\}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\iota(u_{i})=c_{i}v_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
such that the determinant of the
d\times d
matrix with
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}
as row vectors is equal to 1.

Proof.

For
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, let
B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})
denote the
d\times d
matrix haivng
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}
as row vectors. Let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
be the smallest integer in absolute value such that there exist
c_{1},\dots,c_{d}\in\{1,\dots,p-1\}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
\iota(u_{i})=c_{i}v_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq d
such that
\det(B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d}))=m
. Since
\iota(B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d}))
is non-degenerate, we have that
m\notin p\mathbb{Z}
and in particular
m\neq 0
. Replacing
u_{1}
by
-u_{1}
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality
m>0
.

It suffices to show that
m=1
. Suppose on the contrary that
m>1
. Let
b_{i,j}
denote the
(i,j)
-th entry of
B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})
, and
B_{i,j}
be the
(d-1)\times(d-1)
matrix obtained by removing the
i
-th row and
j
-th column of
B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})
. Let
t
be the greatest common divider for all the non-zero numbers among
\det(B_{1,1}),\dots,\det(B_{1,d})
. Then we must have that
m=kt
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Since
m\notin p\mathbb{Z}
, we have that
t\notin p\mathbb{Z}
. If
k\neq 1
, then there exist
x,y\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
xk+yp=1<k
. Clearly
x\notin p\mathbb{Z}
. Let
v_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be such that
v_{1}\cdot(\det(B_{1,1}),\dots,\det(B_{1,d}))=t
.
Then

B(xu_{1}+ypv_{1},\dots,u_{d})=xB(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})+yB(v_{1},u_{2},\dots,u_{d})=xkt+ypt=t<kt=m.

Since
\iota(xu_{1}+ypv_{1})=x\iota(u_{1})=\iota(xc_{1})v_{1}
and since
x\notin p\mathbb{Z}
, we have a contradiction to the minimality of
m
.

So we must have that
k=1
and
m=t
. So
\det(B_{1,j})\in m\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq j\leq d
. Using a similar argument for other rows of
B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})
, we get a similar contradiction unless
\det(B_{i,j})\in m\mathbb{Z}
for all
1\leq i,j\leq d
. Since
\det(B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d}))=m
, by the formula of inverse matrices,
B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d})^{-1}
is a matrix with integer entries. So
m^{-1}=\det(B(u_{1},\dots,u_{d}))^{-1}\in\mathbb{Z}
. This is only possible if
m=\pm 1
, a contradiction. So we must have that
m=1
and we are done.
∎

Let
v_{1},\dots,v_{d^{\prime\prime}}
be a basis of
U_{0}
and
v_{d^{\prime\prime}+1},\dots,v_{d}
be a basis of
T_{0}
. Let
c_{1},\dots,c_{d}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}
be given by Lemma
8.3
. Let
\tilde{U}
be the set of all linear combinations of
u_{1},\dots,u_{d^{\prime\prime}}
over
\mathbb{Z}
, and
U\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
be the set of all linear combinations of
u_{1},\dots,u_{d^{\prime\prime}}
over
\mathbb{Z}
modulo
K\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Define
\tilde{T}
and
T
similarly. Since
\iota(u_{i})=c_{i}v_{i}
, we have that
\iota(\tilde{T})=\iota(T)=T_{0}
and
\iota(\tilde{U})=\iota(U)=U_{0}
. For
1\leq i\leq d^{\prime\prime}
, let
\phi_{\tilde{U}}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be the linear map given by
\phi_{\tilde{U}}(e_{i}):=u_{i}
,
\phi_{U}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}\to U
be the linear map given by
\phi_{U}(e_{i}):=u_{i}\mod\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
, and
\phi_{U_{0}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}\to U_{0}
to be the linear map given by
\phi_{U_{0}}(e_{i}):=\iota(u_{i})=c_{i}v_{i}
, where
e_{i}
is the
i
-th standard unit vector.
Denote
\phi_{\tilde{T}},\phi_{T}
and
\phi_{T_{0}}
in similar ways.
It is not hard to see that all of
\tilde{\phi}_{U},\tilde{\phi}_{T},\phi_{U},\phi_{T},\phi_{U_{0}}
and
\phi_{T_{0}}
are bijections. Moreover,
since the determinant of the
d\times d
matrix with
u_{1},\dots,u_{d}
as row vectors is equal to 1,
we have that
\tilde{T}+\tilde{U}=\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
\tilde{T}\cap\tilde{U}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
,
T+U=\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
and
T\cap U=\{\mathbb{0}\}
.
Let
\pi_{T_{0}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to T_{0}
,
\pi_{U_{0}}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to U_{0}
be the projection maps with respect to the decomposition
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}=T_{0}+U_{0}
.

In this proof of Proposition
8.2
, we need to frequently project vectors in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
to the
\tilde{U}
direction.
For
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
,
Let
\sigma_{m}\colon\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
be the map given by

\sigma_{m}(f)(n):=f(\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n)+p\phi_{\tilde{T}}(m)).

Let
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M^{\prime}=M\circ\phi_{U_{0}}
. Since
\text{rank}(M|_{U_{0}})=d^{\prime\prime}
,
M^{\prime}
is non-degenerate.
For
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
let
\Gamma^{s}_{i}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}},M^{\prime})
be the set of all
(h,I+f)
where
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
,
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
and
I=J^{M^{\prime}}_{V}
for some subspace
V
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
containing
\iota(h)
with
\dim(V)\leq i
if
\iota(h)\neq\mathbb{0}
and
\dim(V)\leq i-1
if
\iota(h)=\mathbb{0}
.
Let
\sim^{\prime}
be a relation defined on
\Gamma^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}},M^{\prime})
such that

(h,I+f)\sim^{\prime}(h^{\prime},I^{\prime}+f^{\prime})
if and only if
h=h^{\prime}
and
f-f^{\prime}\in I+I^{\prime}
.

Proposition 8.4
.

Let
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d}(s)
and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
. Suppose that
d\geq 2k+3
. Then

f\in J^{M}_{V}\Rightarrow\sigma_{m}(f)\in J^{M^{\prime}}_{\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V))}\text{ for all }m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}.

Conversely, there exist
N=O_{d,s}(1)
such that

\sigma_{m}(f)\in J^{M^{\prime}}_{\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V))}\text{ for all }m\in[N]^{d^{\prime}}\Rightarrow f\in J^{M}_{V+T_{0}}.

Proof.

Let
v_{1},\dots,v_{k}
be a basis of
V
.
Assume that
\phi_{U_{0}}(n)=nB
for some
d^{\prime\prime}\times d
matrix
B
. Then
BAB^{T}
is the matrix associated with
M^{\prime}
. Denote
L_{v}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(v\tau(A))\cdot n
for all
v,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
L^{\prime}_{v}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(v\tau(BAB^{T}))\cdot n
for all
v,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
.

Fix any
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
. Let
\tilde{M}
and
\tilde{M}^{\prime}
be the regular liftings of
M
and
M^{\prime}
respectively. Then

(8.1)

\sigma_{m}(\tilde{M})(n)\equiv\frac{1}{p}(\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n)\tau(A))\cdot\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n)\equiv\frac{1}{p}(n\tau(BAB^{T}))\cdot n=\tilde{M}^{\prime}(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}.

If
v\in U_{0}
, then assuming that
v=\phi_{0}(w)
for some
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, we have that

\begin{split}\sigma_{m}(L_{\tau(v)})(n)\equiv\frac{1}{p}(\tau(\phi_{U_{0}}(w))\tau(A))\cdot\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n)\equiv\frac{1}{p}\tau(wBAB^{T})\cdot n\equiv L^{\prime}_{\tau(w)}(n)=L^{\prime}_{\tau(\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(v))}(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

On the other hand, if
v\in T_{0}
, then since
T_{0}=U_{0}^{\perp_{M}}
, we have that

\sigma_{m}(L_{\tau(v)})(n)\equiv\frac{1}{p}(\tau(vA)\cdot\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n))\equiv\frac{1}{p}\tau(vA\cdot\iota\circ\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n))=\frac{1}{p}\tau(vA\cdot\phi_{U_{0}}\circ\iota(n))=0\mod\mathbb{Z}.

So

(8.2)

\sigma_{m}(L_{\tau(v)})(n)\equiv L^{\prime}_{\tau\circ\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(v))}(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}

for all
v\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
.

For all
f\in J^{M}_{V}
, since
d\geq 2k+3
,
by Proposition
A.9
, we may write

f=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s}R_{i}\tilde{M}^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{\tau(h_{j})}^{i_{j}}

for some
R_{i}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z},d}(s-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}))
. By (
8.1
) and (
8.2
), we have that

\sigma_{m}(f)-\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s}\sigma_{m}(R_{i}){\tilde{M^{\prime}}}^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}{L^{\prime}}_{\tau\circ\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}\circ\pi_{U_{0}}(h_{j})}^{i_{j}}

is an integer valued polynomial with
\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}
-coefficients and thus must
belong to
\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z},d}(s)
. So
\sigma_{m}(f)\in J^{M^{\prime}}_{\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V))}.

Conversely, suppose that

\sigma_{m}(f)\in J^{M^{\prime}}_{\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V))}\text{ for all }m\in[N]^{d^{\prime}}
. Fix any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
with
\iota(n)\in V(M^{\prime})\cap(\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V)))^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}
.
We have that

\sigma_{m}(f)(n)=f(\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n)+p\phi_{\tilde{T}}(m))\in\mathbb{Z}

for all
m\in[N]^{d^{\prime\prime}}
.
Since
f(\phi_{\tilde{U}}(n)+p\phi_{\tilde{T}}(\cdot))
is a polynomial of degree at most
s
, by interpolation, for some appropriate
N=O_{d,s}(1)
, then this implies that
f(\tilde{\phi}_{U}(n)+p\tilde{\phi}_{T}(m))\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
with
\iota(n)\in V(M^{\prime})\cap(\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V)))^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}
and for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
.

We claim that for all
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
with
\iota(\phi_{\tilde{U}}(x)+\phi_{\tilde{T}}(y))\in V(M)\cap V^{\perp_{M}}
, we have that
\iota(x)\in V(M^{\prime})\cap(\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V)))^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}
and that
y\in p\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
.
If the claim holds, the for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\iota(h)\in V(M)\cap V^{\perp_{M}}
, we may write
n=\phi_{\tilde{U}}(x)+p\phi_{\tilde{T}}(y)
for some
x\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}^{d^{\prime}}
. So
f(n)=\sigma_{y}(f)(x)\in\mathbb{Z}
and we are done.

So it remains to prove the claim.
Projecting everything down to
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, it suffices to show that for all
x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
and
y\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime}}
with
\phi_{U_{0}}(x)+\phi_{T_{0}}(y)
belonging to
V(M)\cap V^{\perp_{M}}
, we have that
x
belongs to
V(M^{\prime})\cap(\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V)))^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}
and that
y=\mathbb{0}
.
Indeed, for all
t\in T_{0}
, we have that
0=((\phi_{U_{0}}(x)+\phi_{T_{0}}(y))A)\cdot t=(\phi_{T_{0}}(y)A)\cdot t
. Since
M|_{T_{0}}
is non-degenerate, this implies that
\phi_{T_{0}}(y)=\mathbb{0}
and thus
y=\mathbb{0}
. Therefore,
\phi_{U_{0}}(x)
belonging to
V(M)\cap V^{\perp_{M}}
, which implies that
x
belongs to
V(M^{\prime})\cap(\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}(\pi_{U_{0}}(V)))^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}
.
∎

Step 1. structure of the solutions on different layers.

We first provide a description of
\xi
on many layers
U_{0}+v,v\in T_{0}
.

Fix any
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and denote
\sigma:=\sigma_{m}
for convenience.
For
v\in T
and
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, let

\eta_{v}(u):=\sigma\circ\xi(v+\phi_{U}(u))\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d^{\prime\prime}}(s).

Since
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H))
admits a classification
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
, for all
v_{1},\dots,v_{16}\in T
and
w_{1},\dots,w_{16}\in U
with
v_{i}+w_{i}\in H,1\leq i\leq 16
and with
v_{1}+\dots+v_{8}=v_{9}+\dots+v_{16}
,
w_{1}+\dots+w_{8}=w_{9}+\dots+w_{16}
, either
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{i})+\iota(w_{i})\colon 1\leq i\leq 16\}\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
or

\xi(v_{1}+w_{1})+\dots+\xi(v_{8}+w_{8}))-(\xi(v_{9}+w_{9})+\dots+\xi(v_{16}+w_{16}))\equiv f\mod J^{M}_{\iota(v_{1}+w_{1}),\dots,\iota(v_{15}+w_{15})}

for some
f\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
.
Since
d\geq 33
,
by Proposition
8.4
, this implies that

(8.3)

\begin{split}&\text{for all $v_{1},\dots,v_{16}\in T$ and $u_{1},\dots,u_{16}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}$ with $v_{i}+\phi_{U}(u_{i})\in H,1\leq i\leq 16$,}\\
&\text{and with $v_{1}+\dots+v_{8}=v_{9}+\dots+v_{16}$ and $u_{1}+\dots+u_{8}=u_{9}+\dots+u_{16}$,}\\
&\text{either $\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{i})+\iota(\phi_{U}(u_{i}))\colon 1\leq i\leq 16\}\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}$}\\
&\text{or $(\eta_{v_{1}}(u_{1})+\dots+\eta_{v_{8}}(u_{8}))-(\eta_{v_{9}}(u_{9})+\dots+\eta_{v_{16}}(u_{16}))\equiv g\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\dots,\iota(u_{15})}$}\\
&\text{for some $g\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})$.}\end{split}

Since
|H|\geq\delta K^{d}
, there exists
T_{1}\subseteq T
with
|T_{1}|\geq\delta K^{d^{\prime}}/2
such that
|(v+U)\cap H|\geq\delta K^{d^{\prime\prime}}/2
for all
v\in T_{1}
. Let
T_{2}
be the set of
v\in T_{1}
such that
J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0}}\cap\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{0\}
and
(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0})\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
.

Claim 1.
We have that
|T_{2}|\geq\delta K^{d^{\prime}}/4
.

To see this, let
R_{1}
be the set of
v\in T
such that
(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0})\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
, and
R_{2}
be the set of
v\in T
such that
J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0}}\cap\mathcal{C}_{0}\neq\{0\}
.
Since
U_{0}\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, if
(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0})\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
, then
\pi_{T_{0}}(\iota(v))
is contained
\pi_{T_{0}}(Y)
. Since
Y
is the union of at most
C
subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension at most
D
, the set
R_{1}
is of cardinality at most
O_{C}(K^{D})
.
On the other hand, we may write
R_{2}=\cup_{f\in\mathcal{C}_{0}\backslash\{\mathbb{0}\}}R_{2,f}
, where
R_{2,f}
is the set of all

v\in T
, such that

f\in J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0}}
. Then
f\in\cap_{v\in R_{2,f}}J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0}}
.
If
|R_{2,f}|>K^{s}
, then there exist
v_{1},\dots,v_{s+1}\in R_{2,f}
which are
p
-linearly independent. Since

\text{rank}(M|_{U_{0}})=d^{\prime\prime}
, by Lemma
A.1
(iv), we have that
\dim(U_{0}\cap U_{0}^{\perp_{M}})=0
.
By Lemma
A.1
(ii), this implies that

\text{rank}(M|_{U_{0}^{\perp_{M}}})=d^{\prime}
. So
by Proposition
3.7
(setting
r=1
), since
d\geq d^{\prime\prime}+7
(or equivalently,
d^{\prime}\geq 7
),
we have that
f\in J^{M}_{U_{0}}
, a contradiction to the fact that
J_{U_{0}}^{M}\cap\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{0\}
.
Therefore,
|R_{2,f}|\leq K^{s}
for all
f\in\mathcal{C}_{0}\backslash\{0\}
and so
|R_{2}|\leq LK^{s}
. Since
|T_{1}|\geq\delta K^{d^{\prime}}/2
and
d^{\prime}\geq\max\{D+1,s+1\}
, we have that
|T_{2}|\geq\delta K^{d^{\prime}}/4
. This proves Claim 1.

Since
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H))
admits a classification
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
,
by the construction of
T_{2}
and Proposition
7.3
, for all
v\in T_{2}
,
there exists a subset
W_{v}\subseteq(U+v)\cap H\subseteq\iota^{-1}(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v)\}+U_{0})
with
|W_{v}|\geq|(U+v)\cap H|/80^{L}\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 on
W_{v}
. Write
U_{v}:=(H-v)\cap U
. Then
for all
u_{1},\dots,u_{16}\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v})
with
u_{1}+\dots+u_{8}=u_{9}+\dots+u_{16}
, we have that

\sum_{i=1}^{8}\xi(v+\phi_{U}(u_{i}))\equiv\sum_{i=9}^{16}\xi(v+\phi_{U}(u_{i}))\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v+\phi_{U}(u_{i}))\colon 1\leq i\leq 15\}}.

So by Proposition
8.4
,

(8.4)

\begin{split}&\text{for all $v\in T_{2}$ and $u_{1},\dots,u_{16}\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v})$ with $u_{1}+\dots+u_{8}=u_{9}+\dots+u_{16}$,}\\
&\text{we have $\eta_{v}(u_{1})+\dots+\eta_{v}(u_{8})\equiv\eta_{v}(u_{9})+\dots+\eta_{v}(u_{16})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\dots,\iota(u_{15})}$.}\end{split}

Claim 2.
There exist a subset
T_{3}
of
T_{2}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime}}
and some
v_{0}\in T_{3}
such that
|U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}}|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
for all
v\in T_{3}
.

For all
u\in U
, let
S_{u}
denote the set of
v\in T_{2}
such that
u\in U_{v}
. Then
\sum_{u\in U}|S_{u}|=\sum_{v\in T_{2}}|U_{v}|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d}
by Claim 1.
On the other hand, note that

\begin{split}\sum_{v,v^{\prime}\in T_{2},v\neq v^{\prime}}|U_{v}\cap U_{v^{\prime}}|=\sum_{u\in U}\binom{|S_{u}|}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2|U|}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in U}|S_{u}|\Bigr)^{2}-\Bigl(\sum_{u\in U}|S_{u}|\Bigr)\geq O_{\delta,L}(1)^{-1}K^{d+d^{\prime}}-K^{d}.\end{split}

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist
v_{0}\in T_{2}
such that

\sum_{v\in T_{2},v\neq v_{0}}|U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}}|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d}.

Again by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist a subset
T^{\prime}_{3}
of
T_{2}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that
|U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}}|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
for all
v\in T^{\prime}_{3}
. This proves Claim 2 by setting
T_{3}:=T^{\prime}_{3}\cup\{v_{0}\}
.

Let
T_{3}
and
v_{0}
be given by Claim 2.
By (
8.4
), for all
v\in T_{2}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{16}\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
, we have that

u_{1}+\dots+u_{8}=u_{9}+\dots+u_{16}\Rightarrow\eta_{v}(u_{1})+\dots+\eta_{v}(u_{8})\equiv\eta_{v}(u_{9})+\dots+\eta_{v}(u_{16})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\dots,\iota(u_{15})}.

Applying Proposition
7.2
to
\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
, we deduce that if
d^{\prime\prime}\geq\max\{4s+19,35\}
, then there exists
0<\rho=\rho(\delta,d^{\prime\prime})<1/4
such that for all
v\in T_{3}
, there exist
a proper and homogeneous generalized arithmetic progression
P_{v}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
of rank
O_{\delta,d,L}(1)
and cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d,L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
(note that
\phi_{U}(P_{v})
is a generalized arithmetic progression in
U
of the same rank and cardinality), a set
S_{v}\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}}
of cardinality
O_{\delta,L}(1)
, and a map
\omega_{v}\colon P_{v}\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
such that the followings hold:

(1)

B(S_{v},\rho)\subseteq P_{v}\subseteq B(S_{v},1/4)\subseteq 2\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})-2\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
;

(2)

For all
h\in P_{v}
, the set
R(\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}}),h)
is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}
, where for all
E\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
and
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, we denote

R(E,h):=\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in E^{4}\colon h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}-h_{4}=h\}.

(3)

for all
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
with
u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4}\in P_{v}
,

(8.5)

\eta_{v}(u_{1})+\eta_{v}(u_{2})-\eta_{v}(u_{3})-\eta_{v}(u_{4})\equiv\omega_{v}(u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3}),\iota(u_{4})}.

(4)

for all
u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4}\in P_{v}
with
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}
,

(8.6)

\omega_{v}(u_{1})+\omega_{v}(u_{2})\equiv\omega_{v}(u_{3})+\omega_{v}(u_{4})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})},

i.e. the map
\omega_{v}\colon P_{v}\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
is a Freiman
M^{\prime}
-homomorphism of order 4.

Step 2.
\omega_{v}
coincides with
\omega_{v_{0}}
on a large subset
W_{v}
of
P_{v}
(up to a constant
b_{v}
).

Our next step is to compare
\omega_{v}
with
\omega_{v_{0}}
and conclude that they only differ by a constant.
Let
T_{4}
be the set of
v\in T_{3}
such that
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{0}),\iota(v)\}+U_{0}
intersects
Y
trivially. Since
U_{0}\cap Y
and
U\cap T
are trivial, the set of
v\in T_{3}
such that
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{0}),\iota(v)\}+U_{0}
intersects
Y
non-trivially is of cardinality at most

Cp^{D+1}(K/p)^{d^{\prime}}=CK^{d^{\prime}}/p^{d^{\prime}-D-1}
.
Since
d^{\prime}\geq D+2
, we have

|T_{4}|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime}}
.
Pick any
v\in T_{4}
and
u\in B(S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v},\rho/4)
.
Let
G_{v}(u)
denote the set of all
(u_{1},\dots,u_{16})\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v_{0}})^{8}\times\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v})^{8}
such that
u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4}=x
,
u_{5}+u_{6}-u_{7}-u_{8}=x+u
,
u_{9}+u_{10}-u_{11}-u_{12}=y
and
u_{13}+u_{14}-u_{15}-u_{16}=y+u
for some
x\in B(S_{v_{0}},\rho/4)
and
y\in B(S_{v},\rho/4)
.
We first estimate the cardinality of
G_{v}(u)
.
For any
x\in B(S_{v_{0}},\rho/4)
and
y\in B(S_{v},\rho/4)
, since
x+u\in B(S_{v_{0}},\rho)
and
y+u\in B(S_{v},\rho)
, we have that

|R(\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v_{0}}),x)|,|R(\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v_{0}}),x+u)|,|R(\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}),y)|,|R(\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}),y+u)|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}.

So

|G_{v}(u)|\gg_{\delta,L}|B(S_{v_{0}},\rho/4)|\cdot|B(S_{v},\rho/4)|\cdot K^{12d^{\prime\prime}}\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{14d^{\prime\prime}},

where we used the fact that
|B(S,\rho)|\geq\rho^{|S|}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
for all
S\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}}
and
\rho>0
(see Lemma 8.1 of
[
3
]
).

Pick any
(u_{1},\dots,u_{16})\in G_{v}(u)
and let
x\in B(S_{v_{0}},\rho/4)
and
y\in B(S_{v},\rho/4)
be associated to this tuple. Then we have that
x,x+u\in P_{v_{0}}
and
y,y+u\in P_{v}
. Since
\omega_{v}
is a Freiman
M^{\prime}
-homomorphism of order 4 on
P_{v}
, by (
8.3
) and (
8.6
), we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\omega_{v_{0}}(u)-\omega_{v}(u)\equiv\omega_{v_{0}}(x+u)-\omega_{v_{0}}(x)-\omega_{v}(y+u)+\omega_{v}(y)\\
&\equiv\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{5})+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{6})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{7})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{8})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{1})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{2})+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{3})+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{4})\\
&\quad-\eta_{v}(u_{13})-\eta_{v}(u_{14})+\eta_{v}(u_{15})+\eta_{v}(u_{16})+\eta_{v}(u_{9})+\eta_{v}(u_{10})-\eta_{v}(u_{11})-\eta_{v}(u_{12})\\
&\equiv f(u_{1},\dots,u_{16})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\dots,\iota(u_{16})}\end{split}

for some
f(u_{1},\dots,u_{16})\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
, since

\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{0})+\iota(\phi_{U}(u_{1})),\dots,\iota(v_{0})+\iota(\phi_{U}(u_{8})),\iota(v)+\iota(\phi_{U}(u_{9})),\dots,\iota(v)+\iota(\phi_{U}(u_{16}))\}

as a subspace of
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{0}),\iota(v)\}+U_{0}
intersects
Y
trivially.
By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist a subset
G^{\prime}_{v}(u)
of
G_{v}(u)
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,L}K^{14d^{\prime\prime}}
and some
f_{v}(u)\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
such that

\begin{split}\omega_{v_{0}}(u)-\omega_{v}(u)\equiv f_{v}(u)\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\dots,\iota(u_{16})}=J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u),\iota(u_{2}),\dots,\iota(u_{15})}\end{split}

for all
(u_{1},\dots,u_{16})\in G^{\prime}_{v}(u)
(note that
u_{1}
and
u_{16}
are linear combinations of
u,u_{2},\dots,u_{15}
).
So

\begin{split}\omega_{v_{0}}(u)-\omega_{v}(u)\equiv f_{v}(u)\mod\bigcap_{(u_{1},\dots,u_{16})\in G^{\prime}_{v}(u)}J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u),\iota(u_{2}),\dots,\iota(u_{15})}.\end{split}

Since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq\max\{s+15,35\}
,
|G^{\prime}_{v}(u)|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{14d^{\prime\prime}}
, and since
u_{1},u_{16}
are uniquely determined by
u,u_{2},\dots,u_{15}
,
by Proposition
3.10
, we have that

(8.7)

\begin{split}\omega_{v_{0}}(u)-\omega_{v}(u)\equiv f_{v}(u)\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}\end{split}

for all
v\in T_{4}
and
u\in B(S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v},\rho/4)
.
By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
f_{v}\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
such that the set

W_{v}:=\{u\in B(S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v},\rho/4)\colon f_{v}(u)=f_{v}\}

is of cardinality at least

|B(S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v},\rho/4)|/|\mathcal{C}_{0}|\geq(\frac{\rho}{4})^{|S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v}|}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}(L+1)^{-1}\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}},

where again we used Lemma 8.1 of
[
3
]
.

Write
\omega^{\prime}_{v}:=\omega_{v}-\omega_{v_{0}}
.
For all
v\in T_{4}
and
u_{1},u_{2}\in W_{v}
, since
u:=u_{1}-u_{2}\in W_{v}-W_{v}\subseteq B(S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v},\rho)
, it follows from (
8.7
) that

\omega^{\prime}_{v}(u_{1})-\omega^{\prime}_{v}(u_{2})\equiv-f_{v}(u_{1})+f_{v}(u_{2})=f_{v}-f_{v}=0\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2})}.

In other words, the set

X_{v}:=\{(\mathbb{0},J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}+\omega^{\prime}_{v}(u))\colon u\in W_{v}\}\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}},M^{\prime})

is an equivalence class with respect to the relation
\sim^{\prime}
.
Since
|W_{v}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, and
d^{\prime\prime}\geq s+2
, it is impossible for all the elements in
\iota(W_{v})
to be contained in a fixed
s+1
dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
.
By Proposition
5.4
(setting
k=2
), since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq 9
, we have that
X_{v}
is a strong equivalence class. So
there exists
b_{v}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
such that
\omega^{\prime}_{v}(u)=b_{v}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}
for all
u\in W_{v}
.

Step 3.
\eta_{v}
coincides with
\eta_{v_{0}}
on a large subset
U^{\prime}_{v}
of
\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
(up to a constant
g_{v}
).

We next compare
\eta_{v}
with
\eta_{v_{0}}
.
For convenience denote
\eta^{\prime}_{v}:=\eta_{v}-\eta_{v_{0}}
. Our goal is to use the fact that
\omega^{\prime}_{v}
is a constant to conclude that
\eta^{\prime}_{v}
is also a constant. In fact, we have

Claim 3.
For all
v\in T_{4}
, there exists
g_{v}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
and a set
U^{\prime}_{v}\subseteq\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
such that
\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u)\equiv g_{v}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}
for all
u\in U^{\prime}_{v}
.

For
v\in T_{4}
, let
B_{v}
denote the set of
(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})^{4}
such that
u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4}\in W_{v}
.
Since
u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4}\in B(S_{v_{0}}\cup S_{v},\rho)\subseteq P_{v}\cap P_{v_{0}}
for all
(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in B_{v}
, by (
8.5
),

\begin{split}&\quad\eta_{v}(u_{1})+\eta_{v}(u_{2})-\eta_{v}(u_{3})-\eta_{v}(u_{4})\\
&\equiv\omega_{v}(u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4})\equiv\omega_{v_{0}}(u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}-u_{4})+b_{v}\\
&\equiv\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{1})+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{2})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{3})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{4})+b_{v}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3}),\iota(u_{4})}\end{split}

In other words,

(8.8)

\begin{split}\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{1})+\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{2})-\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{3})-\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{4})\equiv b_{v}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3}),\iota(u_{4})}\end{split}

for all
(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in B_{v}
.

Let
B_{v}(u)
denote the set of
(u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})^{3}
such that
(u,u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in B_{v}
.
Since
|R(\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}}),u)|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}
for all
u\in W_{v}\subseteq P_{v}
and since
|W_{v}|\gg_{\delta,L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
U^{\prime\prime}_{v}\subseteq\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
such that for each
u\in U^{\prime\prime}_{v}
,
B_{v}(u)
is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}
.

We need to use the intersection method.
Let
Z
be the set of

((u_{1,2},u_{1,3},u_{1,4}),\dots,(u_{s+3,2},u_{s+3,3},u_{s+3,4}))\in\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v}\cap U_{v_{0}})^{3(s+3)}

such that
u_{i,j},1\leq i\leq s+3,2\leq j\leq 4
are
p
-linearly independent. Consider the bipartite graph

G=(U^{\prime\prime}_{v},Z,E)
such that
(u_{1},(u_{1,2},u_{1,3},u_{1,4}),\dots,(u_{4,2},u_{4,3},u_{4,4}))\in E
if and only if
(u_{i,2},u_{i,3},u_{i,4})\in B_{v}(u_{1})
for all
1\leq i\leq s+3
.
Then by Lemma
A.3
, for each
u_{1}\in U^{\prime\prime}_{v}
, the number of edges connecting
u_{1}
in
G
is at least

\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{(3s+9)d^{\prime\prime}}

since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq 3s+9
. Since
|U^{\prime\prime}_{v}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
((u_{1,2},u_{1,3},u_{1,4}),\dots,(u_{s+3,2},u_{s+3,3},u_{s+3,4}))\in Z
such that the set

U^{\prime}_{v}:=\{u\in U^{\prime\prime}_{v}\colon(u,(u_{1,2},u_{1,3},u_{1,4}),\dots,(u_{s+3,2},u_{s+3,3},u_{s+3,4}))\in E\}

is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
.

Now for all
u,u^{\prime}\in U^{\prime}_{v}
, by (
8.8
), for
1\leq i\leq s+3
,

\begin{split}&\quad\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u)+\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{i,2})-\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{i,3})-\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{i,4})\equiv b_{v}\\
&\equiv\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u^{\prime})+\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{i,2})-\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{i,3})-\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u_{i,4})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u),\iota(u^{\prime}),\iota(u_{i,2}),\iota(u_{i,3}),\iota(u_{i,4})},\end{split}

and so

\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u)\equiv\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u^{\prime})\mod\bigcap_{i=1}^{s+3}J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u),\iota(u^{\prime}),\iota(u_{i,2}),\iota(u_{i,3}),\iota(u_{i,4})}.

Since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq 4s+23
and
u_{i,j},1\leq i\leq s+3,2\leq j\leq s+3
are
p
-linearly independent, by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=2
and
r=3
), we have that

\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u)\equiv\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u^{\prime})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u),\iota(u^{\prime})}.

This means that the set

X_{v}:=\{(\mathbb{0},J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}+\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u))\colon u\in U^{\prime}_{v}\}\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}},M^{\prime})

is an equivalence class with respect to the relation
\sim^{\prime}
.
Since
|U^{\prime}_{v}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
, and
d^{\prime\prime}\geq s+2
, it is impossible for all elements in
\iota(W_{v})
to be contained in a fixed
s+1
dimensional subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
.
By Proposition
5.4
(setting
k=2
), since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq 9
, the set
X_{v}
is a strong equivalence class.
So there exists
g_{v}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)
such that
\eta^{\prime}_{v}(u)\equiv g_{v}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}
for all
u\in U^{\prime}_{v}
. This proves Claim 3.

Step 4. The map
v\mapsto g_{v}
is a Freiman homomorphism modulo
J^{M^{\prime}}
.

We have seen that
\eta^{\prime}_{v}
equals to a constant
g_{v}
. We now show that the map
v\mapsto g_{v}
has a nice Freiman homomorphism structure.
Let
U^{\prime}_{v}
be given by Claim 3. We need the following combinatorial lemma.

Claim 4.
The number of
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\in T_{4}
and
u_{i}\in U^{\prime}_{v_{i}},1\leq i\leq 4
with
v_{1}+v_{2}=v_{3}+v_{4}
and
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}
is at least

\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d}
.

For all
v\in T
, let
Q(v)
denote the set of
(v_{1},v_{2})\in T_{4}^{2}
with
v_{1}+v_{2}=v
. Since

\sum_{v\in T}|Q(v)|=|T_{4}|^{2}\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{2d^{\prime}}
, there exists a subset
T^{\prime}
of
T
with
|T^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that
|Q(v)|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
for all
v\in T^{\prime}
.
Then for all
v\in T^{\prime}
,

\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}}\sum_{(v_{1},v_{2})\in Q(v)}|\{(u_{1},u_{2})\in U^{\prime}_{v_{1}}\times U^{\prime}_{v_{2}}\colon u_{1}+u_{2}=u\}|=\sum_{(v_{1},v_{2})\in Q(v)}|U^{\prime}_{v_{1}}|\cdot|U^{\prime}_{v_{2}}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}+2d^{\prime\prime}}.

By the Pigeonhole Principle, for all
v\in T^{\prime}
, there exists a subset
U(v)
of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime\prime}}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime\prime}}
such that for all
u\in U(v)
, we have

\sum_{(v_{1},v_{2})\in Q(v)}|\{(u_{1},u_{2})\in U^{\prime}_{v_{1}}\times U^{\prime}_{v_{2}}\colon u_{1}+u_{2}=u\}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d}.

So for any
v\in T^{\prime}
and
u\in U(v)
, the number of tuples
(v_{1},v_{2},u_{1},u_{2})
with
(v_{1},v_{2})\in Q(v),(u_{1},u_{2})\in U^{\prime}_{v_{1}}\times U^{\prime}_{v_{2}},u_{1}+u_{2}=u
is
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d}
. For two such tuples
(v_{1},v_{2},u_{1},u_{2})
and
(v_{3},v_{4},u_{3},u_{4})
, it is clear that
v_{1}+v_{2}=v_{3}+v_{4}=v
and
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}=u
. Since there are
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{2d}
such pairs for any
v\in T^{\prime}
and
u\in U(v)
, and there are
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d}
pairs of
(v,u)
with
v\in T^{\prime}
and
u\in U(v)
. So there are in total
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d}
many tuples of
(v_{1},\dots,v_{4},u_{1},\dots,u_{4})
satisfying the requirement of Claim 4.

Denote
U^{\prime}=\cup_{v\in T_{4}}U^{\prime}_{v}
. Then
|U^{\prime}|\geq\inf_{v\in T_{4}}|U^{\prime}_{v}|\cdot|T_{4}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d}
.
By (
8.3
) and (
8.4
),
for all
v_{i}\in T_{4}
and
u_{i}\in U^{\prime}_{v_{i}}\subseteq\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v_{0}}),1\leq i\leq 4

with
v_{1}+v_{2}=v_{3}+v_{4}
and
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}
, either

\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{v_{1}+\phi_{U}^{-1}(u_{1}),\dots,v_{4}+\phi_{U}^{-1}(u_{4})\}\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
or

\begin{split}&\quad f\equiv 4(\eta_{v_{1}}(u_{1})+\eta_{v_{2}}(u_{2})-\eta_{v_{3}}(u_{3})-\eta_{v_{4}}(u_{4}))\\
&\equiv 4(\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{1})+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{2})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{3})-\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{4}))+4(g_{v_{1}}+g_{v_{2}}-g_{v_{3}}-g_{v_{4}})\\
&\equiv 4(g_{v_{1}}+g_{v_{2}}-g_{v_{3}}-g_{v_{4}})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})}\end{split}

for some
f\in\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
.
By Claim 4, the number of
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}\in T_{4}
and
u_{i}\in U^{\prime}_{v_{i}},1\leq i\leq 4
with
v_{1}+v_{2}=v_{3}+v_{4}
and
u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}
is at least

\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d}
.
Since
U_{0}\cap Y
and
U\cap T
are trivial, among these tuples
(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})
, the ones such that
(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{i})\colon 1\leq i\leq 4\}+U_{0})\cap Y\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
is of cardinality at most

3Cp^{2d^{\prime}+3d^{\prime\prime}+(D+2)}(K/p)^{3d}=3CK^{3d}/p^{d^{\prime}-D-2}.

So if
d^{\prime}\geq D+3
, then by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
E\subseteq T^{4}_{4}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime}}
such that for all

(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})\in E
, we have that
v_{1}+v_{2}=v_{3}+v_{4}
,
(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(v_{i})\colon 1\leq i\leq 4\}+U_{0})\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
,
and that the set

Z(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4}):=\{(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in U^{\prime}_{v_{1}}\times U^{\prime}_{v_{2}}\times U^{\prime}_{v_{3}}\times U^{\prime}_{v_{4}}\colon u_{1}+u_{2}=u_{3}+u_{4}\}

is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}
. This means that

\begin{split}g_{v_{1}}+g_{v_{2}}-g_{v_{3}}-g_{v_{4}}\equiv g(v_{1},\dots,v_{4},u_{1},\dots,u_{4})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})}\end{split}

for all
(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})\in E
and
(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in Z(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})
for some
g(v_{1},\dots,v_{4},u_{1}
,
\dots,u_{4})\in 4^{\ast}\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
, where
4^{\ast}
is any integer with
4^{\ast}4\equiv 1\mod p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
. By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist a subset
Z^{\prime}(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})
of
Z(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}
and some
g(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})\in 4^{\ast}\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
such that

\begin{split}g_{v_{1}}+g_{v_{2}}-g_{v_{3}}-g_{v_{4}}\equiv g(v_{1},\dots,v_{4})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})}\end{split}

for all
(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})\in E
and
(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in Z^{\prime}(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})
. So

\begin{split}g_{v_{1}}+g_{v_{2}}-g_{v_{3}}-g_{v_{4}}\equiv g(v_{1},\dots,v_{4})\mod\bigcap_{(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3},u_{4})\in Z^{\prime}(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})}J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\iota(u_{2}),\iota(u_{3})}.\end{split}

Since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq\max\{s+3,11\}
,
|Z^{\prime}(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime\prime}}
and
u_{4}=u_{1}+u_{2}-u_{3}
,
by Proposition
3.10
, we have that

\begin{split}g_{v_{1}}+g_{v_{2}}-g_{v_{3}}-g_{v_{4}}\equiv g(v_{1},\dots,v_{4})\mod J^{M^{\prime}}\end{split}

for all
(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4})\in E
.

Let

\Gamma=\{(v,g_{v}\mod J^{M^{\prime}})\colon v\in T_{4}\}\subseteq T\times(\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)\mod J^{M^{\prime}}),

where
T\times(\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)\mod J^{M^{\prime}})
is a group under the additive operation.

Claim 5.
The set
\Gamma
has additive energy
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime}}
.

Since
|E|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime}}
, by repeatedly using the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
Z\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d^{\prime}}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that for all
u\in Z
, there exists

x\in T_{4}
such that

x+u\in T_{4}
and there exists
T_{4}(u)\subseteq T_{4}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that for all
y\in T_{4}(u)
, we have
y,y+u\in T_{4}
and that

(x,y+u,y,x+u)\in E
.
Then
g_{y+u}-g_{y}\equiv(g_{x+u}-g_{x})-g\mod J^{M^{\prime}}
for some
g\in 4^{\ast}\sigma(\mathcal{C}_{0})
.
Again by the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
T^{\prime}_{4}(u)
of
T_{4}(u)
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that
g_{y+u}-g_{y}\equiv g_{y^{\prime}+u}-g_{y^{\prime}}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}
for all
y,y^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}_{4}(u)
. So the additive energy of
\Gamma
is at least
\sum_{u\in Z}|T^{\prime}_{4}(u)|^{2}\gg O_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime}}
. This proves Claim 5.

Since
\Gamma
is contained in the finite abelian group
T\times(\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{N^{\prime}},d^{\prime\prime}}(s)\mod J^{M^{\prime}})
for some
N^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\Gamma
has additive energy
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{3d^{\prime}}
by Claim 5, we may use some standard additive combinatorial machinery to study the structure of
\Gamma
(see also
[
4
]
). Using the Balog-Szemerédi-Gowers Theorem in
[
2
]
followed by the Plünnecke inequality
[
5
,
6
]
, there exists a subset
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
\Gamma
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that
|8\Gamma^{\prime}-8\Gamma^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
. By Lemma 9.2 of
[
3
]
, there exists a subset
T_{5}\subseteq T_{4}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d^{\prime}}
such that

(8.9)

g_{v_{1}}+\dots+g_{v_{8}}\equiv g_{v_{9}}+\dots+g_{v_{16}}\mod J^{M^{\prime}}

for all
v_{1},\dots,v_{16}\in T_{5}
with
v_{1}+\dots+v_{8}=v_{9}+\dots+v_{16}
.

Step 5. Completion of the proof.
We now have enough information for the maps
\eta_{v}
to complete the proof of Proposition
8.2
.

Let
H^{\prime}:=\cup_{v\in T_{5}}\cup(\phi_{U}(U^{\prime}_{v})+v)
. Then
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
and
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d^{\prime\prime},L}K^{d}
.
For all
h=v+\phi_{U}(u)\in H^{\prime}
with
v\in T_{5}
and
u\in U^{\prime}_{v}
,
define
\eta(h):=\eta_{v_{0}}(u)+g_{v}
(note that
\eta
is well defined since
T\cap U=\{\mathbb{0}\}
).
Then
for all
h_{i}=v_{i}+\phi_{U}(u_{i})\in H^{\prime}
,
v_{i}\in T_{5}
and
u_{i}\in U^{\prime}_{v_{i}}\subseteq\phi_{U}^{-1}(U_{v_{0}}),1\leq i\leq 16
with
h_{1}+\dots+h_{8}=h_{9}+\dots+h_{16}
, since
T\cap U=\{\mathbb{0}\}
, we have that
v_{1}+\dots+v_{8}=v_{9}+\dots+v_{16}
and
u_{1}+\dots+u_{8}=u_{9}+\dots+u_{16}
. So by (
8.4
) and (
8.9
), we have

(8.10)

\begin{split}&\quad(\eta(h_{1})+\dots+\eta(h_{8}))-(\eta(h_{9})+\dots+\eta(h_{16}))\\
&\equiv(\eta_{v_{1}}(u_{1})+\dots+\eta_{v_{8}}(u_{8}))-(\eta_{v_{9}}(u_{9})+\dots+\eta_{v_{16}}(u_{16}))\\
&\equiv(\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{1})+\dots+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{8}))-(\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{9})+\dots+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{16}))+(g_{v_{1}}+\dots+g_{v_{8}})-(g_{v_{9}}+\dots+g_{v_{16}})\\
&\equiv(\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{1})+\dots+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{8}))-(\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{9})+\dots+\eta_{v_{0}}(u_{16}))\equiv 0\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u_{1}),\dots,\iota(u_{15})}.\end{split}

For all
h=v+\phi_{U}(u)\in H^{\prime}
with
v\in T_{5}
and
u\in U^{\prime}_{v}
, since
\iota(u)=\phi^{-1}_{U_{0}}\circ\pi_{U_{0}}\circ\iota(h)
, we have that

\begin{split}\sigma_{m}(\xi(h))=\eta_{v}(u)=\eta_{v_{0}}(u)+g_{v}=\eta(h)\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\iota(u)}=J^{M^{\prime}}_{\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}\circ\pi_{U_{0}}\circ\iota(h)},\end{split}

where we changed the notation of
\sigma
back to
\sigma_{m}
.
Therefore for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{16}\in H^{\prime}
with
h_{1}+\dots+h_{8}=h_{9}+\dots+h_{16}
, it follows from (
8.10
) that

(8.11)

\begin{split}\sigma_{m}((\xi(h_{1})+\dots+\xi(h_{8}))-(\xi(h_{9})+\dots+\xi(h_{16})))\equiv 0\mod J^{M^{\prime}}_{\phi_{U_{0}}^{-1}\circ\pi_{U_{0}}(\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{15})\})}.\end{split}

Since
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime})\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H^{\prime}))
admits the same structure-obstacle decomposition as
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H)\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H))
, for any
N=O_{d}(1)
, replacing
H^{\prime}
by a subset with cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d,L}L^{d}
if necessary, we may use the same argument repeatedly to conclude that (
8.11
) holds for all
m\in[N]^{d^{\prime}}
.
The conclusion of Proposition
8.2

then follows from Proposition
8.4
.

9.
Proof of the main theorem

We are now ready to complete the proof of Theorem
1.5
. Our first task is to
remove the additional modulo over
J^{M}_{T}
in Proposition
8.2
. To this end, we need to use the following lemma to find many structure-obstacle decompositions (which allows us to use the intersection method):

Lemma 9.1

(Abundance of structure-obstacle decompositions)
.

Let
C,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime},D,L,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d,R\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
d=d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}
,
p\gg_{C,d,L,s}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form, and
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)\times\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be a
(L,C,D)
-structure-obstacle pair.
If
d^{\prime}\geq\max\{D,3\},d^{\prime\prime}\geq(R-1)d^{\prime}
and
d\geq 4
, then there exist
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y,M,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime})
-structure-obstacle decompositions
(T_{i},U_{i}),1\leq i\leq R
such that
T_{1},\dots,T_{R}
are linearly independent.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{C,d,L,s}1
.
Let
A
be the matrix associated to
M
.
Suppose that for some
0\leq r\leq R-1
we have chosen
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y,M,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime})
-structure-obstacle decompositions
(T_{i},U_{i}),1\leq i\leq r
such that
T_{1},\dots,T_{r}
are linearly independent. We continue to construct
(T_{r+1},U_{r+1})
.It then suffices to find a subspace
U_{r+1}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
d^{\prime}
such that
{U_{r+1}}^{\perp_{M}}\cap(T_{1}+\dots+T_{r})=\{\mathbb{0}\}
,
\text{rank}(M|_{U_{r+1}})=d^{\prime}
,
U_{r+1}\cap Y=\{\mathbb{0}\}
and
J^{M}_{U_{r+1}}\cap\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
.

Assume that
Y=\cup_{i=1}^{C}V_{i}
and
\mathcal{C}_{0}=\{{0},f_{1},\dots,f_{L}\}
for some subspaces
V_{i}
of dimension at most
D
and
f_{j}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)\backslash J^{M}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be the set of all
d^{\prime\prime}
-tuples
\mathbb{b}=(b_{1},\dots,b_{d^{\prime\prime}})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{d^{\prime\prime}}
. Let
U(\mathbb{b})
denote the span of
b_{1},\dots,b_{d^{\prime\prime}}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
be the set of all linearly dependent
d^{\prime\prime}
-tuples,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
be the set of all
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
such that
M|_{U(\mathbb{b})}
is non-degenerate,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}
be the set of
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
such that
U(\mathbb{b})^{\perp_{M}}\cap(T_{1}+\dots+T_{r})\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
,
\mathcal{B}_{i}
be the set of subspaces
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
such that
U(\mathbb{b})\cap V_{i}\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
, and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{j}
be the set of subspaces
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
such that
f_{j}\in J^{M}_{U(\mathbb{b})}
. To show the existence of
U_{k+1}
, it suffices to show that
|\mathcal{B}|>|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}|+|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}|+\sum_{i=1}^{C}|\mathcal{B}_{i}|+\sum_{j=1}^{L}|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{j}|
.

Clearly
|\mathcal{B}|=p^{dd^{\prime\prime}}
.
By Lemma
A.3
,
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}|=O_{d^{\prime\prime}}(p^{dd^{\prime\prime}-1})
. We now compute
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}|
.
Let
E(\mathbb{b})
be the
d^{\prime\prime}\times d
matrix with
b_{1},\dots,b_{d^{\prime\prime}}
as row vectors. Then for
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
U(\mathbb{b})
is non-degenerate if and only if
\det(E(\mathbb{b})AE(\mathbb{b})^{T})\neq 0
. Not that the map
\mathbb{b}\mapsto\det(E(\mathbb{b})AE(\mathbb{b})^{T})
is a non-trivial polynomial of degree at most
O_{d}(1)
. So it follows from Lemma
A.4
that
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}|=O_{d^{\prime\prime}}(p^{dd^{\prime\prime}-1})
.

We next compute
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}|
.
Let
m:=\dim(T_{1}+\dots+T_{r})\leq(R-1)d^{\prime}
and let
v_{1},\dots,v_{m}
be a basis of
T_{1}+\dots+T_{r}
. Then
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}
if and only if there exist
x_{1},\dots,x_{m}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
not all equal to zero such that
(b_{i}A)\cdot(x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{m}v_{m})=0
for all
1\leq i\leq d^{\prime\prime}
. Since
d^{\prime\prime}\geq m
, this is true only if the determinant of the matrix
C(\mathbb{b}):=((b_{i}A)\cdot v_{j})_{1\leq i,j\leq m}
is zero. Note that
\det(C(\mathbb{b}))
is a polynomial in
\mathbb{b}
of degree at most
O_{d}(1)
.
On the other hand, it is not hard to inductively construct for any
1\leq i\leq m\leq d^{\prime\prime}
vectors
b_{1},\dots,b_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
b_{i}\notin\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{b_{1},\dots,b_{i-1}\}
and
(b_{i^{\prime}}A)\cdot v_{j}=\delta_{i^{\prime},j}
for all
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
and
1\leq j\leq m
. So

\det(C(\mathbb{b}))\neq 0
and thus
\det(C(\cdot))
is not the constant zero polynomial. So it follows from Lemma
A.4
that
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}|=O_{d^{\prime\prime}}(p^{dd^{\prime\prime}-1})
.

Now fix
1\leq i\leq C
and let
v_{1},\dots,v_{m}
be a basis of
V_{i}
for some
m\leq D
. Then
U(\mathbb{b})\cap V_{i}\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
only if there exist
x_{1},\dots,x_{m},y_{1},\dots,y_{d^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
with
y_{1},\dots,y_{d^{\prime\prime}}
not all equal to zero such that

x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{m}v_{m}=y_{1}b_{1}+\dots+y_{d^{\prime\prime}}b_{d^{\prime\prime}}.

If
y_{1}\neq 0
, then
b_{1}
is uniquely determined by the choices of
x_{1},\dots,x_{m},y_{2},\dots,y_{d^{\prime\prime}}
and
b_{2},\dots,b_{d^{\prime\prime}}
. So

|\mathcal{B}_{i}|\leq d^{\prime\prime}p^{(d^{\prime\prime}-1)d+m+d^{\prime\prime}-1}=O_{d}(p^{d^{\prime\prime}d-1})

since
d^{\prime}\geq D
.

Finally, fix
1\leq j\leq L
and let

P
be the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\backslash\{\mathbb{0}\}
such that
f_{j}(n)=(nA)\cdot n=0
. Since
f_{j}\notin J^{M}
, by Lemmas
A.3
,
A.5
and Proposition
A.7
, we have

|P|=O_{d,s}(p^{d-2})
since
d\geq 4
.
For
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
let
P_{U(\mathbb{b})}
be the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
f_{j}(n)=(nA)\cdot n=(hA)\cdot n=0
for all
h\in U(\mathbb{b})
. In other words,
P_{U(\mathbb{b})}
is the intersection of
P
and the subspace

U(\mathbb{b})^{\perp_{M}}:=\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon(hA)\cdot n=0\text{ for all }h\in U(\mathbb{b})\}

which is of dimension
d-\text{rank}(M|_{U(\mathbb{b})})=d^{\prime}\geq 3
.

If
f_{j}\in J^{M}_{U(\mathbb{b})}
for some
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
, then
P_{U(\mathbb{b})}
is the intersection of
V(M)
and the subspace
U(\mathbb{b})^{\perp_{M}}
.
By Lemma
A.5
,
|P_{U(\mathbb{b})}|=p^{d-d^{\prime\prime}-1}(1+O(p^{-1/2}))
.
Therefore, in order to estimate
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{j}|
, it suffices to study the number of
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
such that
|P_{U(\mathbb{b})}|=p^{d-d^{\prime\prime}-1}(1+O(p^{-1/2}))
.

Note that for
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
n\in U(\mathbb{b})^{\perp_{M}}
if and only if
U(\mathbb{b})\subseteq\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{n\}^{\perp_{M}}
, which is a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension 1 since
n\neq\mathbb{0}
. So the number of such
\mathbb{b}
is at most
p^{(d-1)d^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}}|P_{U(\mathbb{b})}|=\sum_{\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}}|P\cap U(\mathbb{b})^{\perp_{M}}|=\sum_{n\in P}|\{\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}\colon n\in U(\mathbb{b})^{\perp_{M}}\}|\\
&\leq|P|\cdot p^{(d-1)d^{\prime\prime}}=O_{d,s}(p^{dd^{\prime\prime}+d-d^{\prime\prime}-2}),\end{split}

which implies that the number of
\mathbb{b}\in\mathcal{B}\backslash\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
with
|P_{U(\mathbb{b})}|=p^{d-d^{\prime\prime}-1}(1+O(p^{-1/2}))
is at most
O_{d,s}(p^{dd^{\prime\prime}-1})
. So
|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{j}|\leq O_{d,s}(p^{dd^{\prime\prime}-1})
for any
1\leq j\leq L
.

Combining the estimates of the cardinalities of
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{B}_{i}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{j}
above, we have
|\mathcal{B}|>|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}|+|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}|+|\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime\prime}|+\sum_{i=1}^{C}|\mathcal{B}_{i}|+\sum_{j=1}^{L}|\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{j}|
. This completes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{\delta,d,r}1
. Since
d\geq(2s+16)(15s+453)
, we may write
d=d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}
for some
d^{\prime}\geq 15s+453
and

d^{\prime\prime}\geq(2s+15)d^{\prime}\geq(2s+15)(15s+453)
.
By assumption, the
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})
is at least
\delta K^{3d}
.
Using the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality (Theorem
5.6
) twice and the fact that
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2})
has the same
M
-energy as
(\xi_{1},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{2})
, we have that the
M
-energy of
(\xi_{1},\xi_{1},\xi_{1},\xi_{1})
is
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{3d}
. From now on we write
\xi:=\xi_{1}
for convenience, and denote
\tilde{\xi}(h)=(h,J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+\xi(h))
for all
h\in H
.

By Theorem
5.9
, since
d\geq 2s+15
, there exists a subset
H_{1}
of
H
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(\tilde{\xi}(H_{1})\hat{+}\tilde{\xi}(H_{1}))=O_{\delta,d}(1)
.
By Proposition
5.19
, since
d\geq\max\{s+32,69\}
, there exists a subset
H_{2}
of
H_{1}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(8\tilde{\xi}(H_{2})\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H_{2}))=O_{\delta,d}(1)
.
Since
8\tilde{\xi}(H_{2})\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H_{2})\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{16}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
and
d\geq 28s+95
,
by Proposition
6.6
,
\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{0})\cap(8\tilde{\xi}(H_{2})\hat{-}8\tilde{\xi}(H_{2}))
admits an
(O_{\delta,d}(1),O_{\delta,d}(1),15s+450)
-classification for some
(O_{\delta,d,s}(1)
,
O_{\delta,d,s}(1),15s+450)
-structure-obstacle pair
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y)
.
By Lemma
9.1
, since
d^{\prime}\geq 15s+450,d^{\prime\prime}\geq(s+15)d^{\prime}
, there exist
(\mathcal{C}_{0},Y,M,d^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime})
-structure-obstacle decompositions
(T_{i},U_{i})
,
1\leq i\leq s+16
such that
T_{1},\dots,T_{s+16}
are linearly independent.
By repeatedly using Proposition
8.2
, since
d^{\prime}\geq 15s+453
and
d^{\prime\prime}\geq\max\{4s+23,35\}
, there exists a subset
H_{3}
of
H_{2}
of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{16}\in H_{3}
with
h_{1}+\dots+h_{8}=h_{9}+\dots+h_{16}
, and
1\leq i\leq s+16
, we have that

\begin{split}\xi(h_{1})+\dots+\xi(h_{8})\equiv\xi(h_{9})+\dots+\xi(h_{16})\mod J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{15})\}+T_{i}}.\end{split}

Since
T_{1},\dots,T_{s+16}
are linearly independent and
d\geq 37+2(s+6)(d^{\prime}-1)
(or equivalently
d^{\prime\prime}\geq(2s+5)d^{\prime}+2s+25
),
by Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=15
and
r=d^{\prime}
), we have that

\begin{split}\xi(h_{1})+\dots+\xi(h_{8})\equiv\xi(h_{9})+\dots+\xi(h_{16})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{15})}\end{split}

and so
\xi
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 16 on
H_{3}
.

Let
0<c=c(r,s)<1
be a constant to be chosen later.
Since
d\geq\max\{4s+19,35\}
,
there exist
0<\rho=\rho(\delta,d)<1/4
,
a homogeneous proper generalized arithmetic progression

P=\{x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D}\colon x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-L_{i},L_{i}),1\leq i\leq D\}

in
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
of rank
D=O_{\delta,d}(1)
and cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
, a set
S\subseteq\hat{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}}
of cardinality
D^{\prime}=O_{\delta,d}(1)
, and a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 4
\omega\colon P\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
satisfying the conclusions of Proposition
7.2
:

(1)

B(S,c\rho)\subseteq P(c)\subseteq P\subseteq B(S,1/4)\subseteq 2H_{3}-2H_{3}
;

(2)

For all
h\in P
, the set
R(H_{3},h):=\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in H\colon h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}-h_{4}=h\}
is of cardinality
\gg_{\delta,d}K^{3d}
;

(3)

there exists
H_{4}\subseteq H_{3}
with
|H_{4}|\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that
2H_{4}-2H_{4}\subseteq P(c)
;

(4)

the vectors
(\{\alpha\cdot v_{i}\})_{\alpha\in S}\in\mathbb{R}^{D^{\prime}},1\leq i\leq D
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
;

(5)

for all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}\in H_{3}
with
h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}-h_{4}\in P
, we have

(9.1)

\xi(h_{1})+\xi(h_{2})-\xi(h_{3})-\xi(h_{4})\equiv\omega(h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}-h_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3}),\iota(h_{4})};

(6)

for all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}\in P
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
, we have

\omega(h_{1})+\omega(h_{2})\equiv\omega(h_{3})+\omega(h_{4})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})},

i.e. the map
\omega\colon P\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
is a Freiman
M
-homomorphism of order 4.

By Theorem
4.3
, since
d\geq\max\{s+10,11\}
, for an appropriately chosen
0<c=c(r,s)\leq 1
depending only on
s
and
r
, there exists a locally linear map
T\colon P(c)\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
such that

\omega(u)\equiv T(u)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(u)}
for all
u\in P(c)
.

Our next goal is to provide an explicit formula for the locally linear map
T
.
Assume that

T(x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D})=u_{0}+x_{1}u_{1}+\dots+x_{D}u_{D}\text{ for all }x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-cL_{i},cL_{i}),1\leq i\leq D

for some
u_{0},\dots,u_{D}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
.
We may naturally extend
T
to a locally linear map on
P
by setting

T(x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D})=u_{0}+x_{1}u_{1}+\dots+x_{D}u_{D}\text{ for all }x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\cap(-L_{i},L_{i}),1\leq i\leq D.

For convenience we also denote this map by
T
.
Let

\Phi\colon P\to\mathbb{R}^{D^{\prime}}
be the map given by

\Phi(x):=(\{\alpha\cdot x\})_{\alpha\in S}.

Since
P\subseteq B(S,\rho)
,
\Phi(P)
lies inside
[-\rho,\rho]^{D^{\prime}}
. Since
\rho<1/4
, it is easy to check that

\Phi(x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D})=x_{1}\Phi(v_{1})+\dots+x_{D}\Phi(v_{D})\text{ for all }x_{i}\in(-L_{i},L_{i}),1\leq i\leq D.

Claim.
For all
1\leq i\leq D
, there exists
w_{i}=(w_{i,1},\dots,w_{i,D^{\prime}})=(p_{i,1}/q_{i},\dots,p_{i,D^{\prime}}/q_{i})\in\mathbb{Q}^{D^{\prime}}
for some
p_{i,j},q_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
|q_{i}|=O_{\delta,d}(1)
such that
w_{i}\cdot\Phi(v_{i})=1
and
w_{i}\cdot\Phi(v_{j})=0
for all
j\neq i
.

Since
\Phi(v_{i}),1\leq i\leq D
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
, it is easy to see the existences of
w_{i}\in\mathbb{Q}^{D^{\prime}}
such that
w_{i}\cdot\Phi(v_{i})=1
and
w_{i}\cdot\Phi(v_{j})=0
for all
j\neq i
. The difficulty of the claim is to bound the value of
q_{i}
.

Since
\Phi(v_{i}),1\leq i\leq D
are linearly independent over
\mathbb{R}
and
P
is a proper generalized arithmetic progression,
the
D
-dimensional volume of the convex hull of
\Phi(P)
as a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{D^{\prime}}
is at most
1/2^{D}
(since it lies inside
[-1/4,1/4]^{D^{\prime}}
). So the
D
-dimensional volume of
D
-dimensional cube in
\mathbb{R}^{D^{\prime}}
generated by
K\Phi(v_{i}),1\leq i\leq D

is at most
\frac{K^{D}\text{vol}(\Phi(P))}{|P|}=O_{\delta,d}(1)
. By the Cauchy-Binet formula, there exists a
D\times D
sub-matrix
B
of the
D\times D^{\prime}
real matrix
(K\Phi(v_{i}))_{1\leq i\leq D}
(where each
\Phi(v_{i})
is viewed as a
D^{\prime}
-dimensional horizontal vector) such that
0<\det(B)=O_{\delta,d}(1)
.
Since
K\Phi(v_{i})\in\mathbb{Z}^{D^{\prime}}
, the claim follows from the knowledge of linear algebra.

Let
w_{1},\dots,w_{D}
be given by the claim.
Then

\Phi(x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D})\cdot w_{i}=(x_{1}\Phi(v_{1})+\dots+x_{D}\Phi(v_{D}))\cdot w_{i}=x_{i}.

Then
for all
x=x_{1}v_{1}+\dots+x_{D}v_{D}\in P
, we have that

T(x)=u_{0}+x_{1}u_{1}+\dots+x_{D}u_{D}=u_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{D}(\Phi(x)\cdot w_{i})u_{i}=u_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{D}(\{\alpha\cdot x\}\cdot w_{i})u_{i}.

Since all the denominators of
w_{i}
are at most
O_{\delta,d}(1)
in absolute values, it is not hard to see
that
T
is a
\mathbb{Z}/Qp^{r}
-almost linear function on
P(c)
of complexity
O_{\delta,d}(1)
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q=O_{\delta,d}(1)
.

Let
Q^{\ast}
be any integer with
Q^{\ast}Q\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
. Then
T^{\prime}:=Q^{\ast}QT
is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear function on
P(c)
of complexity
O_{\delta,d}(1)
. Since
(Q^{\ast}Q-1)T(u)\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z},d}(s)\subseteq J^{M}
for all
u\in P(c)
, we have that

\omega(u)\equiv T^{\prime}(u)+(1-Q^{\ast}Q)T(u)\equiv T^{\prime}(u)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(u)}
for all
u\in P(c)
.

We are now ready to find out the expression for
\xi
.
For all
h_{1},h_{2}\in H_{4}
,
since
h_{1}-h_{2}\in H_{4}-H_{4}\subseteq P(c)
, setting
h_{2}=h_{3}=h_{4}
in (
9.1
), we have

\xi(h_{1})-\xi(h_{2})\equiv\omega(h_{1}-h_{2})\equiv T^{\prime}(h_{1}-h_{2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2})}.

Since
\{\alpha\}+\{\beta\}-\{\alpha+\beta\}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
T^{\prime}
is an almost linear function on
P(c)
of complexity
O_{\delta,d}(1)
, we may find a set
\Omega\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
of cardinality
O_{\delta,d}(1)
such that
T^{\prime}(h_{1}-h_{2})-(T^{\prime}(h_{1})-T^{\prime}(h_{2}))\in\Omega
for all
h_{1},h_{2}\in H_{4}
. So writing
\theta:=\xi-T^{\prime}
, we have that

\theta(h_{1})-\theta(h_{2})\equiv g(h_{1},h_{2})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2})}

for some
g(h_{1},h_{2})\in\Omega
for all
h_{1},h_{2}\in H_{4}
.

We need to use the intersection method one more time.
Since
|H_{4}|\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
and
d\geq s+3
, we may find some
p
-linearly independent
h_{1},\dots,h_{s+3}\in H_{4}
by Lemma
A.3
. By the Pigeonhole principle, there exists a subset
H_{5}
of
H_{4}
with
|H_{5}|\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that for all
h\in H_{5}
and
1\leq i\leq s+3
,
g(h,h_{i})
takes the same value
g(h_{i})
.
Then for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H_{5}
,

\theta(h)-\theta(h^{\prime})\equiv(\theta(h)-\theta(h_{i}))-(\theta(h^{\prime})-\theta(h_{i}))\equiv g(h_{i})-g(h_{i})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(h^{\prime}),\iota(h_{i})}

for all
1\leq i\leq s+3
. By Proposition
3.6
(setting
m=2
and
r=1
), since
d\geq 11
, we have that

\theta(h)\equiv\theta(h^{\prime})\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(h^{\prime})}

for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H_{5}
.
This means that

X:=\{(\mathbb{0},J^{M}_{\iota(h)}+\theta(h))\colon h\in H_{5}\}\subseteq\Gamma_{2}^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)

is a weak equivalence class.
Since
d\geq s+2
, it is impossible for all the elements of
\iota(H_{5})
to lie in a common subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
s+1
.
By Proposition
5.4
(setting
k=2
),
X
is a strong equivalence class since
d\geq 9
.
So there exists
g\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
such that
g\equiv\theta(h)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}
for all
h\in H_{5}
.
Since
\xi(H_{5})\subseteq\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
, it is not hard to see from the proof of Proposition
5.4
that we may further require
g\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
.

In conclusion, we have that

\xi(h)\equiv T^{\prime}(h)+g\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in H_{5}
. Since
\{\alpha\}+\{\beta\}-\{\alpha+\beta\}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
for all
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
T^{\prime}
is an
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear function of complexity
O_{\delta,d}(1)
, there exits a subset

H_{6}
of
H_{5}
with
|H_{6}|\gg_{\delta,d}K^{d}
such that

T
is an
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism on
H_{6}
. We are done.

∎

10.
Open questions

We collection of open questions in this section.
Since the lower bound
d\geq N(s)
obtained in Theorem
1.5
is rather coarse, it is natural to ask:

Question 10.1
.

What is the optimal lower bound for
d
in Theorem
1.5
?

The major cause of the coarse lower bound for
d
in Theorem
1.5
is the error term
X_{b}
appearing in Proposition
6.3
, which has nontrivial intersections with some subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of rather large dimensions (see also Remark
6.4
). Therefore, it is natural to ask:

Question 10.2
.

Can we make the set
X_{b}
in Proposition
6.3
to be empty (or to have a small cardinality)?

A positive answer the Question
10.2
will greatly improve the lower bound for
d
in Theorem
1.5
.
Recall that this question is connection to Conjecture
6.2
, which we restate below:

Question 10.3
.

Let
d,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime dividing
K
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
G
be the relation graph of a subset of
\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
. If
d\gg_{k,s}1
and
p\gg_{d}1
, then is it true that
{\#}_{\text{cc}}(G)=O_{d,{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)}(1)
?

Although we have demonstrated in Section
5
that shifted modules enjoy many additive combinatorial properties similar to the abelian group case, some results in Section
5
are not proved in full generality. For example, one can ask:

Question 10.4
.

Does Proposition
5.15
hold for
B\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{k}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
(instead of
B\subseteq\Gamma^{s}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d},M)
)? Can we upgrade Proposition
5.15
from a quasi triangle inequality to a genial triangle inequality?

Question 10.5
.

Does Conjecture
5.17
(an improvement of Proposition
5.18
) hold?

Finally, it is interesting to ask about the connection between the conventional independence number of a graph and the density dependent number defined in this paper. This is Question
2.12
which we restate below:

Question 10.6
.

For any graph, is the independent number equal to
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)
? If not, then is the independent number bounded below by a constant depending on
{\#}_{\text{dd}}(G)
?

Appendix A
Basic properties for quadratic forms

In this appendix, we collect some results proved in
[
7
]
on quadratic forms which is used in this paper.

A.1.
The rank of quadratic forms

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Let
V^{\perp_{M}}
denote the set of
\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon(mA)\cdot n=0\text{ for all }m\in V\}
.
A subspace
V
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is
M
-isotropic
if
V\cap V^{\perp_{M}}\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
.
We say that a tuple
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})
of vectors in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is
M
-isotropic
if the span of
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}
is an
M
-isotropic subspace.
We say that a subspace or tuple of vectors is
M
-non-isotropic
if it is not
M
-isotropic.

Lemma A.1

(Proposition 4.8 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
, and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

(1)

We have
\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\leq\min\{d-\text{rank}(M)+r,d-r\}
.

(2)

The rank of
M|_{V+c}
equals to
d-r-\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
(i.e.
\dim(V)-\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
).

(3)

The rank of
M|_{V+c}
is at most
d-r
and at least
\text{rank}(M)-2r
.

(4)

M|_{V+c}
is non-degenerate (i.e.
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-r
) if and only if
V
is not an
M
-isotropic subspace.

Lemma A.2

(Lemma 4.9 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
r
, and
V^{\prime}
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
r^{\prime}
. Suppose that
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})=d-r
. Then
\text{rank}(M|_{(V+V^{\prime})^{\perp_{M}}})\geq d-r-2r^{\prime}
.

Lemma A.3

(Lemma 4.11 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
d,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.

(1)

The number of tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{k}
such that
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are linearly dependent is at most
k\frac{K^{dk}}{p^{d-k+1}}
.

(2)

The number of tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{k}
such that
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are
M
-isotropic is at most
O_{d,k}(\frac{K^{dk}}{p})
.

17
17

17

Although Lemma 4.11 of
[
7
]
was stated for the case
K=p
, the general case can be deduced immidiately.

A.2.
Some basic counting properties

We refer the readers to Section
1.2
for the notations used in this section.

Lemma A.4

(Lemma 4.10 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
be of degree at most
r
.
Then
|V(P)|\leq O_{d,r}(p^{d-1})
unless
P\equiv 0
.

Lemma A.5

(Corollary 4.14 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
d,r\in\mathbb{N},d\geq 1
and
p
be a prime number. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
.

(1)

If
s:=\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq 3
, then

|V(M)\cap(V+c)|=p^{d-r-1}(1+O(p^{-\frac{s-2}{2}})).

(2)

If
\text{rank}(M)-2r\geq 3
, then

|V(M)\cap(V+c)|=p^{d-r-1}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).

Lemma A.6

(Corollary 4.16 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
d,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
p
be a prime number. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
h_{1},\dots,h_{r}
be linearly independent vectors.
If
d-2r\geq 3
, then

|V(M)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{r}}|=p^{d-r-1}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).

A.3.
Irreducible properties for quadratic forms

Proposition A.7

(Lemma 6.1 and Proposition 6.3 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime such that
p\gg_{d,s}1
. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form of rank at least 3.
For any
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
s
, either
|V(P)\cap V(M)|\leq O_{d,s}(p^{d-2})
or
V(M)\subseteq V(P)
.

Moreover, if
V(M)\subseteq V(P)
, then
P=MR
for some
R\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
of degree at most
s-2
.

Proposition A.8

(Corollary 6.4 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\gg_{d,k,s}1
be a prime number,

P\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
be a polynomial of degree at most
s
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}
. Suppose that
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq 3
.
Then either
|V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\cap V(P)|\leq O_{d,k,s}(p^{d-k-2})
or
V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\subseteq V(P)
.

Moreover, if
V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\subseteq V(P)
, then

(A.1)

P(n)=M(n)P_{0}(n)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}((h_{i}A)\cdot(n-c))P_{i}(n)

for some polynomials
P_{0},\dots,P_{k}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
with
\deg(P_{0})\leq s-2
and
\deg(P_{i})\leq s-1,1\leq i\leq k
.

In particular, the conclusion of this corollary holds if
d\geq k+\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})+3
, or if
d\geq 2k+3
.

Proposition A.9

(Proposition 7.7 of
[
7
]
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\gg_{d,k,s}1
be a prime number,

M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a non-degenerate quadratic form induced by some
M^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
associated with the matrix
A
,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
for some
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Denote

L_{j}(n):=\frac{1}{p}(h_{j}\tau(A))\cdot(n-\tau(c))

for
1\leq j\leq k
. Suppose that
\text{rank}(M^{\prime}|_{V^{\perp_{M^{\prime}}}})\geq 3
.
The followings are equivalent:

(1)

f
belongs to
\text{poly}(V_{p}(M)\cap\iota^{-1}(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
and is of degree at most
s
;

(2)

we have

Q_{0}f=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some
Q_{0}\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q_{0}\leq O_{d,s}(1)
and some integer valued polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
, and that
p^{\deg(f)}f
is an integer valued polynomial.

Moreover, if all the coefficients of
f
are in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
, then we may write

f=\sum_{i=(i_{0},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon 2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq s,i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}\leq\min\{r,s\}}R_{i}M^{i_{0}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}L_{j}^{i_{j}}

for some integer coefficient polynomials
R_{i}
of degree at most
\deg(f)-(2i_{0}+i_{1}+\dots+i_{k})
.

In particular, the conclusion of this proposition holds if
d\geq k+\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})+3
, or if
d\geq 2k+3
.
